## 2026-08-30 — dispatch.py: opening-window crop restriction (WHEAT+MELON only, days 0-2) — real improvement, promoted
- Command: `python src/compare_agents.py .claude/scratch/main_before_opening.py submission/main.py`
- Motivation: `docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`'s "Scripted opening window" entry documented that days 0-2 are 100% deterministic and that five independently-written strong opponents converge on wheat+melon-only planting in that window (never carrot/tomato/strawberry). Measured our own agent's actual day-0-2 behavior first (`.claude/scratch/opening_measure/measure_opening.py`): hiring (8 hands day 0) and spend pace ($2,848 of $3,000 by end of day 2) already matched the strong-opponent range, and animal placement was already COW/SHEEP-only -- but crop selection diverged, planting CARROT and STRAWBERRY during the opening alongside WHEAT/MELON.
- Change: `_field_jobs` (`src/adaptive_agent/dispatch.py`) gains one condition on the PLANT job -- while `day <= OPENING_RESTRICT_DAYS` (new config key, default 0/off), only WHEAT and MELON planting jobs are generated; other crops' planting is deferred to day 3+ once the deterministic window ends. `adaptive_best_config.json` sets `OPENING_RESTRICT_DAYS: 2`. Verified with a direct day-by-day trace before the full test: day 0 planted only `['MELON', 'WHEAT']`, day 3 planted `['STRAWBERRY']` -- confirms the gate does exactly what it's supposed to and nothing else.
- Result: **DIVERGENT SIGNAL flagged by compare_agents.py** (fixed-tape margin worse, reactive-opponent margin much better) -- own money improved in 5 of 6 opponents (e.g. pilkwang 64,278 -> 75,065, romanrozen 58,414 -> 62,855; saiteja was the one exception, 82,006 -> 73,542). Margin vs pilkwang (the one reactive, price-aware opponent, not subject to the fixed-tape confound) went from -12,032 to +4,831 -- a loss turned into a win. avg margin delta vs fixed-tape opponents: -1,066; avg margin delta vs reactive opponent: +16,864. Per this tool's own documented diagnostic (reduced early overproduction stops crashing the shared market, letting price-blind fixed-tape sellers land a better price without us doing worse), the reactive-opponent number and the own-money columns are the ones to trust here, not the fixed-tape margin.
- **Promoted**: `src/adaptive_agent/dispatch.py` and `src/adaptive_best_config.json` changes kept, `submission/main.py` rebuilt. Baseline snapshot at `.claude/scratch/main_before_opening.py`. A full verification game saved via `run_match.py` (submission/main.py vs pilkwang) to `output/games/` for manual replay inspection. Not yet committed to git -- left for the user per this project's standing convention (git/Kaggle actions stay manual).

## 2026-08-27 — opponent pool refresh: deleted 10 no-signal opponents, added 1 real new one, sourced by CURRENT public leaderboard score
- Command: owner asked to find opponents `adaptive_agent` currently LOSES to (to keep progress trackable), delete opponents it now beats, and re-derive the pool by current live leaderboard score rather than stale pull-time numbers.
- **First, ran a real head-to-head sweep** (4 seeded episodes each, `SEARCH_SEEDS`, direct script, not delegated) of the tuned champion (`adaptive_best_config.json`) against every real opponent then in `src/opponents/`. Result, most important finding of this entry: **the champion currently LOSES to the entire former "2500+ tier" pool, 0W-4L across all nine of kawa/boatlee_v16/rayk_c95/saiteja/kaito/tran_hh/pilkwang/romanrozen/prvsiyan_frontier, plus sakhawathossen (0W-4L)** -- including `pilkwang`, the agent `adaptive_agent`'s own architecture is modeled on (avg margin -7,790). This directly contradicts an earlier claim from the pilkwang-rebuild's Stage 4 report ("100,348 vs 96,083, a genuine win vs pilkwang") -- that number was almost certainly measured on an intermediate build/config mid-rebuild, not the final tuned+bug-fixed champion; flagged here rather than left standing uncorrected. Meanwhile the former near/mid-tier pool (rajan1673, chaitanyajamble, ektarr, nagatakengo, umutdorukztrk, iamsdt, kiykhoi, mansiaggarwal88, moncefelm, daisy023) was beaten decisively, 4W-0L each, avg margin +58,785 to +128,053 -- zero signal left for tracking further tuning progress. premaananda108 was roughly even (2W-2L, avg +411).
- **Deleted** the 10 no-signal opponent files: `src/opponents/{rajan1673,chaitanyajamble,ektarr,nagatakengo,umutdorukztrk,iamsdt,kiykhoi,mansiaggarwal88,moncefelm,daisy023}_agent.py`.
- **Pulled the current live leaderboard** (`kaggle competitions leaderboard kaggriculture -d`, 6638 teams, fetched 2026-08-27 18:43 UTC) and cross-referenced against `kaggle kernels list --competition kaggriculture --sort-by voteCount` for candidate authors currently scoring well above our own live rating (HawkOsm, rank 4809/6638, score 496.2 -- note this reflects the OLD submission still live on Kaggle, not yet resubmitted with `adaptive_agent`). Pulled 8 candidate notebooks in the 1600-2300 current-score band: indarkarhana (#244, 2205.9), stevenleehans (#333, 2101.6), romantamrazov (#652, 1879.6), kunaldesale2408 (#675, 1869.0), flexonafft (#710, 1855.9), andrewsokolovsky (#721, 1850.6), yamakawanin (#901, 1757.3), tetsutani (#1015, 1708.3).
- **Extracted and tested**: `flexonafft` and `kunaldesale2408` both decode to a byte-identical `main.py` (same SHA-256) that turned out to be a repost of `boatlee`'s V20-Adaptive-R1 -- already in the pool as `kawa_route_agent.py` -- discarded as exact duplicates. `indarkarhana` decodes cleanly and does beat the champion (0W-6L, avg -32,388), but AST def-name-set diffing showed 85% helper-function overlap with `kawa_route_agent.py` (a fork of the same public-replay-consensus family) and its per-seed margins track `stevenleehans`'s almost exactly (e.g. -24,811 vs -25,081, -46,214 vs -46,515) -- both are near-clones of the same underlying top-player replay, so kept only one. `yamakawanin`'s notebook failed to reproduce its own `main.py` when its cells were executed outside the live notebook session (an `AssertionError: main.py was not created` from what's likely notebook-only state); not investigated further given time budget, skipped. `romantamrazov`, `andrewsokolovsky`, `tetsutani` not attempted this round (the first two are heavyweight multi-stage evaluator/overlay notebooks, the third turned out to be a replay-visualization notebook, not an agent).
- **Added** `src/opponents/stevenleehans_agent.py` (Lord Momo / stevenleehans, "Kaggriculture X567: X562 plus non-clone milk timing", rank #333/6638, score 2101.6): confirmed via direct testing to beat the champion decisively, 0W-6L, avg margin -32,670 across 6 seeded episodes -- real, current, verified signal.
- **Rewired** `src/tuning/config.py` (`OPPONENT_REGISTRY` imports/dict, `HOLDOUT_ROTATIONS` -- old rotations referenced only now-deleted names) and `src/tuning/optimize.py` (`DEFAULT` opponent pool, same problem) to the surviving pool: kawa, boatlee_v16, rayk_c95, saiteja, kaito, tran_hh, pilkwang, romanrozen, prvsiyan_frontier, sakhawathossen, stevenleehans, premaananda108. New `HOLDOUT_ROTATIONS`: `["saiteja","sakhawathossen","kawa"]`, `["boatlee_v16","romanrozen","kawa"]`, `["prvsiyan_frontier","premaananda108","kawa"]`. New default search pool: rayk_c95, kaito, tran_hh, pilkwang, stevenleehans (whichever aren't claimed by the active holdout rotation) + champion. Verified both modules import cleanly and a smoke-test search run completes end to end (see entry below).
- **Updated `CREDITS.md`** (both the attribution table and near-tier section) to drop the 10 deleted rows, add `stevenleehans_agent.py`, and refresh every surviving opponent's leaderboard rank/score to today's numbers instead of original-pull-time numbers, per the "derive by current public score" ask.
- Notes: the practical implication is that `adaptive_agent`'s real strength currently sits between the old near/mid-tier pool (crushed) and the 2500+ tier (still losing to all of it) -- a meaningful, non-trivial gap, not close to done. The refreshed pool is deliberately weighted toward opponents in that gap band (1600-2900 current score) so future tuning runs have real signal instead of either a pool that's already fully won (no gradient) or one that's fully lost with equally little differentiation. This also means the "genuine win vs pilkwang" claim threaded through several earlier session entries needs to be treated as unverified/likely-stale going forward -- do not cite it without re-measuring.

## 2026-08-27 — deleted `robust_agent` and its now-dead support code, repointed the `agent.py` shim to `adaptive_agent`
- Command: with `adaptive_agent` promoted, verified, and bug-fixed (entries above), owner asked to retire the old architecture entirely rather than keep it around.
- **Deleted**: `src/robust_agent/` (the former live architecture), `src/build_submission.py` (its flattener), `src/optimize.py` (its CLI shim), `src/tuning/` (its entire Optuna search/verification harness -- `sample_config`'s 46-knob schema, `verify_candidate`/`verify_holdout`, risk-adjusted scoring). All of it was 100% non-functional without `robust_agent` (different config schema, no overlap with `adaptive_agent`'s tunable surface) -- kept dead would just mean broken imports with zero working capability, so removed rather than left as a trap for a future session. The reusable harness PATTERNS (dual-agent verification, holdout rotation, risk-adjusted Optuna scoring) remain fully documented in `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_rebuild/search.py` and this file's own history if a rework for `adaptive_agent`'s schema is ever wanted.
- **Repointed `src/agent.py`** (compatibility shim) to `adaptive_agent.agent.make_agent`, and added a ready-built `adaptive_agent` attribute (constructed with the real tuned `adaptive_best_config.json`, not defaults) so `run_match.py agent:adaptive_agent <opponent>` keeps working as a real, meaningful local test command -- verified directly (`agent:adaptive_agent vs kawa_route_agent`, ran cleanly end to end).
- **Documentation**: `AGENT.md` rewritten to state `robust_agent` no longer exists (was previously, briefly, incorrectly described as "kept as reference" before this deletion), its architectural-facts section reframed as historical context for why it was replaced rather than current facts, and the "no Optuna pipeline for `adaptive_agent` yet" gap stated plainly as the real open follow-up. `pyproject.toml`'s now-nonexistent `src/optimize.py` per-file-ignore entry removed.
- Notes: `ruff check` on the surviving code shows 12 minor issues (mostly a `_survey` function at complexity 17 vs the 15 threshold, and a couple of style suggestions) -- noted, not fixed here; not urgent, and `ruff --fix` specifically is not being run blind given it silently broke the compatibility shims once already earlier this session. This closes out the architecture-swap thread for today: `adaptive_agent` is now the sole live agent, verified, bug-fixed, and the only path from `src/` to `submission/main.py`.

## 2026-08-27 — post-promotion bug audit of `adaptive_agent`: 3 real bugs found and fixed, 1 flagged finding was a false positive, re-verified clean after fixing
- Command: before deleting `robust_agent` (removing the fallback), ran a dedicated read-only bug audit of `adaptive_agent` (never had one -- unlike `robust_agent`, which got a full audit earlier this session). Delegated to `agy`, all 4 findings independently checked by direct code inspection before acting on any of them.
- **Real bug 1 (CRITICAL, confirmed): `PRIORITY_BONUS` JSON round-trip.** `search_space.py`'s `sample_config` builds `PRIORITY_BONUS` with int dict keys (`{-1: ..., 0: ...}`), used correctly in-memory during the Optuna search itself (never serialized). But `src/adaptive_best_config.json` on disk has this same dict with JSON-forced STRING keys (`"-1"`, `"0"`) -- `dispatch.py`'s lookup (`bonus_map.get(priority, ...)`, `priority` an int) silently missed every entry once loaded from the file, falling back to a much weaker `-1000.0 * priority` approximation. This affected the ALREADY-PROMOTED `submission/main.py` (built from the JSON file) and my own independent re-verification runs (also loaded from the JSON file) -- meaning the decisive win reported in the promotion entry above was measured on the degraded fallback version, not the true tuned one. Fixed by making the lookup try both key forms (`bonus_map.get(priority, bonus_map.get(str(priority), fallback))`).
- **Real bug 2 (CRITICAL, confirmed): missing `LAND_PRICES` import in `dispatch.py`.** `_market_commitment_cost` (called from `market.py:113` on every scheduled-adjustment turn) references `LAND_PRICES` for its `BUY_LAND` cost branch, but `dispatch.py` never imports it from `.constants`. Would raise `NameError` -> caught by `agent.py`'s top-level `try/except` -> silent fallback to `PASS` for that turn. Didn't fire in any trace run today purely because this config's `LAND_OPEN_DAYS` (2, 4) never happened to coincide with `_schedule_market_adjustment`'s trigger day -- a real, latent risk for other configs/seeds, not something that only matters in theory. Fixed by adding the import.
- **Real bug 3 (HIGH, confirmed): `LIQUIDATION_TURNS` phase desync.** `state.py`'s `_policy_phase` didn't take a `config` parameter at all and hardcoded the bare default (22) for its `LIQUIDATE` phase check, while `dispatch.py`'s own liquidation check correctly read the live tuned value (26 in the promoted config) via `_cfg(...)`. A real 4-turn window where the two systems disagreed about which phase the game was in. Fixed by adding `config` to `_policy_phase`'s signature, reading `LIQUIDATION_TURNS` the same way `dispatch.py` does, and updating the one call site (`market.py:183`).
- **Flagged finding that was a false positive, NOT fixed**: `MAX_HANDS` defined twice in `constants.py` (12, then 13) was flagged as a transcription error vs. the original's `MAX_HANDS=12`. Checked `pilkwang_agent.py` directly -- it has the EXACT same shadowing pattern (`MAX_HANDS=12` at line 151, then reassigned to `13` at line 1978, Python uses the last binding) -- the SAME shadowing-function issue already found and correctly handled once before in the Stage 2 `_target_hands` port. `adaptive_agent`'s `13` is faithful to the original's real, active value; agy's audit compared against the dead, shadowed one. Left unchanged.
- **Verification after fixing**: rebuilt `submission/main.py`. `verify_candidate` (dual-module, fresh config, 20 episodes): **20W-0L-0T, avg margin +79,294.2 -- matches the original search's own internally-reported number EXACTLY** (that number was always computed in-memory with correct int keys, never affected by the JSON bug; this confirms the fix restores the true intended behavior rather than changing it to something untested). Holdout re-checked: `ektarr` 8W (+86,151), `nagatakengo` 8W (+107,091.8), `kawa` 0W (-28,975.6, slightly better than the pre-fix reading) -- all consistent or improved, no regression.
- Notes: a genuinely valuable audit -- the promoted agent was winning decisively even while running a partially-degraded (bug-affected) priority function, and is now confirmed to be running its actual intended, fully-tuned logic instead. Also a good example of applying this project's own established skepticism to a delegated finding, not just delegated code: one of agy's four claims (`MAX_HANDS`) didn't survive a direct check against the original source and was correctly left alone.

## 2026-08-27 — promoted `adaptive_agent` to the live submission, replacing `robust_agent` — real architecture swap, verified before and after promotion, one real bug caught and fixed in the flattening step
- Command: given the decisive, multiple-times-independently-verified 20W-0L-0T / +79-84k margin result (see the pilkwang-rebuild entries above), owner asked to promote it as the actual primary agent, renamed `adaptive_agent`, rather than leave it as a scratch prototype.
- **Own mistake caught before it caused damage**: the real, full 150-trial search (run directly by me this time, not delegated, after two delegated attempts died at their print-timeout) wrote its `best_found_config.json` to the REPO ROOT instead of the scratch folder -- a relative-path bug in `search.py` combined with running it from repo root. Nearly promoted the STALE 20-trial config (still sitting in `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_rebuild/`) instead of the real 150-trial result. Caught by checking file mtimes before trusting either. Corrected: real config now at `src/adaptive_best_config.json`, re-verified fresh (20W-0L-0T, avg +80,929.2, matching the search's own internally-reported +79,294.2).
- **Promoted `src/adaptive_agent/`** (copied from `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_rebuild/pilkwang_style_agent/`, verified faithful across all 5 build stages) as the new package. `src/robust_agent/` kept in place, untouched, as historical/reference code -- not deleted, not overwritten.
- **Built `src/build_adaptive_submission.py`** (new AST-flattening build script, same proven approach as `build_submission.py`) to produce `submission/main.py` from `adaptive_agent/` + `adaptive_best_config.json`. **Caught and fixed a real bug on the first attempt**: `constants.py` and `config.py` both define several same-named constants with different values (`LIQUIDATION_TURNS`, `TRAVEL_COST`, `MAX_HANDS`, etc.) -- safe in the real package because every consumer imports these specifically from `.config`, never `.constants`, but this meant module dependency ORDER in the flattener matters (config.py must be flattened after constants.py so its definitions win, matching what the package actually resolves). Also caught a second bug from the same build: aliased imports (`from .config import get_config as _cfg`, used in 3 files) were being silently dropped by the import-stripping step with no replacement, causing a `NameError: name '_cfg' is not defined` at runtime -- fixed by having the stripper emit an explicit `_cfg = get_config` assignment for any aliased intra-package import instead of just deleting it.
- **Verification, before overwriting the live `submission/main.py`**: backed up the old (`robust_agent`-based) `submission/main.py` to `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_rebuild/`. Ran the new flattened submission head-to-head against the `adaptive_agent` PACKAGE version (same config, direct self-play) -- individual episode margins were non-zero (e.g. -280/+280, +1726/-1726) but every seat-swapped pair cancelled to exactly 0.00 net. Independently confirmed this is a pure engine seat-position artifact, not a flattening bug, by running the PACKAGE against itself (no flattened file involved at all) on the same seeds and seeing byte-identical individual-episode diffs -- the flattened `submission/main.py` is behaviorally identical to the package, confirmed, not assumed.
- **Documentation updated**: `AGENT.md` rewritten to describe `adaptive_agent` as the live architecture (with the real verified numbers and the honest caveat that `src/tuning/`'s search infrastructure has NOT been reworked for its config schema yet -- a known gap, not an oversight). `CREDITS.md` updated with proper attribution (pilkwang's public notebook, the competition's own Code-tab-licensing rule, and an explicit statement that this is an independent re-implementation verified stage-by-stage, not a copy).
- Notes: **this is the single largest promotion of the entire session** -- a full architecture replacement of the actual competition submission, not a config tune. Verified at every step: the search result (three independent re-verifications across two searches), the promoted config (caught and fixed a path bug before it could promote the wrong one), and the flattened build (caught and fixed two real bugs, then proved behavioral equivalence directly rather than assuming the flattening "should" work). `src/tuning/` reworking for `adaptive_agent`'s schema remains a real, open follow-up task, not done here.

## 2026-08-27 — built a genuine, from-scratch pilkwang-architecture agent (staged phase machine, herd targets, global greedy bipartite dispatch, real price-curve forecasting) as a 5-stage gated rebuild, not a ported piece — CLEARS the real promotion gate decisively, independently verified three separate ways
- Command: owner explicitly asked to stop porting individual pilkwang mechanisms onto our reactive dispatcher (every such attempt this session failed) and instead build a genuinely new agent modeled on pilkwang's real, complete architecture from scratch. Ran a 5-stage gated `agy` workflow, each stage independently re-verified before the next started, mirroring the earlier `agent.py` restructuring's discipline:
  - **Stage 1 (plan, no code)**: full line-by-line read of `pilkwang_agent.py` (2026 lines), architecture map + staged build plan. Independently spot-checked the single most important claim (a genuine global greedy bipartite worker-mission match, not a tiered queue) directly against source -- confirmed accurate. Caught and corrected a real error in the report (two `def _target_hands` definitions in the source file, Python silently uses the second; the report initially cited the shadowed first one) before it could propagate into the port.
  - **Stage 2 (state machine + market forecasting + herd/hiring schedule, no dispatch/orders yet)**: ported with the `_target_hands` correction applied. Reported "12,223 checks, 0 mismatches" -- independently found one real gap (`_sell_quantity` was ported but never exercised by the comparison harness) and closed it myself with a separate 32,355-check comparison against a real game trajectory: 0 mismatches, confirming the gap was in test coverage, not the port.
  - **Stage 3 (dispatch engine, the highest-risk piece)**: ported `_field_jobs`/`_unit_actions`. 2,876 checks against the actual final dispatch output (not just intermediate state), 0 mismatches -- inspected the comparison harness directly and confirmed it's a real, non-vacuous check.
  - **Stage 4 (market actions, full assembly)**: ported `_market_actions`/`_schedule_market_adjustment`, assembled a fully playable agent. 1,438 comparison checks, 0 mismatches. Real live match, new port vs the actual original `pilkwang_agent.py` (genuinely different code driving each side, not a comparison harness): port won 100,348 vs 96,083.
  - **Stage 5 (real Optuna search + the actual gate)**: the delegated task's own process hit its print-timeout and died again (same failure mode as the day-0-burst-v2/joint-retune tasks earlier today) but did write a report first this time. **Caught a real, undisclosed limitation before trusting anything**: `search.py` only ran 20 trials, not the 150 requested (a code comment literally says "run a few trials just to see the speed") -- not flagged in the final report at all.
- **Result, independently reproduced three separate ways before being trusted, given the result was so far outside every established reference range it triggered this project's own "verify before believing" rule**:
  1. `verify_candidate` (their run): **20W-0L-0T, avg margin +79,478** vs the real current champion.
  2. **My own from-scratch re-verification** (separate script, separate module loading, `assert ... is not ...` proven, 16 episodes): **16W-0L-0T, avg margin +84,657** -- champion's own scores in this run ($27,878-$35,393) matched its already-established normal self-play range exactly, ruling out a crippled/misconfigured baseline; the candidate scored $78,094-$133,205, 3-4x higher.
  3. **A full-episode sanity trace** (my own script, not delegated): confirmed genuine, non-exploitative play -- 4 animals bought by day 1, a 2nd quadrant successfully unlocked by day 8 (**something the current champion has never once achieved, anywhere, all session**), herd grown to 11 animals, smooth healthy income growth from day 11 onward, final money $78,653.
  4. **Holdout gate spot-check** (my own script, `kawa` specifically): reported -37,916 avg margin (regressed=False, since the champion's own kawa margin is -124,086); independently got -31,447 -- same story, still a loss but a massively smaller one, consistent with "regressed: False."
- **Follow-up: ran the real, full 150-trial search directly myself** (not delegated -- after two delegated searches today died at their print-timeout before finishing, ran `search.py` directly via `nohup`/background `Bash`, resuming the existing 20-trial study as a warm start, `n_jobs=6`, 22 cores / 30GB available). Completed cleanly end to end this time, no process-death risk since I controlled it directly. **Result: 20W-0L-0T, avg margin +79,294.2 -- essentially identical to the under-tuned 20-trial result (+79,478.4), confirming the architecture's advantage is robust across the search space, not an artifact of a lucky/incomplete search** (the search's own best trial was found early, trial 28 of 169 total, and never meaningfully improved on afterward -- consistent with a wide basin of near-optimal configs). Holdout still passes cleanly (`ektarr`/`nagatakengo` both improved further, `kawa` margin -40,668 vs champion's -124,086, regressed=False). **Independently re-verified this specific (full-search) config myself, separately**: 20W-0L-0T, avg +83,099.1 -- confirms.
- Notes: **this is the most significant finding of the entire session.** A genuinely different dispatch architecture (global greedy bipartite matching instead of a tiered priority queue) combined with a scheduled, non-reactive herd/land-expansion plan succeeds at exactly the thing this project's reactive `in_crisis`-gated architecture has failed at from every angle tried today (land-lock diagnosis, Goldilocks search, joint animal+land search, decoupled crisis gate, staged-target dispatcher bolted onto the OLD dispatcher, day-0 burst v1/v2) -- because it doesn't route through `in_crisis` at all, it has its own narrower, urgency-based CRISIS phase (already found and logged earlier today to structurally resemble but differ from ours) that doesn't choke on routine backlog the way ours does. **Not yet promoted to the live submission** -- this is currently a scratch prototype (`.claude/scratch/pilkwang_rebuild/pilkwang_style_agent/`), not wired into `build_submission.py`, and the search that tuned it only ran 20 of the intended 150 trials, meaning there is likely real additional headroom left unexplored. Next real steps: (a) a full 150-trial search (budgeting for the ported dispatch's slower per-turn cost, confirmed real in Stage 5's own struggle to finish even 20 trials), (b) `build_submission.py` compatibility work to make this a genuinely submittable single-file package, (c) a final decision on whether/how to promote given the scale of this change (a full architecture swap, not a config tweak).

## 2026-08-27 — discovered and documented a real, undocumented game rule: animals with `consecutive_unfed >= 2` escape entirely (full capital loss, no partial refund) -- confirms a real gap in our own ROI math, and adds a sharper causal explanation to the day-0-burst failure
- Command: owner watched a replay and asked why animals appeared to be "sold" near the end of the game, and separately raised that our ROI scoring may not properly account for animals having a much longer payback horizon than crops. Investigated by tracing the exact game rather than guessing.
- **Confirmed directly in the real engine source** (`kaggriculture.py`'s `_daily_refresh_animals`, not `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` -- a real documentation gap, now fixed): an animal tile with `consecutive_unfed >= 2` has the animal removed entirely (`farm["tiles"][y][x] = {"kind": ANIMALS[tile["animal"]]["structure"]}`) -- the whole purchase is lost, no partial value recovered. Not a "sell" (animals cannot be sold at all, only their products/fertilizer -- confirmed in `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`'s own Market Actions section), the animal simply vanishes.
- **In the current champion's own self-play (7 seeds checked, all identical)**: both animals escape deterministically at step 696/day 29, every single game -- caused by the workforce collapsing to just the farmer during the wind-down window (hiring stops when `winding_down`, hired hands expire daily and aren't replaced). Financially close to harmless in this specific case, since it happens with ~1 day left and the agent is already in liquidation mode by then -- but the underlying mechanism is real and would NOT be harmless if it fired earlier.
- **Confirmed it fires mid-game, expensively, under labor stress**: re-checked the day-0-burst-v2 variant (already known to run with `in_crisis=True` for 709/720 days) -- it bought 4 animals on day 0 and **lost all 4 to starvation-escape by day 2 (step 48)**. This is a real, previously-missing piece of why that whole experiment failed so badly -- not just cash exhaustion and the `in_crisis` land gate, but the actual animal purchases themselves getting wiped out within 48 hours of game time.
- **Real gap confirmed in `animal_roi_ok`** (`src/robust_agent/scoring.py`): it projects revenue as a certainty across the animal's full `usable_days` horizon with zero probability-of-loss discount. A crop that goes unwatered decays into a bounded loss (that tile's yield, via the weed mechanic); an animal that goes unfed for 2 days is a total, irreversible loss of the full purchase price with nothing produced. This asymmetry is real and currently unmodeled -- owner's intuition was correct and more consequential than either of us initially framed it as.
- Notes: **documentation fix applied** (`docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`'s Animal Care section now states the rule directly, sourced from the real engine, with both the benign end-of-game case and the costly day-0-burst case cited as evidence). **No code fix applied yet** -- a real candidate for a future improvement would be either (a) a feed-priority safeguard so `consecutive_unfed` never reaches 2 for an existing animal regardless of what else is contending for labor that turn, or (b) discounting `animal_roi_ok`'s projected revenue by a modeled escape-risk probability tied to current labor slack -- neither built or tested here, this entry is the diagnosis, not the fix.

## 2026-08-27 — joint re-tune (order-cap fix + generalized wheat-reservation fix, real 182-trial search): fixes work mechanically, but still loses to the champion head-to-head
- Command: built both previously-reverted fixes into one code variant (`.claude/scratch/joint_retune/robust_agent/`) -- the order-cap priority sort (guarded to only fire on turns that actually exceed 10 orders) and a generalized `_reserved_quantity()` sell-reservation (WHEAT reserved against `info.feed`, extensible to other dual-purpose items later). Ran a real 182-trial Optuna search (own sqlite study, separate from the project's persistent one) over the full ~46-knob space with the variant's code, using a genuinely separate dual-`make_agent` setup (candidate = variant module, champion/self-play pool opponent = real module, `assert ... is not ...` confirmed) -- not a monkeypatch.
- **Process note**: the delegated agy task's own process hit its print-timeout and died silently before it could run the verification step or write a report -- the search itself had completed (182 trials in the sqlite DB, real data) but nothing downstream ran. Picked up from the persisted study directly: extracted the best trial, ran the verification myself using the task's own (inspected and confirmed correct) dual-agent script, then independently re-verified from scratch with a separate script and separate module-loading approach.
- **Result**: `verify_candidate` **0W-20L-0T, avg margin -6,576.6** (via the task's own script). **Independently re-verified**: 0W-16L-0T, avg margin -6,396.7 -- confirms the loss, consistent magnitude.
- **The fixes themselves are confirmed still working correctly at the new tuned config**: traced a full episode -- WHEAT buy+sell orders down to 21+47=68 (from the baseline's 466 for a single agent), an ~85% reduction, so the wheat-churn fix generalizes past the specific config it was built against. Final money in this trace was a healthy $33,311 (in the champion's normal range) -- land still never unlocked (`unlocked_quadrants=['NW']`), consistent with every other angle tried today; these are order-hygiene fixes, not something that touches the `in_crisis` land-expansion constraint at all.
- **Interpretation**: this isn't evidence the fixes are bad -- their own direct effect (measured earlier today, no retuning) was small and roughly neutral, and a single fresh 182-trial search starting from scratch is competing against a champion config refined across this project's entire tuning history (multiple full searches, several promotions). The search converging on something respectable-but-not-champion-beating is the more likely explanation than the fixes themselves being harmful. Not promoted.
- Notes: closes this specific investigation thread (order-cap + wheat-churn fixes, tested standalone and jointly, both honestly negative-to-neutral against the current champion) without contradicting anything found earlier today -- the underlying correctness issues are real and stay documented for whoever next does a full champion re-tune (natural to fold both fixes in from the start of that search, rather than as an add-on to an already-converged config, next time one is warranted, e.g. after any future architecture change).

## 2026-08-27 — traced the champion's own day-0-9 actions and found a real, large wheat buy/sell churn bug; separately checked whether `in_crisis` is unique to us (it isn't -- pilkwang has an analogous, narrower "CRISIS" state, and that exact narrower definition was already tried on our agent and regressed)
- Command: owner asked to see our own champion's actual day-0+ actions (mirroring the earlier "Crop Dusta" replay trace) and asked why no opponent model appears to have an `in_crisis`-style gate.
- **Wheat churn, confirmed real and large**: traced a full 720-step self-play episode. `BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT`: 222 orders (442 units). `SELL WHEAT`: 244 orders (525 units). Combined, **over half (466/905) of all market orders in the entire game are WHEAT buying and immediately reselling the same commodity** -- the emergency-wheat mechanism (`_process_emergency_wheat`, queued first, buys when feed demand exists and wheat stock is 0) and the generic sell loop (which treats WHEAT like any other crop, no awareness it might be earmarked for feed) fight each other every turn: buy for feed, get resold before a FEED action can consume it, wheat hits 0 again, buy again next turn. Neither mechanism was wrong in isolation (both individually verified when added); nobody had checked their interaction until this trace. Also directly compounds the order-cap issue from the day-0-burst-v2 entry above (wastes ~2 of the 10-order budget per cycle).
- **Fix built and verified**: reserve `len(info.feed)` units of WHEAT from the sell loop's sellable quantity (`src/robust_agent/market.py`, the `sellable` loop). Rebuilt, verified with the same before/after self-play protocol used for the order-cap fix (fresh config both sides, no monkeypatching, `optimize.SEARCH_SEEDS`, 20 episodes): **9W-11L-0T, avg margin -196.05** -- a small, near-neutral effect, not a clean win, but far smaller in magnitude than the order-cap fix's -5,135.6 regression. Consistent with the same lesson: the current tuned config wasn't calibrated for the corrected behavior, so a raw drop-in isn't a fair test of whether the underlying fix is good. **Reverted from live `src/robust_agent/market.py`** (rebuilt `submission/main.py` to match) rather than promote an unverified, non-positive change -- same discipline as the order-cap fix. Worth a proper Optuna re-tune, ideally together with the order-cap fix since both affect the same order-budget/cash-flow surface.
- **`in_crisis` -- checked directly whether other models have anything like it, rather than assume.** `src/opponents/pilkwang_agent.py` DOES have an analogous mechanism: a `"CRISIS"` policy phase (`_policy_phase`, line ~733), triggered by `at_risk_animals + at_risk_crops > workers` OR `shed_load + carried_load >= 95`. The key difference from ours: pilkwang's trigger is **urgency-based** (`at_risk_*` counts only tiles with `consecutive_unfed`/`consecutive_unwatered >= 1` -- i.e. things already neglected at least one turn, on the verge of real damage) vs. ours, which is **volume-based** (`crisis_backlog` counts ALL pending harvest/water/feed/weed/fertilize tasks regardless of urgency, compared against a flat ratio). Ours fires on routine future work; pilkwang's only fires on stuff about to actually decay.
- **But this exact narrower/urgency-based definition was already tried on our own agent, twice, and regressed both times** (see this file's 2026-08-23 and 2026-08-26 "pilkwang-style crisis redesign" entries: `consecutive_unwatered`/`consecutive_unfed >= 1` trigger, tested pre- and post-batch-hiring-fix, both times a clean regression, the second time worse at higher N). So the honest answer isn't "we invented an unnecessary gate other models don't have" -- pilkwang has one too, just narrower. The real finding (already established, reconfirmed by checking pilkwang's actual source directly rather than assuming) is that **pilkwang's specific crisis definition doesn't transplant cleanly onto our dispatcher** -- consistent with every other "port one piece of pilkwang" attempt this project has tried (dispatch-rewrite, staged-target zones, this) -- his mechanisms work as part of one coordinated system (fixed schedule + herd targets + reserved zones + this specific crisis definition, all tuned together), not as an interchangeable part. `in_crisis` in its current (volume-based) form remains load-bearing for THIS architecture specifically, not because a better definition doesn't exist anywhere, but because ours hasn't been rebuilt as a coordinated whole the way pilkwang's has.
- Notes: two real, concrete findings from directly tracing our own agent's actual play rather than reasoning about it abstractly -- a genuine, sizeable, previously-unnoticed inefficiency (wheat churn) with a built, verified, currently-neutral fix pending a re-tune, and a definitive, source-verified answer to the `in_crisis` question that corrects an understandable but incorrect premise (no other model has this) while confirming the deeper, already-established finding (transplanting isolated pilkwang mechanisms doesn't work on this architecture) still holds even for this specific piece.

## 2026-08-27 — day-0 burst v2 (land knobs included in the search this time): still loses, deeper cause identified (`in_crisis`, not land affordability) — and a real order-cap bug found + fixed + reverted pending a proper re-tune
- Command: direct follow-up to the first day-0 burst loss. Re-ran the same `burst_agent` mechanism with `land_reserve_multiple`/`land_min_days_left` added to the Optuna search this time (previously inherited unchanged from the baseline, identified as the likely cause of the first loss).
- **Result**: `verify_candidate` **0W-20L-0T, avg margin -12,805.7** (agy's run). **Independently re-verified**: 0W-16L-0T, avg margin -9,877.1 -- confirms the loss. Land still never unlocked (`unlocked_quadrants=['NW']` at game end, traced directly).
- **Deeper cause, this time confirmed by trace**: even with `land_reserve_multiple` searched down to 2.48 (cheaper cushion), the search's best config spends 709 of 720 days with `in_crisis=True` -- the burst's own day-0 planting/animal/hire spike permanently trips the SAME `crisis_backlog_ratio` gate this project has diagnosed all day, which preempts `land_phase_ok` before `land_reserve_multiple` is ever even evaluated. Land affordability was never the real bottleneck; `in_crisis` is, again -- consistent with every other "unlock land" angle tried today. Not promoted.
- **Separately, in the course of investigating this**: found a real bug via direct measurement -- `market_orders()`'s `orders[:10]` truncation (matching the real `maxMarketOrdersPerTurn=10` competition limit) keeps orders in raw build order (sells first, hire/animal/land last), so on turns with heavy selling this project's own trace showed 19/2160 sampled turns exceeding 10 total orders, with real `HIRE` orders silently dropped every time (up to 3 in one turn). Built and verified a fix (priority-sort only on the rare turn that actually exceeds 10, `HIRE`/`BUY_ANIMAL`/`BUY_LAND`/`BUY_PRODUCT` protected ahead of `SELL`) -- confirmed via trace it correctly rescues the previously-dropped `HIRE` orders without touching decision logic or the `remaining` cash-tracking sequence. **First verification attempt showed a huge, implausible +26k margin improvement -- caught as a bug in the test script itself** (a broken "before" reconstruction that returned `None` on ~99% of turns instead of the real original behavior, not the actual pre-fix agent). Corrected the reconstruction and re-ran: **the real fix, evaluated fairly against the actual original code, LOSES 0W-20L-0T avg -5,135.6 against the current tuned config.** Diagnosis: the fix is real and behaviorally non-trivial (unlike an earlier no-op bug fix today), but the current champion's tuned parameters were implicitly selected around the old buggy throttle -- recovering a few extra `HIRE` orders per game nudges effective hiring rate up slightly, and this architecture's fragility to exactly that (established repeatedly today, e.g. the Goldilocks `max_structures` finding) means the existing config is no longer well-matched to the corrected order logic.
- **Reverted the fix from live `src/robust_agent/market.py`** (back to plain `orders[:10]`, `submission/main.py` rebuilt to match) rather than leave it applied-but-unverified -- the underlying correctness issue is real and worth revisiting, but only via a proper Optuna re-tune around the fixed logic (comparable to how `batch hiring` earlier in this project's history was a real win specifically because it was tuned together with the rest of the config, not dropped in cold). Not done yet.
- Notes: a genuine two-for-one session -- closes the day-0-burst thread with a clear final cause (`in_crisis`, confirmed the true root blocker across every angle tried: land-lock diagnosis, Goldilocks search, joint search, decoupled gate, staged dispatcher, burst v1 cash-affordability, burst v2 in_crisis), and separately surfaces a real, verified (if not yet net-positive) order-cap correctness bug for a future re-tune. Also a good example of the "verify surprising results" discipline catching a bug in a verification SCRIPT, not just in agy's own output -- the +26k number was mine, not agy's, and it was wrong.

## 2026-08-27 — day-0 "opening burst" (hire+animals+seeds all at once, modeled on a real top-player replay): loses cleanly, cash-exhaustion diagnosed, land still never unlocks
- Command: owner pulled a real Kaggle leaderboard replay (top player "Crop Dusta" self-play, `boardSize=10`) and found its turn-1 day-0 action was a single fixed burst: 4x HIRE, 2x BUY_ANIMAL COW, 2x BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP, BUY_SEED MELON 5, BUY_SEED WHEAT 8 -- nearly the whole $3000 starting balance, no staging -- and that this player unlocks a 2nd quadrant by day 6, 3rd by day 9-10, with no sign of the paralysis our own `in_crisis`-gated champion shows. Built a day-0-only extension of the existing `opening_hires_day0` pattern (`opening_structures_day0`, `opening_animals_day0`, `opening_seeds_day0` -- all new knobs, default to today's exact behavior, no permanent gate loosening) via agy, then tuned the new knobs (plus `max_hires_per_day`/`pasture_target_ratio`) with a real Optuna search before ever comparing to the champion.
- **Result**: `verify_candidate` **0W-20L-0T, avg margin -12,658.6** (agy's run). **Independently re-verified from scratch** (`importlib`-loaded separate modules, no monkeypatching, 16 episodes, `optimize.SEARCH_SEEDS`): **0W-16L-0T, avg margin -12,629.1** -- confirms the loss, consistent magnitude. Holdout: daisy023 still 20W (+2,968.8), chaitanyajamble 0W (-12,180.8), pilkwang 0W (-110,610.5) -- worse across the board than the champion's established ranges.
- **Land still never unlocks**: independently traced a full 720-step episode of the best-found config -- 0 `BUY_LAND` orders issued, `unlocked_quadrants=['NW']` at game end, final money $22,714 (well below the champion's typical $30-40k range).
- **Causal diagnosis (agy's, independently spot-checked)**: the burst legitimately empties the day-0 cash reserve, but the search's best-found config still inherited `land_reserve_multiple=3.81`/`land_min_days_left=16` UNCHANGED from the baseline -- neither was included in this search's tuned parameter set. With cash exhausted early, the agent can't rebuild the ~4x-cost cushion `land_reserve_multiple` demands before the day-16 cutoff, so land purchase never becomes affordable in time regardless of the burst. This is a real gap in the search space actually tried, not proof the underlying idea is dead: the burst was tuned in isolation from the land-purchase knobs it directly competes with for cash.
- Notes: **the specific mechanism as searched loses cleanly and reproducibly** -- not promoted. But unlike most of today's other closed ideas, this one has an identified, cheap next step rather than a dead end: re-run the search with `land_reserve_multiple`/`land_min_days_left` included in the tuned parameter set alongside the burst knobs, so the search can find a config where the burst's cash cost and the land-affordability cushion are balanced against each other instead of fighting. Given this project's base rate on "unlock more animals/land" attempts (the large majority regress even after a legitimate-looking fix), the expected outcome of that follow-up is still more likely another loss than a win -- but it hasn't been tried yet, and this is the first attempt this session to test a bundled, replay-grounded opening burst rather than a single isolated knob change.

## 2026-08-27 — restructured `agent.py`/`optimize.py` into packages (`robust_agent/`, `tuning/`) with real classes and a lint/complexity toolchain, via a 3-stage gated agy workflow — verified behavior-identical at every stage, caught and fixed 3 real bugs the restructuring itself introduced
- Command: owner asked for the two flat ~1000-1200-line files split into idiomatic modules/classes ("agent file, and under that the agent.py stuff; optimize file and under that the optimize stuff"), with `ruff`/`radon` complexity tooling added, done via a multi-stage `agy` (`gemini-3.1-pro-high`) workflow rather than one shot. Ran 3 gated stages, each independently re-verified before proceeding to the next -- never trusted a stage's own self-report.
- **Stage 1 (plan only, no code):** agy read `agent.py`/`optimize.py`/`build_submission.py`, researched real conventions for single-file-submission competition code, and proposed `robust_agent/` (`config`/`scoring`/`scanning`/`market`/`dispatch`/`agent`) + `tuning/` (`config`/`search_space`/`simulation`/`scoring`/`verification`/`optimize`) packages, 3 classes (`Dispatcher`, `FarmState`, `HoldoutGate`) only where real state exists, and a `build_submission.py` rewrite to keep flattening to one file (Kaggle requires this). **Independently re-ran `radon cc` myself before approving**: confirmed exactly `market_orders`=64(F), `plan_units`=60(F), `main`=25(D) as cited.
- **Stage 2 (implementation, scratch only):** agy ported the code into the new structure. Claimed a perfect tie vs. the real `src/agent.py` except a seed-8042 "positional asymmetry" it explained via 3 checks. **Independently re-ran the tie-check myself from scratch (16 episodes, no shared code with agy's script)**: 1W-1L-14T, avg margin exactly 0.00 -- confirmed clean, and independently reproduced the seed-8042 explanation by running the ORIGINAL unmodified agent against itself (same ±263.0 split). Also caught and corrected two of agy's own findings that didn't hold up: the "`crop_pool` closure bug" claim was wrong (direct inspection: Phase 1 reassigns in `plan_units`'s own scope, Phase 2 correctly uses `nonlocal` -- neither shadows), and the "`state["unit_targets"]` leak causes noisy Optuna outcomes" claim was wrong (every real call site in `optimize.py` creates a fresh `make_agent()` per episode, so there's no cross-episode reuse in this project's actual evaluation pipeline).
- **Stage 3 (real complexity reduction + lint):** `plan_units` had only gone from F(60)->F(59) in stage 2 (relocated, not decomposed) -- had agy actually break `Dispatcher.plan_units` into separate methods. agy's own re-verification this time claimed a NEW split on seed 1042 (previously clean) alongside 8042. **Re-ran my own tie-check on the current code and got a clean 1W-1L-14T again** (only the known 8042 artifact) -- agy's own stage-3 script had an apparent bug (unreproducible), not the actual code, which I verified directly.
- **Promotion to live `src/` (done by me, not agy)**, with real verification at every step, not trusted from any report:
  - Found `tuning/optimize.py` didn't actually import (`DEFAULT_STORAGE`/`LOG_PATH`/`BEST_CONFIG_PATH` were used but never defined in the new `tuning/config.py`) -- a real bug agy's "ready for phase 3!" claim missed because it never actually tried running the `tuning` package's CLI. Fixed by porting the missing path constants over.
  - Smoke-tested the real CLI (`python -m tuning.optimize --n-trials 2 ...`) before trusting it -- this genuinely ran against the live resumable Optuna study and correctly logged an (harmless, rejected) outcome, but exposed a SECOND real bug: a misindented f-string terminator in the ported `_log_outcome` appended 4 stray spaces instead of a blank line before the next entry, corrupting this file's structure. Fixed in `tuning/optimize.py`; the resulting malformed LOG.md entry (the one directly below this one, dated the same day, `n-trials 2`) was repaired by hand.
  - Wrote `src/agent.py`/`src/optimize.py` as thin compatibility shims (re-exporting from the new packages, including a `verify_holdout` wrapper preserving the old standalone-function signature now that the real logic lives on `HoldoutGate`) so every existing caller (`optimize.py`'s `from agent import ...`, `run_match.py`'s dynamic loader, historical scratch scripts) keeps working unchanged.
  - Ran `ruff check --fix` for the lint pass -- **this stripped every import out of both compatibility shims as "unused"** (their only purpose is re-export, ruff can't know that), fully breaking both files. Caught immediately by re-running the shim-import smoke test right after. Fixed properly with `# noqa: F401` + a `pyproject.toml` `per-file-ignores` entry for both shim files, then re-verified the shims still work.
  - **Final, most important check**: rebuilt `submission/main.py` from the new `build_submission.py` and ran it head-to-head against a pre-promotion backup of the old `submission/main.py`, 10 episodes, real `play_episode`/`SEARCH_SEEDS`: **0W-0L-10T, avg margin 0.0 -- a perfect tie on the actual artifact that gets submitted to Kaggle.** Also confirmed `build_submission.py` is deterministic (byte-identical output on a second run) and every file in `src/`, `robust_agent/`, `tuning/` compiles cleanly.
- **`radon cc` complexity, before -> after (live, final numbers):** `market_orders` 64(F) -> 25(D); `plan_units` 60(F) -> decomposed into `Dispatcher` methods, worst is `_do_shed_pickups_and_drops` at 15(C); `optimize.py`'s `main` 25(D) -> 14(C). `ruff check` passes clean on the full `src/robust_agent`+`src/tuning`+shims tree (`pyproject.toml`, `max-complexity=15`).
- Notes: **this is the most heavily-verified change of the entire session** -- a 3-stage agy workflow where NONE of the three stages' own "done"/"verified" claims were taken at face value, each independently re-checked, and the promotion step itself (done directly, not delegated) surfaced 3 more real bugs (missing config constants, a LOG.md-corrupting formatting bug, and a self-inflicted `ruff --fix` regression) that no amount of trusting agy's reports would have caught. `src/best_config.json` is untouched -- this is a pure architecture change, and the final gate that matters (the actual `submission/main.py` artifact, old vs. new, direct self-play) is a clean, exact tie.

## 2026-08-27 — full-repo code + doc cleanup: fixed a real cash-tracking bug, removed ~140 lines of dead RL-hook code now that `train_rl.py`/`rl_policy.py` are gone, verified behavior-neutral
- Command: delegated a strictly READ-ONLY bug/doc-staleness audit to agy (`gemini-3.1-pro-high`, `.claude/scratch/full_audit/REPORT.md`) across all of `src/*.py` and `docs/*.md`. Findings reviewed and independently verified before acting on any of them (per this project's standing practice); the audit itself made no source-tree edits (confirmed via mtimes -- `src/agent.py`/`optimize.py`/`best_config.json` untouched by the run).
- **Real bug, confirmed and fixed**: `market_orders`'s emergency wheat buy (`src/agent.py`, ~line 918) ordered `min(len(info["feed"]), 3)` units of WHEAT but only deducted 1x `base_price("WHEAT")` from the running `remaining` cash tracker regardless of quantity -- under-tracking spend by up to 2x base price when ordering 2-3 units in one turn. Fixed to deduct `base_price("WHEAT") * wheat_units`.
- **Dead code removed**: the entire "RL policy hook" block in `src/agent.py` (`KNOB_SPECS`, `KNOB_KEYS`, `FEATURE_NAMES`, `RL_MONEY_SCALE`, `extract_features`, `decode_knobs`, and `make_agent`'s `policy_fn` parameter/branch) -- ~140 lines, entirely unreachable now that `train_rl.py` (its only caller) no longer exists in the repo. `sell_backlog_multiple` config key flagged by the audit as "dead" was already known and documented as intentionally unused (not a new finding).
- **Verification**: rebuilt `submission/main.py` (succeeded, no missing-symbol errors from the AST extractor). Since these changes predate any single git commit boundary (uncommitted work stacked on uncommitted work), verified by diffing a before/after copy of just this change (dead-code removal + wheat-cost fix) against an unmodified copy, both loaded as separate modules, direct self-play, `optimize.SEARCH_SEEDS`, 10 episodes: **0W-0L-10T, avg margin 0.0 -- exact tie in every single episode.** The wheat-buy bug's fix had zero observed effect in this run (consistent with the champion's current ~1-2 animal count keeping `len(info["feed"])` at 1 in the emergency-buy path most of the time, so the old and new deduction amounts coincided) but is a real correctness fix worth keeping for any future config where more animals are in play simultaneously.
- Also: deleted `CLAUDE.md` (content migrated into a new top-level `AGENT.md`, which also documents the working conventions this session established -- no monkeypatching, fresh-config-per-comparison, scratch-file discipline, etc.), deleted 13 root-level `test_*.py` debug scripts and `summary.txt` (one-off scratch output from earlier in the day, superseded by `docs/AGENT_DESIGN_NOTES.md`'s existing writeup of the same finding -- confirmed via grep before deleting).
- Notes: not promoted/applied to `best_config.json` (nothing here is a config change) -- this is a code-hygiene pass, verified not to alter agent behavior. `docs/tests/CONFIG_INFRASTRUCTURE_NOTES.md` and `docs/AGENT_DESIGN_NOTES.md` still reference config toggles/values removed in earlier sessions (`animal_enabled`, `enable_coop`, `buy_fertilizer`, old `max_structures`/`risk_sensitivity` values) -- doc text fixes to follow.

## 2026-08-27 — built and tested a genuine STAGED-TARGET dispatcher (pilkwang-style pre-reserved zones + day-indexed herd/land targets) as a real architecture change, not another reactive-dispatcher knob search — loses decisively, with a concrete causal diagnosis
- Command: owner explicitly asked to try the one door none of today's/yesterday's knob searches ever opened -- a non-reactive, staged-target dispatch architecture modeled on `pilkwang_agent.py`'s real mechanism (`_reserved_animal_slots`/`_herd_targets`: pre-reserved per-quadrant animal zones + a day-indexed herd-size target, replacing our agent's fully reactive per-turn priority recompute + `in_crisis`/ROI-gated spending). Delegated the build to agy (`gemini-3.1-pro-high`), with an explicit mandate to treat day 0 as its own question: build with day-0 logic completely unchanged first (reusing the champion's existing `opening_*` day-0 override), and only add separate day-0 handling if the staged model showed it was needed. `.claude/scratch/staged_dispatcher/staged_agent.py`.
- **Day 0 decision**: left entirely unchanged from the champion (`opening_*` params reused verbatim) -- the staged architecture only replaces day 1+ mid/late-game target-setting (hardcoded day-indexed herd size and a 2nd land-quadrant purchase on day 5). Correct call per the mandate; isolates the mid-game architecture question from day-0 setup risk.
- **Result**: `verify_candidate` **0W-6L-0T, avg margin -30,587.2** (agy's own run). **Independently re-verified from scratch, no patching of any kind** (`.claude/scratch/staged_dispatcher/independent_verify.py`, two genuinely separate modules loaded via `importlib.util`, `assert real_agent_mod.make_agent is not staged_mod.make_agent`, 10 episodes instead of agy's 6, `optimize.SEARCH_SEEDS`): **0W-10L-0T, avg margin -27,949.1** -- confirms the loss, consistent magnitude.
- Methodology note: agy's own comparison script used `unittest.mock.patch("src.optimize.make_agent", side_effect=...)` with object-identity dispatch (`if config is candidate_config: ...`) -- technically a form of monkeypatching, which the spec explicitly banned. Checked whether this was the same class of bug that invalidated the earlier decoupled-land-crisis result: it wasn't -- `verify_candidate` passes the exact same config objects through by reference (`src/optimize.py:580-581`), so the identity check correctly routed candidate calls to the staged module and champion calls to the real one. Confirmed sound by the from-scratch, patch-free re-verification above landing on the same result.
- **Causal diagnosis** (traced first 10 days, both agents side by side): the staged agent hit its hardcoded `staged_land_day2=5` threshold with ~$1,006 cash and bought the 2nd land quadrant immediately, bypassing the reactive `land_roi_ok` gate entirely. That dropped cash to ~$6, too little to afford $10 emergency wheat when feed ran out -- its first animal starved to death by day 8. The champion's dynamic ROI gate delayed the same purchase until cash flow justified it, keeping the animal alive and winning comfortably.
- Notes: **a real, informative negative result, not a repeat of a known-bad idea** -- this is the first time the staged-target architecture itself (not just its adjacent knobs) has actually been built and tested. It closes cleanly: the failure mode is not "day 0 was broken" (it wasn't touched) but that a hardcoded day-indexed spending schedule, once decoupled from real-time ROI/cash-flow feedback, can commit to a purchase the farm's actual finances can't support -- exactly the kind of risk the existing reactive ROI/crisis gates exist to prevent. A staged architecture might still be viable with its target schedule ALSO gated by a live cash/ROI check rather than day-number alone, but that's a different, untested design, not what was tried here. Not promoted.

## 2026-08-26 — decoupled land-crisis gate re-tested with a CORRECT, verified-no-monkeypatch comparison: definitively closes land expansion for this architecture
- Command: direct re-do of the invalidated decoupled-land-crisis result above, this time with an explicit, verified ban on monkeypatching `optimize.py`'s internals -- confirmed by reading the actual comparison script: `REAL_AGENT_MOD = load_module("src/agent.py", ...)` and `VARIANT_MOD = load_module(".claude/scratch/decoupled_land_crisis/agent.py", ...)` are loaded as two genuinely separate modules, with `champion_fn = REAL_AGENT_MOD.make_agent(...)` and `candidate_fn = VARIANT_MOD.make_agent(...)` never aliased onto each other. 25-trial search over `land_crisis_backlog_ratio`/`land_utilization_threshold`/`land_reserve_multiple`/`land_min_days_left` only, animal-related knobs held fixed at the current champion's values.
- Result: best-found config vs. the REAL current champion (12 episodes, `optimize.SEARCH_SEEDS`): **0W-1L-11T, avg margin -21.9** -- statistically a tie, not a win. But tracing this "best" config's actual play shows **0 `BUY_LAND` orders** -- the search itself discovered that the only way to avoid a severe loss is to tune the gate so restrictively (`land_min_days_left=18`, a tight `land_utilization_threshold`) that land never actually unlocks anyway, converging back to behaving like the champion.
- Notes: **this fully and cleanly closes land expansion for this architecture, from both directions now.** Every config that genuinely unlocks land (the original monkeypatch-invalidated attempt at `land_crisis_backlog_ratio=7.31`, independently re-verified: 0W-10L, -26,266.0) loses severely; the best config that avoids losing does so by suppressing land purchases back to zero. There is no tuning of a decoupled land-crisis gate, at least within this search space, that both unlocks land and remains competitive -- the mechanism is code-correct (land CAN be made to buy) but the current reactive dispatcher genuinely cannot absorb a second quadrant's labor demand without disrupting the animal/crop balance that makes the rest of the champion work. Not promoted. This closes the entire land-expansion investigation thread from today (land-lock diagnosis -> Goldilocks-style search -> joint animal+land search -> decoupled gate, invalidated -> decoupled gate, correctly re-verified) with a single consistent conclusion.

## 2026-08-26 — decoupled land-crisis-gate idea genuinely unlocks land (a session first) and is mechanically correct, but the claimed "passed verify_candidate" result was INVALID -- a monkeypatch bug made it fight itself, not the real champion
- Command: today's land-lock finding showed land and animals share the exact same `in_crisis`/`crisis_backlog_ratio` gate, which is why loosening it for land breaks the animal economics that already work. Tried the more surgical fix: a separate, independently-tunable `land_crisis_backlog_ratio` computing its own `land_in_crisis` for the land gate specifically, leaving the animal-facing `in_crisis` completely untouched. **Confirmed by direct code read**: `.claude/scratch/decoupled_land_crisis/agent.py` line 1006 computes `land_in_crisis` from the new key (falling back to the shared one if unset) and uses it only in `land_phase_ok`; line 871's original `in_crisis` (used by `animal_phase_ok`) is byte-identical to the current champion's, exactly as intended. A 20-trial search found `land_crisis_backlog_ratio=7.31` (vs the shared `crisis_backlog_ratio=0.99`) as the best value.
- **Land genuinely unlocks under this config -- independently worth confirming on its own, separate from the invalid claim below**: 7 `BUY_LAND` orders, ending with 4 unlocked quadrants (`['NW','NE','SE','SW']`) in the traced episode -- the first time ANY variant tested today has actually gotten land expansion working at all.
- **The reported "verify_candidate PASSED, 6W-2L-2T, +6,162.2" claim is INVALID, caught by independent re-verification.** Its own `search.py` contains `optimize_mod.make_agent = VARIANT_MOD.make_agent` -- a global monkeypatch of `optimize.py`'s `make_agent` function to point at the variant's code. Since the real `verify_candidate()` internally does `candidate = make_agent(candidate_config); champion = make_agent(champion_config)`, this monkeypatch means BOTH sides ran on the VARIANT's code, differing only by which config dict was passed in -- it was never actually tested against the real, current champion (`src/agent.py`) at all, despite nominally "calling optimize.py's own verify_candidate." Independently re-ran the comparison correctly (real `agent.py` for the champion side, the variant's code only for the candidate side, same `SEARCH_SEEDS`/seat-alternation pattern as the real function): **0W-10L-0T, avg margin -26,266.0 -- a severe loss, the opposite of what was reported.**
- Notes: **land expansion CAN be mechanically unlocked via a decoupled gate, but this specific tuning is not remotely competitive with the real champion -- not promoted, and the reported verification was worthless due to the monkeypatch.** A genuine, fair test would need its own search run through a CORRECTLY-scoped verification (champion side must use the real, current `agent.py`, never the variant's code) -- this hasn't been done yet; the search that WAS done only tuned the variant against itself under two different configs, which explains why it looked good (its own land-unlocking behavior scored consistently under its own code either way) without ever being compared to what actually matters. Real methodology lesson: an instruction to "use the real verify_candidate" can still be silently defeated by monkeypatching the module's internals to redirect what "make_agent" resolves to -- checking that a delegated task literally imported and called the real function isn't enough; the actual comparison it performs must be independently reconstructed and checked when a result is this important.

## 2026-08-26 — 2nd `optimize.py` promotion of the day (`--n-trials 150`, continued the resumable study, full ~46-knob space): clean 10W-0L verification, holdout passed, independently confirmed better on all 3 tracked benchmarks
- Command: `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 150 --n-jobs 8` (full space, not narrowly scoped like the several targeted crisis/labor searches earlier today) run as a genuine continuation, not a repeat, of the study that produced the first promotion.
- Result: dedicated verification (10 episodes) **10W-0L-0T, avg margin +2,120.2** -- a clean sweep, not a marginal edge. Holdout check (`ektarr`/`nagatakengo`/`kawa`) passed cleanly on all three (`ok`). `best_config.json` updated (`roi_margin` 2.84->3.00, `crisis_backlog_ratio` 0.75->0.99, `lambda_labor` 3.00->3.94, `animal_purchase_last_day` 6->7); `submission/main.py` rebuilt to match.

All three metrics improved over the already-once-promoted champion, not just the search's own opponent pool.

## 2026-08-26 — RL pipeline readiness check: torch is fine, but the existing checkpoint is now shape-incompatible with the current config -- caught an environment mistake in the delegated check along the way
- Command: `src/train_rl.py`/`rl_policy.py` hadn't been touched or checked at all today, despite `src/agent.py` being substantially rewritten (renames) and `src/best_config.json` being fully replaced by `optimize.py`'s promotion. Delegated a bounded readiness check (import + checkpoint-shape check only, explicitly NOT a real multi-hour training run) to agy.
- **agy's check was wrong, caught immediately**: it reported `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'torch'` and concluded the pipeline is "not currently usable as-is." Independently re-checked: `torch` (2.13.0+cu130) is genuinely installed in the project's own `.venv` -- confirmed by directly importing it there. agy's check simply ran without activating the venv (bare system Python, which indeed has no torch) rather than a real environment problem. `train_rl` imports cleanly once the venv is active.
- **Real finding, completed directly after catching the environment issue**: `output/training/best.pt` (generation 235, `best_margin=-2319.6`, saved 2026-08-21) has output-head shapes `alpha_head`/`beta_head`: `(12, 2048)` -- trained for a 12-knob action space. The CURRENT `agent.py`'s `KNOB_SPECS` now has **46** entries (input side matches fine: both checkpoint and current `FEATURE_NAMES` are 17). This checkpoint cannot be resumed or loaded against the current config space -- the knob set has grown substantially since generation 235 was last trained (extra `priority_weight_*` entries and others added later).
- Notes: **the RL pipeline itself is not broken -- but any future attempt to continue training from `best.pt`/`latest.pt` needs to start a fresh run, not `--resume`, or it will fail on a state_dict shape mismatch.** Also a repeatable lesson for delegated checks generally: an environment-dependent negative result ("X isn't installed/doesn't work") from a fresh agent process should be independently re-verified in the actual project environment before being trusted, the same way a numeric claim would be -- a missing `source .venv/bin/activate` is exactly the kind of silent, plausible-looking false negative that's easy to miss.

## 2026-08-26 — joint animal+land search, built around the real `verify_candidate`/`verify_holdout` gates from the start this time — correctly caught and rejected a degenerate solution
- Command: applied the lesson from the land-search non-transitivity trap immediately -- searched the combined knob space (`lambda_labor`, `crisis_backlog_ratio`, `max_structures`, `roi_margin`, `animal_reserve_multiple`, `land_reserve_multiple`, `land_utilization_threshold`, `hire_backlog_ratio`, `max_hires_per_day`) but required `optimize.py`'s own `verify_candidate()` (direct self-play vs. the current champion) to pass BEFORE trusting anything against third-party opponents, not after.
- Result: the search's best-found config picked **`max_structures=0`** -- a degenerate solution that structurally forbids building ANY pasture/coop, i.e. gives up on animals entirely, and happened to score well against the specific third-party pool (chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang) it was evaluated against. `verify_candidate` (10 episodes, seeded, direct vs. current champion) immediately caught this: **0W-10L-0T, avg margin -12,185.8** -- a rout, not a close call. `verify_holdout` was correctly never run since the first gate failed outright.
- Notes: **the gate worked exactly as designed this time -- no after-the-fact correction needed.** Also a real methodology note for any future narrow re-search of this knob set: `max_structures`'s search range should probably exclude 0 (or very low values) unless deliberately testing a "no animals" strategy, since it's a degenerate corner that can fool an objective scored only against a fixed opponent pool. This closes out today's "search around the crisis/labor dynamics for more animals or land" line -- three separate targeted searches (2-animal Goldilocks: real win, already promoted; 3-animal generalization: statistical tie, not an improvement; land expansion: fails direct promotion gate; joint animal+land: fails even worse) converge on the same picture -- the 2-animal Goldilocks config already found by `optimize.py`'s own full run is very close to a local optimum for this architecture, not a starting point for further easy gains via the same lever.

## 2026-08-26 — land-Goldilocks search candidate looked like a real improvement against 3 third-party opponents, but LOSES the champion's own direct head-to-head promotion gate — a genuine non-transitivity trap, not noise
- Command: follow-up to the land-lock finding above -- tried the same "search around the crisis dynamics instead of loosening the gate directly" approach that worked for animal count. 25-trial search (`lambda_labor`, `crisis_backlog_ratio`, `land_utilization_threshold`, `land_reserve_multiple`, `hire_backlog_ratio`, `max_hires_per_day` free) found a config agy reported as "slightly better" (40%->66.7% on chaitanyajamble, N=15). Independently re-verified at N=30 with fresh seeds (101000-101029): the effect shrank but was still real and consistent across ALL THREE tracked opponents simultaneously -- chaitanyajamble 30.0%->46.7% (+777 margin), daisy023 +1,215.8, pilkwang +4,219.0. Land still never unlocks either way (0 `BUY_LAND` orders, both configs).
- **This looked like the strongest candidate of the day -- consistent gains on 3/3 opponents, not a single noisy matchup -- so it was run through the project's own actual promotion gate (`optimize.py`'s `verify_candidate`/`verify_holdout`) instead of just trusted.** Result: `verify_candidate` (10 episodes, direct candidate-vs-champion self-play, the project's real promotion criterion) gives the candidate **4W-6L, avg margin -475.6 -- it LOSES to the champion head-to-head** and never even reaches the holdout-check stage.
- Notes: **a real, important lesson distinct from every other "false positive" caught today.** Those (real_options_maturity, 3-animal generalization) were plain small-sample noise that vanished at larger N. This one is different: the larger-N numbers against chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang were genuinely real and reproducible, not noise -- the candidate really does perform better against each of those three opponents SEPARATELY than the champion does. But performing better against shared third-party opponents does not imply winning a DIRECT head-to-head match -- exactly the non-transitivity failure mode `docs/tests/LOG.md`'s own v11-revert history and `HOLDOUT_ROTATIONS`' design comment already warn about (a config can look better against everyone it's compared to while still losing to the specific opponent that matters, the current champion, because match dynamics between two adaptive/reactive agents aren't the same as either one's dynamics against a fixed third party). **Any future "found a better config" claim must be run through `verify_candidate` (direct self-play) before being treated as promotable, not just checked against the same fixed benchmark trio.** Not promoted.

## 2026-08-26 — the new champion NEVER expands land (permanently stuck at 1 quadrant) — real, structural, independently verified; a sibling test claiming a related result was INVALID due to a caught config bug
- **`land_timing_new_champion` (agy, verified independently):** traced the current champion's `BUY_LAND` behavior for the first time since the optimization promotion. Finding: `BUY_LAND` never fires, ever -- confirmed by agy's 30-episode trace AND independently re-confirmed directly (5 fresh seeds, `final unlocked_quadrants` always exactly `['NW']`, zero `BUY_LAND` orders in any episode). Root cause: `land_phase_ok`'s `not in_crisis` condition -- a single fully-planted quadrant generates enough daily watering/harvest backlog (~64 tasks) to permanently exceed the new, much tighter `crisis_backlog_ratio=0.745` threshold (~13 units * 0.745 ≈ 9.7) from around day 5 onward, for the rest of the game. A narrow fix (drop `not in_crisis` from the land gate alone) was tried and caused a severe regression (daisy023 100%->0% win rate) -- expanding land while every OTHER system still behaves as if in crisis just starves the new land of workers too. Not a quick fix; would need the backlog definition itself reconsidered, which is out of scope for a narrow test. **This is a real, previously-unknown structural limitation of the promoted champion, not a blocker to its promotion** (it still measurably beats the old champion on every tracked metric) -- but land expansion is completely dead code in practice right now.
- **`separate_crisis_throttle` test result is INVALID, not a real finding -- caught via independent verification.** This sibling test (separating `lambda_labor`'s economic signal from a dedicated hard planting throttle) reported "hurts, and neither champion nor variant ever buys an animal (0.0 average both)" -- a result that directly contradicted every other measurement of the champion today (which reliably buys 1-2 animals) and a chaitanyajamble win rate (16%) outside the established 26-53% range. Investigated: agy wrote its files to the WRONG path (`~/.claude/scratch/` instead of the project's `~/Projects/Kaggriculture/.claude/scratch/`) -- found via a broader filesystem search once the expected path came up empty. Once found, its own saved `best_config.json` snapshot showed `lambda_labor: 0.0389` for BOTH the "champion baseline" and the variant -- the champion-side comparison was contaminated with the variant's modified value, not run against the real champion (`lambda_labor: 3.00`) at all. **Discard this test's conclusion entirely; the underlying question (does separating the throttle from the economic signal help) remains genuinely untested**, not closed.
- Notes: two lessons from one batch. (1) A result with numbers outside every established reference range is a hard stop signal, not a detail to note in passing -- both anomalies here (0.0 animal buys, 16% win rate) were caught specifically because they were checked against known ranges before being trusted, per this project's own standing practice. (2) Always verify a delegated task's claimed output PATH actually contains what it claims, especially when the project's own directory convention (relative paths, `.claude/scratch/<name>/`) could resolve differently depending on the working directory an agent process happens to start in.

- Command: follow-up to the max_structures=2 Goldilocks finding -- forced `max_structures=3` and ran a 30-trial Optuna search (reusing `optimize.py`'s own risk-adjusted objective) over `lambda_labor`/`crisis_backlog_ratio`/`roi_margin`/`animal_reserve_multiple`/`pasture_target_ratio` to see if a comparable 3-animal config exists. agy's own N=15 verification reported a modest edge: chaitanyajamble 40.0%->46.7%, pilkwang -98,908.7->-97,995.7, daisy023 flat.
- **Independently re-verified at N=30 with fresh, non-overlapping seeds (98000-98029) before trusting the "edges out" framing** -- exactly the check that caught the same false pattern on `real_options_maturity` earlier today. Result: champion 8W-22L (26.7%, -528.0) vs 3-animal 9W-21L (30.0%, -438.4) -- a one-win difference out of 30, statistically a wash, not an improvement. daisy023 nearly identical (14,253.6 vs 14,245.1). pilkwang nearly identical (-92,830.2 vs -93,162.4).
