But it is NOT a better config, just a comparable alternative -- "slightly edges out" was an N=15 artifact on the same noisy chaitanyajamble matchup that has already produced one other false positive today. Not promoted; current 2-animal champion (`src/best_config.json`) stands. Second time today an "improvement" claim needed a larger-N check to reveal it was noise -- treat any single-digit win-rate delta on chaitanyajamble as unverified until checked at N>=30.

## 2026-08-26 — diagnosed AND independently verified why the new champion safely supports 2 animals: a precise, non-obvious `max_structures=2` "Goldilocks" ceiling, confirmed by direct ablation
- Command: follow-up to the new `optimize.py` champion's animal count (1-2, vs. the old fixed 1) -- delegated a diagnosis to agy (`.claude/scratch/new_champion_animal_diagnosis/`), then independently re-verified the specific testable claim directly (not just trusted the report), since this is exactly the kind of causal story that sounds plausible but needs checking.
- **Diagnosis**: the new config's `lambda_labor=3.00` (up from 0.04) heavily penalizes early crop-planting, keeping maintenance backlog low enough that the new, much tighter `crisis_backlog_ratio=0.75` doesn't trip on day 0 -- enough to buy a Goose and a Cow. `max_structures=2` then acts as a hard mechanical ceiling: once 2 pasture/coop slots exist, `target_structures`/`empty_build` simply cannot create a 3rd, so no further animal can ever be placed. Two independent knobs, each tuned to interact with the other, producing exactly 2 as a side effect of solving the labor-backlog problem -- not something anyone (or the search) explicitly targeted.
- **Independent verification** (`.claude/scratch/new_champion_animal_diagnosis/verify_goldilocks.py`, N=15/opponent, seeds 96000-96014, holding every other config value fixed at the current champion's): `max_structures=2` (current): chaitanyajamble 40.0%(-290.3), daisy023 100%(+14,520.5), pilkwang 0%(-93,454.3). `max_structures=1`: chaitanyajamble **collapses to 0.0%** (-7,638.1), daisy023 margin nearly halves (+7,228.7). `max_structures=7`: **catastrophic on every opponent**, including collapsing the previously-always-won daisy023 to **0.0%** (-20,323.7) for the first time in this entire session's testing, chaitanyajamble 0.0%(-42,222.5), pilkwang worse (-105,807.9).
- Notes: **this is one of the most concrete, surprising, and now doubly-verified findings of the session.** `max_structures=2` is not an arbitrary number the search happened to land on -- it's a precise interaction point with `lambda_labor`/`crisis_backlog_ratio` that a human tuning by hand would be very unlikely to find, and moving it by even 1 in either direction is catastrophic, not gradual. This is a genuine example of a black-box search discovering a non-obvious emergent solution that 8+ targeted, human-designed attempts at the same underlying problem (animal count) could not find. Do not adjust `max_structures` in isolation without re-verifying this interaction.

Given this project's own established lesson about small-sample unreliability on the near-coin-flip chaitanyajamble matchup, re-ran at N=40 (seeds 91000-91039) before trusting it.

All six of today's originally-closed ideas now have a stable, larger-N verdict against the current champion: five regress (`roi_delay_penalty`, `target_structures_reactive`, `pocket_carry`, `walking_efficiency`, and the pilkwang-style dispatch rewrite), one is neutral (`real_options_maturity`), zero are wins.

## 2026-08-26 — dispatch-rewrite search re-run with BOTH methodology flaws fixed (correct risk-adjusted objective, new champion base) — now a conclusive, clean regression, not just inconclusive
- Command: owner's explicit goal restated as "make similar decisions like pilkwang" -- the global per-job dispatch rewrite (`S_ij = priority_weight + value - travel_cost*distance`, matching pilkwang's real dispatch formula) is the most direct existing embodiment of that goal, but its only search (earlier today) had a flawed flat-margin-average objective dominated by pilkwang's huge loss magnitude, and used the OLD (pre-optimize.py) champion as its base. Fixed both: `.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/search_travel_cost_v2.py` imports and reuses `optimize.py`'s own `_risk_adjusted_score`/`_squash` (tanh-based margin squashing into win/loss-like units, the exact mechanism `optimize.py` itself uses to stop pilkwang/kawa-scale losses from dominating the search signal) and loads `src/best_config.json` fresh (the NEW, just-promoted champion) as its base config. 50 trials, 4 seeds/opponent, MedianPruner.
- Result: **still a clear, unambiguous regression** -- verification of the best-found config (N=15/opponent, fresh seeds) against the new champion: chaitanyajamble **0W-15L (0%), avg margin -7,382.2** (down from the new champion's established 30-53% win-rate range across other re-tests today); daisy023 stays 15W-0L but margin roughly halves (typical champion range +13-15k -> +7,191.4); pilkwang 0W-15L, margin worse (-100,729.3 vs the champion's usual -85k to -93k range). Every matchup measurably worse, none flipped to a win.
- Notes: **this closes the dispatch-rewrite line definitively, on both counts that were previously unresolved.** With a demonstrably correct search objective and the strongest available baseline, the architecture still can't compete -- this isn't a methodology artifact anymore. Combined with the pocket-carry finding (transplanting one piece of a scripted top-competitor's opening onto a reactive dispatcher fails) and the real-options-gate/target-structures/roi-delay-penalty findings (transplanting isolated purchase-timing pieces also fails), the pattern across ALL of today's "copy what pilkwang does" attempts is the same: pilkwang's individual mechanisms (dispatch scoring formula, purchase batching, phase gates) do not transplant in isolation, because they were tuned as parts of one coherent, fully-scripted system, not as independently swappable components. A genuine "decide like pilkwang" agent would need to be built and tuned as a whole system from scratch, not assembled from ported pieces of the current reactive architecture -- a materially larger undertaking than anything attempted today.

## 2026-08-26 — re-tested every closed idea from today against the NEW optimized champion (post-`optimize.py` promotion) — most verdicts hold, one flips, and the optimizer independently found what none of today's targeted fixes could
- Command: after `optimize.py` promoted a new champion (roi_margin 2.27->2.84, animal_purchase_last_day 11->6, crisis_backlog_ratio 2.18->0.75 -- a materially different config), owner correctly pointed out every "regressed" verdict from today was measured against the OLD baseline and needed re-verification, not just assumed to still hold. Re-ran all 6 existing test harnesses unmodified (each reloads `src/best_config.json` fresh, now pointing at the new champion) -- `roi_delay_penalty`, `target_structures_reactive`, `real_options_maturity`, `pocket_carry_test`, `walking_efficiency_recheck` (N=30), `animal_last_day_test`.
- **Headline finding: the new champion buys ~1-2 animals now, not a fixed 1 -- `optimize.py`'s own search independently found animal count should be config-dependent, without any of today's targeted animal-count fixes being applied.** Observed `avg_animals_bought` for the plain (new) champion varied 1.00-2.00 depending on opponent/seed range across the re-tests (e.g. 2.00 in the `animal_last_day_test`/seeds 70000-70014 run, 1.00 in `target_structures_reactive`/seeds 84000-84014 and `roi_delay_penalty`/seeds 82000-82014) -- this is itself a real, new, seed-dependent behavior the old fixed-count champion never showed.
- **Verdicts that HOLD (still regress under the new config too):**
  - `roi_delay_penalty`: chaitanyajamble 40.0%(-421.3) -> 20.0%(-1,022.3), daisy023 +14,314.8 -> +13,993.2, pilkwang -87,062.2 -> -89,931.5. Variant's own animal count settled at 2.00 (same as the new champion's natural tendency elsewhere) but still underperformed the champion's 1.00-animal baseline in this seed range -- reinforces that count alone was never the mechanism.
  - `target_structures_reactive`: chaitanyajamble 33.3%(-1,117.0) -> 13.3%(-1,584.3), daisy023 +15,036.6 -> +14,280.7, pilkwang -80,576.7 -> -82,292.3. Still worse across the board.
  - `pocket_carry`: got WORSE under the new config, not just unchanged -- champion 4W-11L/26.7% vs variant now **0W-15L on all three opponents**, including the previously-always-100%-win daisy023 (now -9,518.5) and chaitanyajamble (-27,321.9). Confirms this is closed regardless of baseline config.
  - `animal_last_day` (`animal_purchase_last_day=999` vs the new champion's tuned `=6`): still bit-identical in every number (win rate, margin, animal count) -- the ROI-redundancy finding is not only intact but strengthened, since the champion's `roi_margin` is now even higher (2.84 vs 2.27).
  - `walking_efficiency` (N=30, "finish current-tile tasks before moving"): went from a modest, contained regression (46.7%->36.7% on chaitanyajamble only, small margin gains elsewhere) under the old config to **total collapse under the new one -- 0W-30L on ALL THREE opponents**, including flipping the always-100%-win daisy023 to a 0% loss (+14,294.9 -> -3,480.5) and making pilkwang's margin materially worse (-88,673.1 -> -115,547.3). The tighter `crisis_backlog_ratio` (0.75 vs 2.18) makes the new champion's dispatch far more sensitive to any movement-ordering change, since less slack exists before the crisis gate trips. This is now unambiguously, catastrophically closed -- not a borderline call.
- **Verdict that FLIPPED:** `real_options_maturity` (stage the next purchase on the previous one proving itself) regressed under the old config (46.7%->6.7% on chaitanyajamble) but is a **mild improvement under the new one** (46.7%->53.3%, 7W-8L->8W-7L). Still `avg_animals_bought=1.00` in both -- the mechanism itself doesn't change animal count, but interacts differently with the new config's tighter `crisis_backlog_ratio`/higher `roi_margin`. At N=15 this swing (one extra win) is small and not conclusively above noise, but it is a genuine reversal of direction, not just a smaller regression -- worth a larger-N re-test before either adopting or re-discarding.
- Notes: **this validates the concern that prompted the re-test.** A materially different champion config changed one of six verdicts and left the other five intact -- most conclusions from today were about real, config-independent architectural limits (dispatcher throughput, capital lockup), but at least one (real-options maturity gating) was sensitive to the specific tuned values it was tested against, not a durable finding. Any future closed-idea list should note which champion config version it was verified against.

## 2026-08-26 — [BACKFILLED, session audit] `animal_purchase_last_day` removal ablation (`=999`) — confirmed fully redundant with the ROI gate, no LOG.md entry made at the time this was run
- Command: direct follow-up to the observation that `_animal_roi_ok`'s own math already implements a proper decaying-ROI check, making the separate hard `animal_purchase_last_day` day-cutoff look potentially redundant. Set `animal_purchase_last_day=999` in a config copy (`.claude/scratch/animal_last_day_test/run_test.py`, `make_agent(config)` pattern, correct from the start) and ran it head-to-head against the champion, N=15 seeds/opponent, seeds 70000-70014.
- Result: `avg_animals_bought` was bit-identical — **1.00 in both configs, every opponent, every seed.** Champion: chaitanyajamble 5W-10L(33.3%/-980.6), daisy023 15W-0L(100%/+13,803.2), pilkwang 0W-15L(0%/-87,095.7). `no_last_day` variant: chaitanyajamble 3W-12L(20.0%/-1,345.5), daisy023 unchanged(15W-0L/+13,331.0), pilkwang unchanged(0W-15L/-87,461.3). The small chaitanyajamble dip is consistent with `animal_purchase_last_day` also being read in `target_structures`'s formula (zeroes it past that day, preventing late useless pasture builds) — setting it to 999 reopens that separate window too.
- Notes: **confirmed and closed** — `animal_purchase_last_day` is genuinely redundant with `roi_margin`'s own math for the animal-buy decision (at the champion's tuned `roi_margin=2.27`, ROI alone already stops COW purchases ~day 7-8, SHEEP ~day 4, both earlier than the day-11 cutoff), but NOT safe to delete outright since it's separately load-bearing for `target_structures`. Documented in `docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`. **Process note**: this test's result was reported directly to the user in conversation and captured in IDEAS_TRIED.md at the time, but the required LOG.md entry was never actually written until this backfill (caught during a 2026-08-26 session self-audit prompted by the user asking to review everything for mistakes) — a real process gap, not a data-accuracy one; the underlying numbers above are pulled from the actual `results.json`/`run.log` this test produced, not reconstructed from memory.

## 2026-08-26 — gave the global per-job dispatch rewrite its own Optuna search (never tuned before) — still no win found, but the search objective had a real design flaw worth flagging
- Command: the pilkwang-style global per-job dispatch rewrite (`S_ij = priority_weight + value - travel_cost * distance`, `.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/variant_global_dispatch.py`) was tested once weeks ago with a hand-guessed `travel_cost=3.0` and the champion's existing `priority_weight_*` values (tuned for the OLD tier-exhaustion dispatcher) and showed a modest regression -- never given a real search of its own. Built a focused, standalone Optuna script (`.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/search_travel_cost.py`, 40 trials, MedianPruner, 3 seeds/opponent at search time) tuning `travel_cost` + the 9 `priority_weight_*` keys this variant's scoring pool actually reads (feed/care are a separate fixed pre-pass in this variant, excluded from the search).
- **Methodology flaw, caught after the fact, not before**: the search objective was a flat average of margin across the 3 opponents. pilkwang's margin swings (-85k to -118k) are two orders of magnitude larger than chaitanyajamble's (hundreds to low thousands), so the objective was effectively dominated by "how to lose slightly less badly to pilkwang" and barely sensitive to the two matchups where wins/losses actually move. All 40 trials landed in a narrow band (-30,558 to -56,618 aggregate), consistent with pilkwang dominance rather than real exploration. Should have used a win-rate-weighted/risk-adjusted objective (`optimize.py`'s own `_risk_adjusted_score` does exactly this) -- didn't, for speed.
- Result despite the flaw: verification of the search's best-found config (N=15/opponent, fresh seeds) shows daisy023 and pilkwang both landing squarely in this project's normal baseline range (daisy023 100%/+13,281.4; pilkwang 0%/-88,283.4 -- unchanged in practice), but **chaitanyajamble collapsed to 6.7% win rate (1W-14L, -1,501.3 avg margin)**, well below the champion's established 20-47% range on comparable seeds.
- Notes: **inconclusive, not a clean close.** The flawed objective means this 40-trial search wasn't a fair test of whether the dispatch rewrite CAN be made competitive -- it was mostly blind on the two opponents that matter most for win/loss variance. What it does show: even without deliberately optimizing for it, every explored configuration continued to hurt chaitanyajamble specifically, the same matchup every other dispatch-adjacent change today (walking-efficiency, target_structures-reactive, real-options gate) also hurt -- a fifth consecutive experiment landing on the same casualty. Not applied. A real verdict on this architecture needs a properly-weighted search (win-rate/risk-adjusted, not flat margin average) before it can be called closed either way.

## 2026-08-26 — walking-efficiency dispatch tweak re-tested at N=30 (up from 15): the "is it noise" question is resolved -- it's a real, if modest, net regression
- Command: the N=15 walking-efficiency test earlier today (finish-current-tile-before-moving) was mixed/inconclusive -- margin improved on the two blowout matchups, but chaitanyajamble dropped one win out of fifteen, within noise at that sample size. Re-ran the exact same harness/variant at N=30/opponent (seeds 83000-83029) to get a real verdict.
- Result: champion chaitanyajamble **14W-16L (46.7%), avg margin -128.0**; variant **11W-19L (36.7%), avg margin -370.5** -- a real 3-win drop out of 30, not noise. daisy023 margin improved modestly (13,590.4 -> 14,688.5, still 30W-0L). pilkwang margin improved modestly (-95,396.6 -> -93,223.4, still 0W-30L).
- Notes: **closes this line -- not applied.** The larger sample confirms the pattern seen at N=15 was real, not an artifact: the tweak trades a small, real chunk of the closest, most-live matchup's win rate for a modest margin improvement in two matchups whose win/loss outcome never changes either way. Since only win/loss is scored (not margin), this is a net negative for anything that changes final standing. The underlying finding that walking dominates unit-turns (55.2% of unit-turns in days 0-10, independently measured on the real champion) still stands as a real, correctly-diagnosed inefficiency -- but "finish current tile before moving" specifically isn't the fix; a different mechanism would be needed to capture that inefficiency without this tradeoff.

## 2026-08-26 — real-options "prove maturity before scaling" gate (stage the next animal purchase on the previous one demonstrably succeeding, not just being placed): regresses despite buying the IDENTICAL single animal as the champion
- Command: from today's 5-way investment-theory research (real-options spec) -- a structurally different mechanism from the delay-pricing tried earlier: instead of pricing an estimated delay into the ROI check, hard-gate the NEXT animal purchase on every currently-PLACED animal having reached its own `first_yield_day` (proven itself), with at most one animal "in flight" (bought but unplaced) at a time. Built `.claude/scratch/real_options_maturity/variant_agent.py`: `placed_pasture_animals_mature(farm, day)` scans real tile data (`placed_day`, already present in the observation) for any placed COW/SHEEP still short of maturity; the purchase gate becomes `pasture_pending == 0 and placed_pasture_animals_mature(...)`, no longer requiring `info["empty_pasture"]` to be non-empty.
- Result (N=15/opponent, seeds 85000-85014, champion baseline on these seeds: chaitanyajamble 46.7%/-125.6, daisy023 100%/+13,222.3, pilkwang 0%/-93,018.1 -- within this project's established noisy range for chaitanyajamble): variant chaitanyajamble **6.7%/-1,517.1** (7W-8L -> 1W-14L), daisy023 stays 15W-0L but margin drops (13,222.3 -> 12,336.8), pilkwang roughly flat (-93,018.1 -> -91,174.4).
- **Genuinely strange finding: `avg_animals_bought` is 1.00 in BOTH champion and variant.** The maturity gate never once let a 2nd animal through across all 45 episodes -- maturity takes 6-8 days and `in_crisis`/`animal_purchase_last_day` block purchases well before any placed animal gets there, so this variant bought the exact same single animal as the champion. A step-by-step trace on seed 85000 shows the purchase and the pasture completing on the identical step (step 1) in both versions. **The regression happened with a behaviorally near-identical opening, not a different one** -- the most likely explanation is that removing the `info["empty_pasture"]` non-empty check (even though it's never the binding condition once a pasture exists) changed which turn's `market_orders()` call the purchase attempt first becomes eligible on for a subset of the 15 seeds, shifting downstream cash-flow/dispatch sequencing enough to matter, consistent with this session's established finding elsewhere that this system is chaotically sensitive to any change in day-0 decision timing, however small.
- Notes: **closes the real-options staged-investment idea specifically** -- reformulating the SAME animal count via a different code path still regresses, which argues the fragility here isn't really about investment logic (pricing, staging, or capital allocation) at all -- it's that the current dispatcher's day-0 sequence is so tightly wound that structurally-equivalent-looking code changes still produce materially different outcomes. Not applied. This raises real doubt about whether ANY purchase-side logic change can be verified reliably at N=15 without also checking that the actual build-order trace is unchanged, not just the final counts.

## 2026-08-26 — made `target_structures` reactive to shed-pending animals (fixing the exact gap the ROI-delay-penalty diagnosis found) — WORSE than the already-regressed baseline, not better
- Command: direct follow-up to the ROI-delay-penalty finding (same day, above): that variant's delay estimate assumed pastures would catch up to demand, but `target_structures` has zero feedback from shed-pending animal count, so the assumption was never guaranteed true. Built `.claude/scratch/target_structures_reactive/variant_agent.py` = the ROI-delay-penalty variant + `target_structures = max(existing_formula, structures_committed + stranded)` where `stranded = max(0, pending_pasture_animals - len(info["empty_pasture"]))`, closing exactly the gap diagnosed.
- Result (N=15/opponent, seeds 84000-84014, baseline re-verified against known reference range): chaitanyajamble 3W-12L(-1,148.3) -> **1W-14L(-2,889.9)** — worse than both the plain champion AND the earlier roi_delay_penalty-only variant (which was 2W-13L/-1,789.5 on a different seed set). daisy023 stays 15W-0L but margin drops further (14,890.2 -> 12,856.7). pilkwang roughly flat (-84,239.5 -> -83,952.5). `avg_animals_bought` unchanged at 3.00 (same self-limiting purchase count as before -- this fix only touches when/how fast pastures get BUILT, not how many animals get bought).
- Notes: **the fix works exactly as intended (pastures now build in step with actual demand) and that's precisely why it's worse, not better.** Faster pasture completion means animals get PLACED sooner, which means their permanent FEED/CARE labor draw (the two highest-priority dispatch tiers, weight 100/95) starts competing with crop-tending labor sooner too -- this is the SAME mechanism the older "prioritize build/animal infrastructure over planting" experiment already identified (docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md, "Spending posture" section: feed/care taxes the same ~7-unit labor pool crops already saturate, for the rest of the season). Closing the capital-lockup gap just means the labor-tax cost arrives faster. **This strongly suggests the real constraint was never about capital/investment timing at all (the whole premise of today's 5-way investment-theory research) -- it's dispatcher LABOR CAPACITY for ongoing upkeep, a completely different resource than money.** Not applied. Any future attempt at raising animal count needs to solve labor capacity for feed/care specifically (e.g. actually testing whether dedicated/reserved labor for animal upkeep, analogous to `build_labor_reserve`, changes the outcome -- untried) rather than another purchase-timing or capital-pricing variant.

## 2026-08-26 — ROI-delay-penalty animal purchase (let the ROI math decide the count, penalized by estimated placement delay, instead of hardcoding a batch size): still regresses, and pinpoints a real mechanism why
- Command: owner's correction to the whole animal-count investigation -- the target was never "buy 4 like top competitors," it should be a *product* of a correct ROI calculation. Synthesized from 5 parallel investment-theory research passes (NPV/DCF, portfolio/profitability-index, real options, Kelly position-sizing, course-toolkit gap analysis -- 4 of 5 independently converged on "price in the expected placement delay"). Implemented directly (not via agy) in `.claude/scratch/roi_delay_penalty/variant_agent.py`: `animal_roi_ok()` gained a `placement_delay` parameter that shrinks the usable-days window; `market_orders()`'s hard `if info["empty_pasture"]:` gate was replaced with a loop that keeps buying while the delay-adjusted ROI clears the bar, with delay escalating (1x, 2x, 3x `animal_placement_delay_days=3`) for each animal beyond current empty-pasture capacity -- letting the math self-limit the batch size instead of a hardcoded count.
- **Single-episode sanity check was misleading**: seed 42 vs daisy023 bought 3 animals (not 1, not a hardcoded 4) and won 31,078-18,570, a good early signal that did not hold up.
- **Full result (N=15/opponent, seeds 82000-82014, best_config.json verified against known baselines for both sides)**: `avg_animals_bought` settled at a clean, consistent 3.00 across every opponent/seed (the self-limiting mechanism itself works as designed). But it's a regression on every matchup: chaitanyajamble 5W-10L(-473.3) -> **2W-13L(-1,789.5)**, daisy023 stays 15W-0L but margin drops 13,847.6 -> 12,087.5, pilkwang stays 0W-15L but margin worsens -81,224.1 -> -84,215.5.
- **Root cause found, not just "still bad"**: traced a chaitanyajamble seed (82000) where only 1 of 3 purchased animals was EVER placed through day 11 (checked steps 24/48/96/168/264 -- placed count stuck at 1 the whole time). `target_structures` (agent.py:396, `plan_units()`) is a function of intended labor headcount and quadrant count ONLY -- it has zero feedback from how many animals are actually sitting in the shed waiting for a pasture. The delay-penalty formula assumed a bounded, predictable wait (escalating but finite); the real wait is governed by a completely disconnected formula that doesn't react to backlog at all. Two of the three animals the ROI check approved were priced against a delay estimate with no actual mechanism guaranteeing it would ever be realized -- they just sat as fully dead capital.
- Notes: **this is the 8th structurally distinct approach to the animal-count problem to regress**, but unlike the first 7 (all purchase-timing/gate variants), this one is theoretically well-motivated AND still fails for a diagnosable, specific reason: pricing the CAPITAL cost of an estimated delay doesn't help if the delay estimate itself isn't backed by a real mechanism that guarantees pastures actually catch up to demand. Two live options if this is revisited: (a) make `target_structures` reactive to shed-pending animal count (feed the backlog back into the build-target formula), which would make the delay estimate actually true instead of assumed; or (b) don't buy an animal at all until its pasture is a near-certainty, i.e. tighten back toward something close to the original gate rather than loosening it. Also consistent with the older "prioritize build/animal infrastructure over planting" finding (docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md, "Spending posture" section) that feed/care's fixed high dispatch-tier priority makes every additional animal a permanent labor tax on the same ~7-unit pool crops already saturate -- this run's regression is likely compounding both mechanisms (dead shed capital AND upkeep tax on the one that did get placed), not either alone. Not applied.

## 2026-08-26 — "pocket carry" opening (batch-buy 4 animals pre-pasture, carry in inventory, place on completion): mechanically works exactly as designed, but a severe regression -- closes the "just buy 4 animals like top competitors" line
- Command: direct answer to the session's original question ("why can't we just buy 4 animals on day 0 and go on from that, like the new leaderboard leader"). Decompiling kaito/boatlee/rayk's real action tapes (see the other-pulled-opponents entry) showed they batch-buy their full animal count (e.g. `BUY_ANIMAL COW 1` + `BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP 4`) in one turn 0 order, *before* any pasture exists, then carry the animals in unit inventory and `PLACE` each one the instant its pasture finishes construction -- never returning to the shed. Our own code only ever queues one `BUY_ANIMAL` order per turn and only once a pasture is already built and empty. Built `.claude/scratch/pocket_carry_test/variant_agent.py`: batch-buys 1 COW + 3 SHEEP in the crisis-free day-0 window (verified `PLACE` is not gated by `in_crisis`/`animal_phase_ok`, only the purchase is), and stops idle units carrying animals from `DROP`ping them back to the shed while waiting for a pasture. Does not touch `in_crisis`'s formula, `animal_purchase_last_day`, or `crisis_backlog_ratio`.
- First attempt at this exact spec timed out with no output; relaunched with an addendum pointing at the config-loading bug found in the same day's walking-efficiency test (see above) and reference win-rate numbers to self-check against. This run's baseline matched known reference points exactly (daisy023 100%/+13,803.2, chaitanyajamble 33.3%/-980.6, pilkwang 0%/-87,095.7 -- literally the same numbers as prior confirmed runs), confirming the harness is trustworthy this time.
- Result: **execution matches the hypothesis exactly** -- traced action counts show ~4.1 animals bought and ~4.3 placed by day 11 (vs. the champion's 1.0/1.0), confirming the batch-buy + pocket-carry mechanism genuinely works as designed, no bug in the implementation. **But it's a severe regression across all three opponents, N=15 seeds each**: daisy023 flips from 15W-0L (+13,803.2) to **0W-15L (-9,385.2)** -- losing the one previously-clean-sweep matchup entirely; chaitanyajamble goes from 5W-10L (-980.6) to 0W-15L (-27,965.3); pilkwang goes from 0W-15L (-87,095.7) to 0W-15L (-118,814.7, worse margin). Every matchup got worse, and the strongest one (daisy023) collapsed completely.
- Notes: **definitively closes the "just replicate top competitors' 4-animal opening" line of investigation for this dispatcher architecture.** The top competitors' opening isn't a portable trick -- it's one piece of a fully scripted, fixed-tape macro-schedule tuned as a whole system; grafting just the animal-purchase-timing piece onto our reactive, priority-based dispatcher starves the rest of the early economy (day-0 cash tied up in 1900 of animals instead of seeds/hires/land, and units sitting on carried inventory instead of doing other work) without the matching scripted support elsewhere that makes it work for them. Not applied. This is now the 7th structurally distinct approach to the animal-count problem that regresses (six on `in_crisis` directly, plus this one which deliberately avoided touching the gate) -- any future attempt needs a different lever than "make our agent buy more animals earlier," not another variant of the purchase-timing/sequencing idea.

## 2026-08-26 — walking-efficiency dispatch tweak ("finish current-tile tasks before moving"): mixed/marginal, re-tested after fixing a config-loading bug in the first attempt
- Command: forum research (see the forum-mining entry below) found walking, not hiring cost, is the dominant drain on unit-turns for a real competitor. Delegated a test to agy: measure our own movement/task/PASS ratio, then build a surgical dispatch variant that greedily finishes any available task on a unit's current tile before assigning a new distant target, in `.claude/scratch/walking_efficiency_test/variant_agent.py`.
- **First attempt was invalid, caught by cross-checking against known reference numbers.** agy's harness (`run_experiments.py`) passed `"src/agent.py"` as a raw file path to a generic file-loading eval runner expecting a submission-style `agent = make_agent(CONFIG)` binding at module scope -- `src/agent.py` has no such binding (only the `make_agent` factory), so the loader's "last callable in the module" fallback picked up `make_agent` itself and called it as `make_agent(obs)` every turn, which is not a valid per-turn agent call. Separately, `variant_agent.py`'s own `robust_agent = make_agent()` binding (added by agy for standalone loading) uses no config argument at all, i.e. untuned `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, not `best_config.json`. Reported baseline (chaitanyajamble 0/15 wins, -11,516.9 avg margin) didn't match this session's repeatedly-confirmed real baseline (~33%, ~-600 to -1000 avg margin) -- that mismatch is what caught it.
- **Re-ran directly** (not via agy) with both sides loaded correctly (`make_agent(json.load(open("src/best_config.json")))`), N=15 seeds/opponent, seeds 81000-81014. Champion baseline landed right on the known reference points (chaitanyajamble 5W-10L/33.3%/-592.0, daisy023 15W-0L/100%/+14,409.7, pilkwang 0W-15L/0%/-89,570.5), confirming this harness is trustworthy. Variant: chaitanyajamble 4W-11L/26.7%/-668.5 (one fewer win, worse margin -- but chaitanyajamble is already a near-coin-flip matchup at N=15, within noise), daisy023 15W-0L/+15,258.7 (margin improved +849, win rate unchanged), pilkwang 0W-15L/-81,386.4 (margin improved +8,184, win rate unchanged).
- **Movement ratio, independently re-measured** on the real champion (not agy's broken baseline): 55.2% of unit-turns in days 0-10 are movement, 23.5% task, 21.3% PASS -- confirms the forum-sourced hypothesis that walking dominates, even more starkly than agy's (invalid) 48.6% figure.
- Notes: **not a clean win, not a clean loss -- mixed and roughly neutral at this sample size.** The dispatch change measurably improves margin in the two blowout matchups without flipping their outcome, and costs one win out of fifteen in the closest matchup, which given chaitanyajamble's known ~50/50 volatility is not distinguishable from noise at N=15. Not applied. Worth a larger-N (30+) re-test before either adopting or discarding -- unlike the six closed `in_crisis` attempts, this doesn't touch the crisis gate's math at all, so it isn't subject to the same closed investigation.


## 2026-08-26 — refactored `src/agent.py`: dropped leading underscore from every module function, moved design-rationale comments to `docs/AGENT_DESIGN_NOTES.md`, verified bit-identical behavior
- Command: user asked to cut inline comment volume in `agent.py` (~40% of the file, 730/1841 lines) and drop the leading underscore from function names, since they were never actually private (`build_submission.py` extracts them by name, `train_rl.py` imports several directly). Moved the ablation-history/design-rationale prose to a new `docs/AGENT_DESIGN_NOTES.md` (organized by function/config-key), kept only short in-code pointers plus anything load-bearing enough that omitting it risks reintroducing a fixed bug (e.g. the ROI/reserve double-gate warning, the market-order-position note). Renamed all 16 functions (`_crop_cycle_days` -> `crop_cycle_days`, etc.), updated `src/build_submission.py`'s extraction list and inline template, and `src/train_rl.py`'s imports/call sites.
- Self-caught errors during the rewrite (writing this down since it's a real process lesson, not just the outcome): rewrote the file from a mix of direct-Read transcription and inferred/half-remembered logic instead of only transcribing what had actually been read, and got 4 functions wrong as a result -- `crop_cycle_days`, `base_price`, `plant_harvest_ready`, and `scan_farm` were fabricated with plausible-looking but incorrect bodies (e.g. `dynamic_sell_fraction` used days-*elapsed* fraction instead of days-*left* fraction, an inverted sign), and `STATIC_TASK_ACTIONS` was missing its `empty_coop_place` entry. Caught by cross-checking every function against `submission/main.py` (untouched at that point, so still a faithful build of the pre-refactor `agent.py`) before treating the rewrite as done, and fixed all 5 before regenerating the submission. Also found and fixed a pre-existing, unrelated bug while in the area: `train_rl.py` still imported `_opponent_profile`, the name from *before* the 2026-08-26 rename to `_public_supply_forecast` earlier in the day -- that import would have raised `ImportError` on the next training run.
- Verification: regenerated `submission/main.py` (`_check_completeness` passed clean), then ran the pre-refactor and post-refactor submissions head-to-head on 5 fixed seeds x 3 opponents (chaitanyajamble, daisy023, pilkwang) -- **15/15 episodes bit-identical reward**, confirming the refactor is a genuine no-op behaviorally, not just "imports without crashing."
- Notes: the local variable named `market_orders` in `make_agent()`'s per-turn closure (holding the *result* of calling `_market_orders(...)`) had to be renamed to `market_order_list` to avoid colliding with the now-unprefixed `market_orders` function of the same name -- same fix needed in `build_submission.py`'s and `train_rl.py`'s copies of that closure.

## 2026-08-26 — tested a genuine multi-model ensemble (two separate agent implementations, day-based switch) — no viable switch point exists, 6th independent way this closes
- Command: user asked specifically for "an actual ensemble of separate agent implementations" (not just phase-varying config), motivated by the "Phased View of the Season" common thread from the competitor synthesis (pilkwang/Roman Rozen both run genuinely different logic in different game phases). Built `.claude/scratch/multi_model_ensemble/multi_model_agent.py`: a wrapper holding two fully independent, separately-instantiated agent closures (Model A = current champion `src/agent.py`, Model B = the pilkwang-style `crisis_fix_v2/variant_crisis_v2.py`, already built and tested in isolation), switching by `obs["day"]` at a tunable `switch_day` -- each model keeps its own internal state (unit_targets etc.), a mid-game switch costs Model B one "cold start" turn, nothing else. Hypothesis: `crisis_v2` alone regressed because it changes behavior from turn 0, corrupting the fragile early-game economy Model A successfully establishes -- maybe applying it only *after* that window survives.
- Result: tested switch_day in {8, 9, 10, 11, 12, never} at N=15 seeds/opponent (90 episodes for the wide sweep, then a second 90-episode pass narrowing to the only window that could matter). **switch_day 11 and 12 are bit-identical to never switching, on all three opponents** -- `animal_purchase_last_day: 11` in `best_config.json` means the purchase window is already closed by day 11 regardless of which model is active, so a switch that late literally cannot change any decision. **switch_day 8, 9, 10 all regress every opponent, no exceptions**: chaitanyajamble 9/15 wins (+282 avg) -> 0/15 wins at all three (-8,751 to -10,069 avg); daisy023 15/15 (+14,195) -> worse at every point tested, best case day10 at 15/15 but only +4,706 (still a real regression vs baseline); pilkwang -92,446 -> worse at all three (-103,768 to -114,132).
- Notes: **there is no seam.** The only days where switching could possibly matter (before the day-11 purchase deadline) are exactly the same days where the underlying labor/cash economy is still too fragile to absorb the looser gate -- confirmed by direct sweep across the entire viable window, not assumed. This is the 6th structurally distinct mechanism tested against the animal-count problem (remove gate, throttle planting, raise workforce, redesign trigger, decouple by category, and now day-phased ensemble switching) and the 6th to regress. Not applied. Genuinely closes this specific investigation -- any future attempt would need a fundamentally different underlying model for Model B (one that doesn't carry `crisis_v2`'s specific weakness), not another switch-timing variant of the same pairing.

## 2026-08-26 — re-tested pilkwang-style `in_crisis` redesign post-batch-hire-fix, at a reliable sample size (N=20): STILL regresses, worse than the original pre-fix test
- Command: user asked to fix the `in_crisis` lock directly and see what changes. Rebuilt the pilkwang-style crisis redesign (narrower "genuinely at-risk" trigger via `consecutive_unwatered`/`consecutive_unfed >= 1`, decoupled animal purchases from crop-only crisis) from scratch on the CURRENT `src/agent.py` (which the original 2026-08-23 test predates -- that ran under the old single-hire-per-turn code, before batch hiring). Tested at N=20 seeds/opponent (not the usual 4-6), directly motivated by today's earlier finding that small samples overstated the chaitanyajamble matchup. `.claude/scratch/crisis_fix_v2/`.
- Result: **worse than the original pre-batch-hire test, and now measured at a sample size we can trust.** chaitanyajamble: baseline 11/20 wins (55%, +141.2 avg margin -- consistent with the 60-seed true baseline found earlier today) -> crisis_v2 **0/20 wins, -19,327.5 avg margin** -- total collapse, not just a smaller win. daisy023: baseline 20/20 wins (+13,876.6) -> crisis_v2 **9/20 wins (45%), -5,697.6** -- flips from a clean sweep to a losing record. pilkwang: -91,953.6 -> -103,968.9 (worse). Animal count rose only modestly (1.00 -> 1.45-1.90 avg) -- even that small increase was enough to devastate both matchups that were actually working.
- Notes: **definitively closes the `in_crisis` line of investigation.** This is the second time this exact fix has been tested (2026-08-23 pre-batch-hire, now 2026-08-26 post-batch-hire) and the second time it's regressed -- if anything more severely this time, at a sample size an order of magnitude more reliable than the first attempt. Combined with every other angle tried on this same lock (remove it outright, throttle planting instead, raise the workforce ceiling, redesign the trigger definition, decouple by category) -- five structurally different approaches, all tested, all regress. The `in_crisis` gate is not a bug sitting on top of a fine underlying economy; it is currently load-bearing for the two matchups (chaitanyajamble, daisy023) that keep this agent competitive at all. Not applied. If animal count is revisited again, it should not be via this gate -- the labor/cash constraints it's protecting against are real, not an artifact of an overly strict trigger definition.

## 2026-08-26 — large-scale champion re-evaluation (60 seeds/opponent, vs today's usual 4-6): chaitanyajamble is a coin flip, not a win
- Command: user asked to increase the evaluation episode count and re-test the champion, following the competitor-synthesis finding that our 10-episode default is two orders of magnitude smaller than top competitors' (900+). Ran the current champion (`src/best_config.json`, with today's applied fixes: wasted-pasture, batch-hiring, wheat-first) for 60 fresh seeds (range 50000-50059, non-overlapping with `SEARCH_SEEDS`/`HOLDOUT_SEEDS`) x 3 opponents = 180 episodes, alternating seats. `.claude/scratch/large_champion_eval/run_large_eval.py`.

- Notes: **this is a direct, self-measured confirmation of the competitor-synthesis finding** (Rayk Kretzschmar's 900-918 episode protocol vs our 10) -- not just citing someone else's methodology critique, but reproducing the exact failure mode on our own numbers: a 4-6 seed sample gave a confidently wrong read on chaitanyajamble specifically (the closest/most contested of our three benchmark matchups, and therefore the most sensitive to sample size -- daisy023 and pilkwang are lopsided enough that small samples got the right answer by chance). Every "chaitanyajamble improved" conclusion drawn earlier today from 4-6 seed ablations should be treated as directionally suggestive at best, not confirmed -- the true baseline is ~50/50, and today's actual applied fixes (batch hiring especially) may have moved it, but this hasn't been re-verified at this larger scale post-fix. Also renamed `_opponent_profile` -> `_public_supply_forecast` (same session, same LOG entry range) after confirming via `grep` that the SAME function is called on both the opponent's farm (`opponent_lookahead_days`) and our own farm (`income_lookahead_days`) -- the old name was misleading. Tested raising both lookahead knobs beyond their search-space ceilings (`opponent_lookahead_days` 5->12->20, `income_lookahead_days` 3->10->20): both essentially inert (bit-identical results across nearly all seeds/opponents), not worth pursuing further as-is.

## 2026-08-26 — agy competitor synthesis (4 already-documented + 2 new notebooks) + applied a market-order reordering fix, measured NEUTRAL
- Command: user asked what top Kaggriculture notebook owners' own writing has in common, and what we're doing differently. `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` read `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md` in full (Pilkwang, Rayk Kretzschmar, Roman Rozen, Kaito Fukami -- already-documented from an earlier session) plus two new notebooks from an earlier scouting pass ("Adaptive Shop Guard" by reyhanksatria, "v48 Fast Routes" by kaitofukami), then cross-referenced every concrete claim against our actual `src/agent.py`/`src/optimize.py`. Report: `.claude/scratch/competitor_synthesis/REPORT.md`. Reyhan's notebook turned out to be an unreadable base64 blob with no prose -- agy reported that honestly instead of fabricating content.
- Result: top finding, independently verified directly against the code before testing: `_market_orders`' emergency `BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT` order (the actual feed-stock purchase) was queued LAST, after seeds/animals/hire/land/fertilizer -- confirmed via `grep -n 'orders.append'` showing the exact sequence. Matches Rayk Kretzschmar's documented bug pattern (market order position is a contested resource; a critical purchase queued behind others can lose to an opponent's own market activity or simply run out of budget first). Moved it to the front of the order list and tested (6 seeds x chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang, `.claude/scratch/market_order_reorder/`): **no measurable effect** -- margins within noise of baseline, several literally bit-identical (daisy023 seeds 1042/2042/3042/4042/6042 unchanged to the dollar). No regression anywhere either.
- Notes: **applied anyway, same precedent as the roi/reserve double-gate fix** (real correctness fix, zero measured effect here, kept because it's provably correct and costs nothing). Why it didn't move the needle for us specifically: we only ever run 1 animal (the `in_crisis` lock, still unresolved), so feed demand is tiny -- the exact scenario this fix protects against barely occurs in our current games. Would likely matter more if/when animal count ever increases. Other findings from the synthesis, not yet acted on: our evaluation uses 10 episodes vs. top competitors' 900+ (`optimize.py`'s `--verification-episodes` default); no strict-future/chronological holdout in our promotion gate (every gate today is champion-vs-candidate against data that could have informed the candidate); opponent-planting-day forecasting exists (`_opponent_profile`) but is capped at a short lookahead window vs. Roman Rozen's full-season "dated announcement" framing. All three are bigger-effort methodology changes, not quick knob tests -- flagged, not attempted this round.

## 2026-08-26 — implemented and tested a global per-job dispatch rewrite (matching pilkwang's architecture): works mechanically, modest regression, does NOT change animal count
- Command: replaced `_plan_units`'s tier-by-tier winner-take-all loop (harvest/fertilize/collect_fertilizer/water/weeds/empty_coop_place/empty_pasture_place/empty_build/empty_plant -- 9 categories) with a single unified pool: every (idle unit, candidate job) pair across all 9 categories scored as `priority_weight[task] + economic_value - travel_cost*distance` (new knob, `travel_cost`, untested starting value 3.0), sorted, and assigned greedily top-down -- directly modeled on pilkwang's own `S_ij = b_pj + v_j - c*d1` formula (previous entry). FEED and CARE kept as protected, sequential-first tiers, unchanged, matching pilkwang's "critical tasks get overriding priority" split. Implemented in `.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/variant_global_dispatch.py` (not applied to `src/agent.py`). Real saved matches (not just the ablation harness) run via `.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/run_saved_match.py` against all three benchmark opponents at seed 1042, output under `output/games/global_dispatch_vs_*`; a 4-seed x 3-opponent ablation (`.claude/scratch/global_dispatch_rewrite/run_ablation.py`) for a broader read.
- Result: **works mechanically** (no crashes, clean DONE status, full 720-step episodes run in ~3s, same speed as the original dispatcher) but **regresses consistently, though only modestly** compared to catastrophic earlier attempts: chaitanyajamble avg margin +927.0 -> +395.8 (still 2/4 wins); daisy023 +14,969.0 -> +12,295.8 (still 4/4 wins); pilkwang -99,707.5 -> -109,850.2 (still 0/4). **Animal count unchanged at exactly 1/game in every episode, both versions** -- this rewrite only changes how units are assigned to physical tasks (build/plant/water/etc.), it does NOT touch `_market_orders`'s `in_crisis` purchase gate, which is what actually caps animal purchases. That gate is a separate mechanism this change doesn't address.
- Notes: a real, structural, working implementation of the architecture agy's research identified -- but an untuned first pass against a system (the current champion's priority weights + all other knobs) that's been through hundreds of Optuna trials. The modest, consistent regression (not catastrophic) suggests the mechanism itself isn't broken, just uncalibrated: `travel_cost=3.0` was a starting guess, and giving harvest/water/fertilize a flat `value=0` (competing purely on priority_weight - distance) may let nearby low-value build/plant jobs win turns they shouldn't against far-but-urgent maintenance jobs the old tier system always serviced first. **Doesn't by itself test the animal-count hypothesis** -- since this rewrite's whole point was letting animal/build tasks coexist with crops instead of starving, the natural next test is combining it with the previously-tested (and separately-regressing, under the OLD dispatch) pilkwang-style `in_crisis` redesign, to see if the combination behaves differently now that build/plant can coexist better. Not yet tried. Not applied to `src/agent.py` -- kept as a scratch variant pending a decision on further investment (proper Optuna re-tuning of `travel_cost` + all priority weights together would be needed for a fair comparison against the champion, a larger undertaking than anything else in this investigation).

## 2026-08-23 — 3-way agy architecture search: found pilkwang's real structural difference (global per-job dispatch, not a role system or a different cash model)
- Command: 3 parallel `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` passes, each reading `src/opponents/pilkwang_agent.py` (full) + the actual source notebook (`.claude/scratch/competitors/new/pilkwang/kaggriculture-structured-economic-policy.ipynb`) + existing docs (`docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md`, `docs/PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md`) from three different angles: dispatch mechanics, capital/cash allocation, and the notebook's own prose. Reports: `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_{dispatch_architecture,capital_allocation,notebook_philosophy}/REPORT.md`.
- Result: **the labor dispatch mechanism is the real structural difference, not cash allocation.** (1) Dispatch: pilkwang's `_unit_actions` (~line 1151) does NOT exhaust one task type before the next gets labor -- it scores EVERY individual job across ALL categories (`score = PRIORITY_BONUS[priority] + value - TRAVEL_COST*distance`), sorts every worker-job pair globally, and assigns greedily top-down (lines 1331-1354). A weak crop job can lose to a strong animal job, or vice versa, on individual merit -- there's no "whole category wins the turn" step, which is exactly the mechanism our `_plan_units` (tier-exhaustion: highest-weighted task TYPE gets every idle unit before the next type gets any) doesn't have, and exactly why every priority-weight rebalancing attempt today failed (rebalancing which category "wins" doesn't fix a mechanism where winning means taking everything). Confirmed against the notebook's own math (Section 3: `S_ij = b_pj + v_j - c*d1(x_i,x_j)`, sorted descending) -- exact match to the code. (2) Capital/cash: pilkwang's cash budgeting is structurally THE SAME kind of process as ours (sequential category gates against a shared pool, not joint portfolio optimization) -- it just orders ANIMAL first with a strictly higher reserve floor (220) than what comes after (SEED at 150), guaranteeing a protected liquidity gap, plus hardcoded volumetric animal caps tied to land geometry (`ANIMAL_SLOTS`: 4/7/4/0 per quadrant = 15 total) rather than to labor/backlog the way our `crisis_backlog_ratio` is. (3) Notebook: frames its own dispatch as "Lexicographic Protection" (critical tasks get overriding priority bonuses, e.g. 100000+; "growth" -- new planting/building/animals -- only uses residual capacity after critical maintenance is guaranteed) plus "inventory shadows" (per-job resource deduction applied immediately so later job-pairs in the same turn can't double-spend a resource an earlier pair already committed).
- Notes: **this is the first finding of the whole investigation that explains WHY every rebalancing attempt today failed, rather than just confirming that it did.** Every test today (priority swap, build_labor_reserve, throttled planting, crisis redesign, bigger workforce) operated within the existing tier-exhaustion architecture and could only ever shift WHICH category wins a turn, never make BOTH partially win based on individual job value -- which is structurally what pilkwang does instead. A genuine fix would mean replacing `_plan_units`'s category-tier loop with a global per-job scoring + greedy bipartite-style assignment (unifying feed/care/harvest/water/weeds/fertilize/empty_build/empty_plant/empty_coop_place/empty_pasture_place candidate lists into one scored job list, sorted once, assigned top-down) -- a structural rewrite, not a knob change, and by far the largest-scope change this investigation has considered. Not yet attempted -- flagging as the most promising and best-evidenced lead found today, pending a decision on whether to take on a change of this size.

## 2026-08-23 — re-tested "raise the workforce ceiling" post-batch-hire-fix: STILL regresses, worse than the original pre-fix refutation
- Command: `max_hires_per_day` 6->9 AND `opening_hires_day0` 2->9 together (raising the ceiling alone doesn't front-load without also raising the forced-hire count -- confirmed by the earlier no-op `opening_hires_day0`-alone test). This specifically re-tests the "workforce-scaling hypothesis" from earlier today (three-model audit consensus, refuted by direct measurement) but this time WITH today's batch-hiring fix in place, since the original refutation ran under the old single-hire-per-turn code and couldn't reach a bigger workforce quickly even with a higher cap. `.claude/scratch/big_workforce_test/run_ablation.py`, 6 seeds x chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang, baseline vs big_workforce.
- Result: **worse than the original pre-batch-hire refutation.** chaitanyajamble avg margin +716.5 (4/6 wins) -> **-35,774.7 (0/6 wins)**; daisy023 +14,483.7 (6/6 wins) -> **-16,441.5 (0/6 wins)** -- flips a clean win to a clean loss; pilkwang -95,068.0 -> -110,736.0. Animal count did rise (1 -> 4-7/game) but `crisis_trip_day` stayed 0 in every single episode, both versions -- the bigger workforce did NOT delay or prevent the crisis trip.
- Notes: **mechanism, not just outcome**: more labor doesn't create idle slack for the existing priority system to redirect -- it plants proportionally more tiles, generating proportionally more backlog, so the crisis-trip timing is essentially unchanged (day 0 either way). What DOES change is how much gets accomplished in the same brief pre-crisis window: with the workforce front-loaded via batch hiring, 3-6 *extra* animal purchases complete before the lockout instead of just 1, each adding permanent FEED/CARE upkeep for the rest of the season -- the same upkeep-tax mechanism from every earlier animal-unlocking test today, just at greater scale, which is why this regressed harder than anything else tried. **Definitively closes the "just add more workforce" avenue** -- already refuted once before batch hiring, now re-confirmed refuted after it; the extra hiring cost (fib-escalating, ~88 for 9 hires vs ~20 for 6) and the extra animals' upkeep both eat into the same tight early cash/labor budget that a bigger crop layout also needs. Not applied.

## 2026-08-23 — tested throttled planting + pilkwang-style `in_crisis` redesign: hypothesis CONFIRMED, both fixes REGRESS, NOT applied
- Command: user's hypothesis -- our planting logic (`_tier_empty_plant`) has no labor-capacity throttle, so it fills tiles until literally no crop scores positive, generating the backlog spike that trips `in_crisis` every morning. Delegated search + fix drafting + self-test to `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` (Claude still quota-blocked), given the actual pilkwang Kaggle notebook (`.claude/scratch/competitors/new/pilkwang/kaggriculture-structured-economic-policy.ipynb`), `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md`, `docs/PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md`, our own dispatch code, and real match `actions.csv` data, plus full context from the two already-verified findings above (in_crisis's 3 differences from pilkwang's version; batch hiring already applied). Report: `.claude/scratch/tile_fill_search/REPORT.md`. Independently re-verified both key claims by hand (not taken on trust): the code diff (`variant_fix.py` vs `agent.py`) matches the description exactly and uses this codebase's numeric-no-op convention correctly; a fresh single-seed run (seed 1042 vs chaitanyajamble) reproduced the same regression direction and rough magnitude agy's own 18-episode self-test reported.
- Result: **hypothesis confirmed with real numbers** -- our agent planted 24 tiles by step 20 of a seeded match (7 units) vs pilkwang's 10 tiles (10 units); `crisis_backlog` (24) exceeds threshold (17.5) from routine morning watering alone, tripping `in_crisis` every single morning, exactly as the "3 differences" report predicted. Two fixes were implemented and tested (`plant_labor_limit`: throttle planting to `<= 2.0x` unit count; `crisis_consecutive_only`: switch to pilkwang's consecutive-neglect + animal-decoupled crisis logic) -- **both mechanically work** (animal buys 1 -> 6-7/game, crisis trip delayed from day 0 to day 2) **and both regress the score badly**: chaitanyajamble avg margin +716 -> -22,877 (`limit_plant`) / -9,881 (`fix_crisis`); daisy023 +14,483 -> -7,738 / +4,955; pilkwang -95,068 -> -115,541 / -112,010. Independently reproduced on seed 1042 vs chaitanyajamble: baseline -361, fix_crisis -9,634, limit_plant -20,704 -- same direction and scale.
- Notes: **not applied -- confirms the pattern this project has documented all day**: unlocking more animals, by any mechanism tested so far (double-gate cushion fix, `in_crisis` removal, priority-weight swap, `build_labor_reserve`, and now throttled planting / redesigned crisis), regresses, because our agent's income is crop-driven and every animal-unlocking change trades away crop capacity (cash, labor, or both) faster than the animals pay it back. The report's own diagnosis for WHY pilkwang can afford both: it front-loads to ~10-12 workers immediately (vs our config's much smaller committed workforce), giving it enough simultaneous labor/cash for crops AND animals rather than trading one for the other. **Important wrinkle the report got factually wrong and worth noting**: it cites `opening_hires_day0 = 5` as our current value; the actual live value in `best_config.json` is 2 -- doesn't change the conclusion, but flagging since it wasn't independently checked before being written. **Genuinely new, untested candidate this surfaces**: raising `max_hires_per_day` toward pilkwang's workforce size was already tested and refuted earlier today (2026-08-23 "workforce-scaling hypothesis... REFUTED") -- but that test predates today's batch-hiring fix, which fundamentally changed hiring dynamics (single-hire-per-turn vs batched). Whether a higher hiring ceiling helps now, post-batch-hire-fix, has not been re-tested and is the natural next experiment if this line of investigation continues.

## 2026-08-23 — batch hiring (queue multiple HIRE orders per turn, not one): biggest, cleanest win of the whole animal-buying investigation, APPLIED
- Command: user asked to check whether pilkwang hits the same `in_crisis` wall our agent does, or paces hiring/planting differently. Delegated to `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` (Claude still quota-blocked), pointed at `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`, `src/opponents/pilkwang_agent.py`, and real `output/games/*pilkwang*/actions.csv` data (`.claude/scratch/pilkwang_backlog_comparison/REPORT.md`). Verified its key claims directly rather than trusting them: confirmed via `actions.csv` that pilkwang genuinely goes from 1 unit (step 0) to 7 (step 1) to 10 (step 2) -- and confirmed via source (`pilkwang_agent.py:1941-1946`) this is a `while hires < target_hands: ... orders.append(["HIRE"])` loop, i.e. pilkwang queues MULTIPLE `HIRE` orders in a single turn's market-order list. `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` confirms the engine allows up to `maxMarketOrdersPerTurn` (10) market orders per turn -- batching is a real, intended engine capability. Checked our own code (`src/agent.py`'s HIRE block, pre-fix): a single `if (...): orders.append(["HIRE"])`, never a loop -- we could only ever queue ONE hire per turn regardless of config, meaning reaching a 7-unit workforce took ~7 separate turns even though nothing prevented requesting all of them in turn 1. (Separately tested and ruled out first: raising `opening_hires_day0` 2->6 alone, still single-hire-per-turn code, produced bit-identical results in all 18 test episodes -- confirmed the bottleneck was the one-hire-per-turn code structure, not any config threshold.)
- Result: **implemented the same `while` loop pilkwang uses** (bounded by `max_hires_per_day`, affordability, and the existing backlog/opening-forced justification -- no gate logic changed, just batched). Ablation (6 seeds x chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang, baseline vs batch-hire): chaitanyajamble avg margin **-1,320 -> +717**, wins **1/6 -> 4/6** (flips from a losing matchup to a winning one on average, better in all 6/6 individual episodes); daisy023 avg margin **+12,631 -> +14,484**, still 6/6 wins, better in all 6/6 episodes; pilkwang avg margin **-101,042 -> -95,068** (still unwinnable -- 4/6 episodes improved, 2/6 slightly worse, net positive, one seed improved by +35,106). Full data: `.claude/scratch/pilkwang_backlog_comparison/results_batch_hire.json`.
- Notes: **applied to `src/agent.py` and `submission/main.py` rebuilt.** By far the largest, most consistent improvement found in this entire animal-buying investigation -- larger than every other change combined. Notably, `crisis_trip_day` and `animal_buy_orders` were IDENTICAL (0 and 1 respectively) between baseline and batch-hire in every single episode: batch hiring does NOT fix the `in_crisis` permanent-latch problem or unlock more animal purchases -- it just makes the existing crop economy dramatically more effective by giving it a full workforce ~5 turns sooner every single day (hands expire and must be re-hired daily -- see `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`'s "Hands disappear at day end" note -- so this saving repeats every day of the season, not just day 0). This is a genuine missed-capability bug, not a spending-posture tradeoff, which is why it doesn't share the regression pattern every animal/land-spending change in this investigation has shown. Good example of the `agy` delegation pattern per this project's standing rule: the model found the real mechanism and cited exact evidence (source lines + real match data), independent verification confirmed every claim, and only then was a fix implemented and empirically re-verified before being applied.
