## 2026-08-23 — fixed wasted late-pasture builds (real bug, agy/Gemini-diagnosed, verified + APPLIED): small, consistent, zero-downside win
- Command: user watched a replay (pilkwang match, seed-less) and noticed pastures being built at day 23/25, near the end of the game, and asked what caused it. Traced `output/games/20260823-175402_.../actions.csv` directly: confirmed `BUILD_PASTURE` at steps 1/18/568/618 (days 0/0/23/25) but only ONE `PLACE COW` ever, at step 5 (day 0) — the day-23/25 pastures are permanently empty, built 12+ days after `animal_purchase_last_day: 11` closed the purchase window for good. Delegated root-cause + fix diagnosis to `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` (Claude models still quota-blocked from the earlier 4-way audit), pointed at `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`, `src/agent.py`, and that exact replay (`.claude/scratch/wasted_pasture_diagnosis/REPORT.md`). Verified the diagnosis directly against the code before trusting it, then tested the proposed fix with the same seeded-ablation methodology as every other change today (`.claude/scratch/wasted_pasture_diagnosis/run_ablation.py`, 6 seeds x chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang, baseline vs fix).
- Result: **agy's diagnosis was correct and precise**: `target_structures` (agent.py, `_plan_units`) is computed from labor capacity and land alone and only drops to 0 during `wind_down_days` — it never accounts for `animal_purchase_last_day`, so `_tier_empty_build`'s `continue_fn=lambda: structures_committed < target_structures` keeps assigning idle units to `BUILD_PASTURE`/`BUILD_COOP` on day 23+ even though nothing built that late can ever hold an animal. **Fix**: also zero `target_structures` once `day > config["animal_purchase_last_day"]`. Ablation result: **better in 18/18 episodes, no exceptions** — chaitanyajamble avg margin -1,739 -> **-1,320**; daisy023 +12,245 -> **+12,631** (still 6/6 wins); pilkwang -101,448 -> **-101,042**. Build orders dropped from ~6-7/game to exactly 2/game in every episode (confirms the mechanism precisely: only the 2 day-0 pastures, built before the cutoff, still happen). Animal count unchanged at 1/game in every episode, as expected — this fix doesn't touch purchase economics at all, only stops wasting labor on structures that were already guaranteed useless.
- Notes: **applied to `src/agent.py` and `submission/main.py` rebuilt** (`python src/build_submission.py`) — the first change from today's whole animal-buying investigation that's small, positive, and carries essentially no risk (confirmed empirically, not just asserted). Distinct from every other tested idea today (double-gate fix: real bug, zero effect; `in_crisis` removal: regresses; priority-weight swap: craters; `build_labor_reserve`: craters) in that it doesn't touch animal count or purchase gating at all — pure "stop wasting labor on something already provably useless." Good example of the `agy` delegation pattern working as intended per this project's standing rule: the model did the labor (read the rules/code/replay, found the exact mechanism, drafted an exact diff), the actual verification (re-deriving the line numbers, running the seeded ablation) was done independently before trusting or applying it.

## 2026-08-23 — implemented `build_labor_reserve` (guaranteed build-labor slice, not a full priority swap): STILL regresses badly at just 1 unit/turn — reveals the real constraint is animal upkeep, not construction speed
- Command: added `build_labor_reserve` (int, default 0/no-op) to `src/agent.py` — reserves up to N idle units per turn for `empty_build` *before* the winner-take-all `empty_build`/`empty_plant` race runs on the rest (see code comment at the `land_tier_order` site, ~agent.py:1076). Added to `optimize.py`'s search space (`build_labor_reserve`, 0-3). Tested reserve=0/1/2, 6 seeded episodes each vs chaitanyajamble/daisy023/pilkwang (`.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/run_ablation4_reserve.py`).
- Result: **even reserve=1 (a single guaranteed unit per turn, the smallest possible dose) is catastrophic, nearly as bad as the full priority swap from the previous entry.** chaitanyajamble: -1,739 -> **-27,367** avg margin (reserve=1) -> -31,196 (reserve=2). daisy023: +12,245 (6/6 wins) -> **-10,454, 0/6 wins** (reserve=1) -> -15,505 (reserve=2) — flips to a clean loss at the *smallest* possible reserve, same as the full swap. pilkwang: -101,448 -> -125,039 (reserve=1) -> -108,293 (reserve=2, non-monotonic vs pilkwang specifically but still worse than baseline). Animal count: 1 -> 5 (reserve=1) -> 6 (reserve=2); pasture builds: ~7 -> 7 -> 8 (barely changes — confirms pastures were already reaching close to target even at baseline, construction speed was never the bottleneck).
- Notes: **this reveals the real constraint, and it isn't dispatch-related at all: it's the recurring daily FEED+CARE labor cost of each animal, not the one-time construction/purchase cost.** `build_orders` barely moved (7->7->8) between reserve=0 and reserve=1, meaning pastures were already getting built close to `target_structures` even at baseline — construction speed was never the bottleneck. What changed is animal *count* (1->5), and `feed`/`care` are the two highest-weighted tiers in the whole dispatch system (`priority_weight_feed: 100`, `priority_weight_care: 95`, both above every crop-maintenance tier). Every animal bought creates a permanent, unavoidable, top-priority daily labor draw for the rest of the season — 5-6 animals' worth of feed/care taxes the same ~7-unit labor pool that crops already saturate, for all ~29 remaining days, not just during the initial construction window. This is a materially different (and more complete) explanation than the previous entry's "one-time opportunity cost of delayed planting" framing: it's an ongoing structural tax, which is why even the smallest possible nudge (reserve=1, minimal one-time construction cost) still produces near-worst-case damage once the animals it enables start drawing upkeep. Combined with the already-confirmed refutation of "just add more workforce" (2026-08-23 workforce-scaling entry, three models' hypothesis, directly refuted), this closes off both directions: neither more raw labor nor smarter one-time dispatch allocation fixes this — top opponents' higher animal counts require their labor to be used more *efficiently* per unit (smarter routing/specialization), not more abundant or differently front-loaded. `build_labor_reserve` stays in the code as a real, tested, off-by-default mechanism (like every other `*_multiple`/gate knob here) — **default 0, not applied to `best_config.json`.**

---

## 2026-08-23 — tested inverting plant-vs-build priority: DOES unlock 4-6 animals (matches top-opponent counts), CRATERS score 10-30x
- Command: `.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/run_ablation3.py` — same `src/agent.py`, single config change: swapped `priority_weight_empty_build` (40.43) and `priority_weight_empty_plant` (89.89) so build/animal-infrastructure tasks claim labor ahead of planting each turn (the dispatcher greedily drains the highest-weight tier before touching the next — this is a pure ordering flip of two already-tunable knobs, not a code change). 6 seeded episodes each vs chaitanyajamble, daisy023, pilkwang.
- Result: **animal count went 1 -> 6 per game in every episode (18/18) — this is what "buy 4-5 animals like top opponents" actually looks like, mechanically. And it's a disaster:** chaitanyajamble avg margin -1,739 -> **-31,913** (18x worse); daisy023 avg margin +12,245 (6/6 wins) -> **-13,934 (0/6 wins)** — our one clean winning matchup flips to a clean loss; pilkwang avg margin -101,448 -> **-122,783** (worse). No opponent, no seed, no exception improved. Full data: `.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/results_build_over_plant.json`.
- Notes: **this answers the user's actual question — why do we plant instead of building/buying animals at the start.** The dispatcher is a single global greedy priority queue (tiers sorted by `priority_weight_*`, fully drained top-to-bottom before the next tier gets any labor); Optuna already searched this exact tradeoff (both weights are free 0-100 knobs in `KNOB_SPECS`) and converged on planting dominant because **it wins** — crops are this agent's primary income source, and delaying them to chase animal/pasture construction starves the revenue that pays for everything else, tanking the score far more than the animals gain back. Combined with the previous two entries (double-gate bug: real but inert; `in_crisis` gate removal alone: mildly regresses), this closes the investigation: **going from 1 to 4-6 animals is achievable with a one-line config change, and every single measurement says not to.** The animal-count gap with top opponents is a symptom of a structurally different, more labor-efficient economy (parallel dedicated labor for build+animal alongside planting, not winner-take-all sequencing) — not a misconfigured knob in ours. Closing it for real would need a dispatch redesign (e.g., reserve a small fixed labor fraction for build/animal tasks that runs *alongside* planting rather than pre-empting it), not another priority-weight or gate change — flagging as a real, larger project rather than attempting a quick patch here.

## 2026-08-23 — tested removing the `in_crisis` gate from animal purchases directly: unlocks 1 more animal, REGRESSES every matchup
- Command: `.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/run_ablation2.py` — a variant of `src/agent.py` (`.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/variant_no_crisis_gate.py`) with `animal_phase_ok` no longer requiring `not in_crisis` (land/hire untouched), vs the baseline (previous entry's double-gate fix, still `in_crisis`-gated). 6 seeded episodes each (`SEARCH_SEEDS[:6]`, alternating seats) vs chaitanyajamble, daisy023, and pilkwang.
- Result: **animal count went from 1 -> 2 per game in every single episode (18/18) once the `in_crisis` gate was removed for animals -- confirms `in_crisis` really was the binding constraint. But the extra animal made the score WORSE in every matchup tested, no exceptions:** chaitanyajamble avg margin -1739.0 -> -2593.7 (still 1/6 wins either way), daisy023 avg margin +12245.2 -> +10781.0 (still 6/6 wins, but a smaller margin), pilkwang avg margin -101447.8 -> -102493.0. Full data: `.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/results_no_crisis_gate.json`.
- Notes: **directly answers the "why do we buy 1 animal when top opponents buy 4-5" question — it is very unlikely to be a fixable bug in our purchase gating.** `in_crisis` blocking further purchases after day 0 is, empirically, the right call given this codebase's current architecture: removing the block gets a 2nd animal but it's a net loss of value in every opponent tested, joining the long list of "loosen animal spending" attempts in `IDEAS_TRIED.md` that all regressed (different mechanism this time -- a hard structural gate rather than a reserve/ROI threshold -- but the same outcome). Consistent with the standing structural conclusion from the 2026-08-22 `IDEAS_TRIED.md` entries and the pilkwang 0W-16L study: strong opponents' higher animal counts almost certainly come from a fundamentally different system (staged herd targets, per-quadrant crop mix, opponent fingerprinting, dedicated dispatch capacity) that makes more animals *pay for themselves* in their architecture -- not from a looser purchase gate that would also pay off in ours. Buying more animals here just diverts cash from things that currently earn more per dollar under our thinner labor pool. **Recommendation: do not chase animal count as a target.** The `in_crisis` fix stays unapplied to `src/agent.py` (the double-gate fix from the previous entry is the only change kept). If animal economics are to be revisited, it should be via genuine structural changes (dedicated animal-tending labor allocation, herd-size staging) verified the same way, not another purchase-gate loosen.

## 2026-08-23 — fixed the roi/reserve double-gate bug, measured ZERO effect, found the real root cause: `in_crisis` latches permanently true and never recovers
- Command: applied the fix both completed agy audits (previous entry) recommended — restored the pre-refactor exclusivity between the ROI check and the `animal_reserve_multiple` cushion in `src/agent.py`'s `_market_orders` (GOOSE, COW/SHEEP, and land purchase blocks), using `roi_margin > 0` as the numeric no-op switch instead of reintroducing a boolean. Snapshot of the pre-fix code kept at `.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/buggy_agent_snapshot.py`. Ran a seeded before/after ablation (`.claude/scratch/animal_fix_ablation/run_ablation.py`, 4 episodes each vs chaitanyajamble and pilkwang, alternating seats, `SEARCH_SEEDS[:4]`) plus a turn-by-turn instrumented trace (`trace2.py`) with temporary debug prints (removed after).
- Result: **the fix is real (verified against the diff) but had measurably ZERO effect on animal count** — every one of the 16 ablation episodes (buggy and fixed alike) bought exactly 1 animal and 0 land purchases the entire game; chaitanyajamble rewards were bit-identical buggy vs fixed. Instrumenting `in_crisis` directly (day, `crisis_backlog`, `unit_count_now`, threshold) showed the actual mechanism: `crisis_backlog` (unfed/unwatered/unharvested/weeded/unfertilized tile count) climbs past `unit_count_now * crisis_backlog_ratio` (2.177) partway through day 0 once hiring caps out around 7 units (threshold ~15.24 vs backlog reaching 18-24 same day) and **never drops back below it for the rest of the game** (day 1: backlog 22-47 the whole day, same pattern continuing). Since `animal_phase_ok = not in_crisis and day <= animal_purchase_last_day`, this permanently blocks the entire animal-purchase block (and the equivalent land block) for the rest of the season after day 0 — independent of the reserve/ROI cushion math the double-gate bug affects. Pasture construction (`_tier_empty_build` in `_plan_units`) is a separate code path not gated by `in_crisis`, which is why extra `BUILD_PASTURE` events still appear later (~day 20+) with no animals ever bought to fill them.
- Notes: **this supersedes the previous entry's root-cause framing** — neither completed agy audit found this; both centered on the reserve/ROI conjunction (real, but not binding: fixing it changed nothing) and, for the docs-blocked run, a dispatch-priority theory that also didn't hold up (the agent does keep pursuing `target_structures=7` over time, matching `max_structures`, just with no animals bought to place in them). This is the concrete, current-code instance of the same pattern this project's own history keeps finding: model-audit hypotheses sound plausible and sometimes partially right, but only direct instrumentation caught the actual binding mechanism (see the workforce-scaling entry below, where 3 models agreed on a wrong causal direction). The double-gate fix is being kept in `src/agent.py` regardless (it's a genuine logic defect, restores intended pre-refactor semantics, doesn't regress anything measured) but does NOT by itself fix the reported symptom. **Not yet fixed**: whether `in_crisis` latching permanently is itself a bug (a crisis gate that never recovers isn't doing triage, it's a one-way switch) or a sign that `crisis_backlog_ratio`/hiring capacity are mistuned for this economy's actual workload is unresolved — flagging rather than hand-patching, consistent with `IDEAS_TRIED.md`'s standing caution against isolated land/animal-gate tweaks. Next step should be measuring whether `in_crisis` ever un-latches under ANY of this project's benchmark opponents/seeds before deciding whether this is a threshold-tuning question or a design defect (a backlog-clearing/hysteresis mechanism, e.g. requiring backlog to drop well below threshold rather than just below it, might be the real fix).

## 2026-08-23 — 4-way agy model audit (2 Gemini completed, 2 Claude blocked by quota): confirmed root cause of the animal-buying collapse
- Command: 4 parallel `agy` diagnostic investigations (claude-opus-4-6-thinking x2, gemini-3.1-pro-high x2 — one of each pair with full `docs/` access, the other blocked from reading `docs/`), each tasked with empirically tracing why the current agent buys only ~1 animal vs 4-5+ for strong opponents (pilkwang, kawa). Both `claude-opus-4-6-thinking` and `claude-sonnet-4-6` failed instantly with "Individual quota reached... resets in 144h" (an Antigravity account-wide limit, not per-model) — only the 2 Gemini runs completed. Reports: `.claude/scratch/animal_audit_{B_gemini_docs,D_gemini_nodocs}/REPORT.md`.
- Result: **both completed Gemini runs independently converged on the same primary root cause**, which was then independently reproduced here directly against the code (not taken on trust): the uncommitted "remove boolean toggles" refactor to `src/agent.py`'s `_market_orders` collapsed what was previously an either/or gate — `roi_gate_enabled=True` -> ROI check only, cheap `_cushion(reserve_floor)`; `False` -> no ROI check, full `animal_reserve_multiple`-scaled cushion — into an always-both conjunction. The last committed champion config had `roi_gate_enabled: true`, so it now pays the full `animal_reserve_multiple` (4.725, ~$2290 cash for a COW) cushion on top of the ROI check it used to skip entirely. Confirmed directly: `_animal_roi_ok("COW", ...)` returns True for day 0-7 and False from day 8 onward (`season_days - day - first_yield_day - wind_down_days` shrinks below the `roi_margin=2.27` payback bar) — the agent can't accumulate ~$2290 liquid cash that fast after day-0 hire/land spending, so at most one purchase clears both gates before the window shuts. Run D (docs-blocked) additionally found and verified, purely from code + its own trace, a secondary contributing bottleneck: `priority_weight_empty_plant` (89.89) vs `priority_weight_empty_build` (40.43) in `best_config.json` starves pasture construction of labor — the agent builds only 1 pasture early on and doesn't build a second until ~day 20, past the `animal_purchase_last_day: 11` cutoff regardless of the reserve bug.
- Notes: a real uncommitted regression, not a config-tuning question — `git diff HEAD -- src/agent.py` shows the exact merged branches. **Not yet fixed or applied.** Each completed run's report has its own 5 suggested actions (10 total); they overlap on: (1) restore the roi-gate/reserve-multiple exclusivity without booleans, (2) retune `priority_weight_empty_build` up relative to `empty_plant`, (3) re-run an isolated ablation (HEAD vs the fix) before trusting it, consistent with this project's standing bar that model-audit findings get measured, not applied on confidence alone (see the workforce-scaling entry two below this one). The 2 Claude-perspective slots (with-docs / no-docs) are still open, blocked on agy's quota reset (~2026-08-29); user chose to proceed with just the Gemini pair for now rather than substitute another model or run them via local Claude Code budget.

## 2026-08-23 — removed all nine boolean *_enabled toggles; every mechanism now always-on, disabled only by its own numeric no-op
- Command: refactor of `src/agent.py` + `src/optimize.py`. Removed `animal_enabled`, `enable_coop`, `roi_gate_enabled`, `phase_gate_enabled`, `buy_fertilizer`, `opening_enabled`, `relative_wealth_enabled`, `opponent_awareness_enabled`, `crash_sell_enabled`. Each mechanism now runs unconditionally, switched off only by its own knob reaching a no-op value: `roi_margin=0`, `risk_sensitivity=0`, `opening_days=0`, `coop_ratio=0`, `fertilizer_stock_target=0`, a large `crash_sell_margin`. Two booleans that had no numeric equivalent were replaced by continuous knobs: `enable_coop` -> `coop_ratio` (0.0-1.0, fraction of structures built as coops), `buy_fertilizer` -> `fertilizer_stock_target` (int, buy until the shed holds this many). No `if True:` remnants or dead branches left behind. `KNOB_SPECS`/`sample_config` synced; `best_config.json` migrated (removed keys translated to their continuous equivalents; `crash_sell_margin` set to 999 to keep a mechanism the champion had OFF from silently switching on).
- Result: **verified behaviour-preserving for the champion** -- vs chaitanyajamble identical (1W-3L, -1,162 both pre and post); vs rajan1673 same 4W-0L, slightly better margin (+27,055 -> +30,124, from unifying the two ROI cushion branches). Compiles, builds, runs clean episodes.
- Notes: rationale is the Halite IV 4th-place solution's own stated lesson (`docs/PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md`): *"continuous scoring models rather than binary discrete threshold conditions... a much smoother fitness landscape."* Booleans create cliffs TPE cannot climb, and the v16 pruner-starvation incident showed exactly how badly a categorical arm can get starved. Also removes the `_resolve_search_config` special-case that forced `max_structures=0` when animals were off -- no longer needed. Study bumped to `v19_toggles_removed` (9 categoricals out, 2 numerics in = a real search-space shape change, so v17/v18 history is not comparable). Search launched with `pilkwang` (~2008, the strongest confirmed genuinely-reactive opponent) in the pool for the first time, per the audit finding that the search has never faced upward pressure from a strong reactive agent.
## 2026-08-23 — workforce-scaling hypothesis TESTED AND REFUTED (all three audits agreed on it; direct measurement says no)
- Command: `.claude/scratch/ablate_workforce.py` -- 4 seeded episodes each vs pilkwang (~2008, genuinely reactive), chaitanyajamble (516), rajan1673 (427), comparing the champion against three scaled-up variants.
- Result: **raising the workforce cap makes things uniformly WORSE.**

  | variant | pilkwang | chaitanyajamble | rajan1673 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | champion (6 hires, backlog 2.46) | 0W  -105,705 | **1W**  -1,162 | **4W  +27,055** |
  | A: 12 hires | 0W  -125,786 | 0W  -12,952 | 3W  +1,048 |
  | B: 12 hires + backlog 1.2 | 0W  -122,874 | 0W  -14,862 | 2W  -212 |
  | C: B + land_startup_days 2 | 0W  -122,874 | 0W  -14,862 | 2W  -212 |

- Notes: **this is the single most-agreed-upon finding from three independent full-repo audits (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-OSS 120B), and it does not survive direct measurement.** All three independently concluded "max_hires_per_day: 6 is half the workforce of stronger opponents, raise it"; measuring it directly reproduces the exact pattern already documented 9+ times in IDEAS_TRIED.md's spending-posture section -- more hands the dispatcher cannot productively route just means more units walking, not working. The audits reasoned correctly *from observed correlation* (pilkwang genuinely does run ~13 units and earn ~4x) but the causal direction doesn't hold for our dispatcher. Recording this prominently because model consensus is NOT evidence -- three models agreeing was worth exactly one ablation to check. Note also variant C is byte-identical to B, meaning `land_startup_days=2` changed nothing: land purchase is gated elsewhere (`land_min_days_left`/utilization threshold), so that knob is inert at this setting -- worth knowing before anyone tunes it.
## 2026-08-23 — full root-cause audit (2 models, cross-verified): fixed a real dispatch bug, submission staleness confirmed as the dominant score cause
- Command: two independent full-repo audits (Claude Opus 4.6 and Gemini 3.1 Pro) on the question "why is the live score ~500 while others reach 2000+". Gemini's first pass contained a fabricated finding (claimed commit df052d2 postdated and corrupted v14's config -- git history shows df052d2 is FIVE commits BEFORE v14; the real explanation is the legitimate v15/v18 promotion chain). Opus's pass was independently verified here: every config number it cited (max_hires_per_day=6, hire_backlog_ratio=2.46, priority_weight_feed=27 vs priority_weight_care=78, animal_purchase_last_day=11, land_min_days_left=20, land_startup_days=7) matched best_config.json exactly, and its claim that Gemini's "double assignment" finding was a non-issue was confirmed by direct code read (`candidates.remove(target)` genuinely prevents cross-tier re-assignment).
- Result: **fixed a real, verified bug** -- in `_plan_units`'s sticky-continuation phase, a unit walking toward a remembered `empty_plant` target could arrive to find every crop's shadow-priced score had gone negative during the walk (labor/land scarcity rose). `action` stayed `None` and got committed as that unit's entire turn. Confirmed via direct engine test that a `None` action doesn't crash (engine silently no-ops it, not caught by the SAFE_FALLBACK exception handler) -- so this was a real but narrow single-turn waste per occurrence, contributing to but not solely responsible for the ~14% idle-turn rate the audit measured against kawa. Fixed by releasing the unit back to `pending` for a fresh assignment that turn, matching the existing fallback pattern exactly (`pending.append([idx, pos, inv])`). Verified: compiles, runs a clean episode.
- Notes: **the dominant finding stays what was already suspected: nothing has been submitted in 18+ hours**, so the live ~500 score reflects pre-Phase-0 code entirely. Beyond that, the audit surfaced a real structural pattern worth a dedicated search: the current champion's workforce/land/animal parameters (6 hires cap, narrow day 7-10 land window, animal buying stops day 11, FEED priority ranked below CARE despite CARE banking nothing without FEED) all look tuned for a small-workforce equilibrium -- because the search pool has never included a strong reactive opponent (pilkwang, ~2008, confirmed genuinely reactive not a tape) to apply upward pressure. Worth adding to a future search pool specifically to test the "search never had reason to scale up" hypothesis, not applied yet -- this session prioritized the verified, low-risk dispatch fix over an unverified hand-tune of 6+ parameters at once.

## 2026-08-23 — v18 champion-weighted search PROMOTED, but independent re-check shows a weak/marginal result, not a clean win
- Command: `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 1000 --n-jobs 6 --opponents chaitanyajamble,daisy023,kiykhoi,rajan1673,umutdorukztrk,selfvar_no_expand,selfvar_panic_seller,champion,champion,champion,champion --study-name v18_champion_weighted_rot0` -- champion weighted 4/11 slots instead of 1/8, direct response to the prior entry's diagnosis (pool-average optimization misaligned with the champion-specific promotion target).
- Result: **6W-4L-0T verification (avg margin -651.8), holdout PASSED on all three** (ektarr/nagatakengo/kawa all within tolerance of the prior champion). Promoted, `best_config.json` updated, `submission/main.py` rebuilt.
- Notes: **champion-weighting fixed the gate-clearing problem** -- this is the first search all night to actually pass verification (prior two: 0W-10L, 4W-6L). But independent re-verification on 8 fresh HOLDOUT_SEEDS episodes (never seen by this search) came back **4W-4L, avg margin +168** -- essentially noise-level parity with the prior (v15) champion, nowhere near v14's clean 6W-0L/+8833 confirmation. Being honest rather than overselling: this promotion is procedurally legitimate (cleared the project's own two-gate bar, which is now correctly built -- seeded, win-based, pruner-fixed) but the evidence for it actually being BETTER is weak. Consistent with this project's own established convention (see the v5 promotion entry: "the dedicated gate is the actual promotion mechanism... kept the promotion for now"), keeping it rather than reverting on ambiguous-not-negative evidence -- but flagging plainly that this is a marginal result, not a confirmed advance. Next: raising verification/holdout episode counts for future searches on this now-correctly-aligned setup, since the actual problem is thin evidence, not a wrong direction.

## 2026-08-23 — v17 main 1500-trial search: DECISIVELY REJECTED (0W-10L), worse than the 300-trial run -- pool/champion mismatch, not noise
- Command: `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 1500 --n-jobs 6` (fixed pruner, default 8-opponent pool, rotation 0).
- Result: best trial verified **0W-10L-0T, avg margin -4,921.8** against the champion -- decisively worse than the smaller 300-trial run's near-miss (4W-6L, -611.0) on the identical pool/objective. More search budget found a MORE overfit peak, not a better one.
- Notes: this is a known failure shape, not new -- the objective averages margin across 8 pool opponents where the champion is 1/8 of the signal (`episodes_per_opponent` episodes each, "champion" appearing once in the default pool), while promotion requires beating the champion SPECIFICALLY. A config can dominate the pool average by beating the other 7 opponents harder while losing to the one that actually gates promotion. Same root shape as v11's reverted promotion and v15's holdout-margin erosion -- pool-level optimization pressure misaligned with the champion-specific promotion target. Per standing instruction (autonomous overnight work, don't repeat a result pointing at an old finding): NOT rerunning this search again. Pivoting to directly reweighting "champion" in the training pool so search pressure aligns with what promotion actually requires, rather than hoping a bigger random search finds a lucky generalizing peak.

## 2026-08-23 — v17 pruner-fix validation: v16's "animals are useless" conclusion INVALIDATED, near-even verification
- Command: `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 300 --n-jobs 6 --study-name v17_pruner_validation2` (first attempt at 300 trials crashed mid-run on `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked`, caused by running 5 concurrent agy audit processes alongside the 6 search workers -- same class of contention this project hit once before at n_jobs=8. Rerun clean with no concurrent load, completed normally).
- Result: **`animal_enabled=True` reversed from 1.5% of completions (v16, broken pruner) to 91% (v17, fixed pruner) -- 19/19 (100%) of the top trials have it ON.** Confirms the v16 "animals are useless" verdict was the pruner-starvation artifact diagnosed earlier, not a real finding -- treat that verdict as void. Unexpected: `lambda_labor` is now the single dominant parameter (importance 0.371), directly contradicting the "labor shadow-pricing converges near-zero" result confirmed independently in v10/v11/v14 -- worth investigating rather than dismissing, since none of those searches ran with a working pruner either and may have under-explored labor-scarce (animal-heavy) regions for the same starvation reason. Verification: **4W-6L-0T, avg margin -611.0** -- did not promote (holdout not reached), but a near-even result, not a decisive rejection.
- Notes: this genuinely reopens the animal-economy question this project spent most of a day concluding was settled-negative (9+ documented "spend more aggressively" failures) -- those failures may have been real, or may share the same pruner-starvation root cause if they were ever decided by a search rather than a dedicated hand-built ablation. Not rewriting that history without direct re-evidence. Immediate next step: a longer search on the now-correctly-measured landscape to see if it can close the 4W-6L gap, since a near-even verification on a newly-opened, previously inaccessible search region is a promising lead, not a confirmed dead end.

## 2026-08-22 — implemented + tested pilkwang-style phase-window gates, regressed a third time
- Command: added `phase_gate_enabled`/`land_min_days_left`/`animal_purchase_last_day`/
  `crisis_backlog_ratio` to `agent.py`, modeled directly on `src/opponents/pilkwang_agent.py`'s real
  source (`LAND_OPEN_DAYS`+`left>=12` land cutoff, `ANIMAL_PURCHASE_LAST_DAY=18` animal cutoff, and
  a CRISIS phase that pauses new capital spending when at-risk items exceed labor) -- purely
  ADDITIVE restrictions on top of the existing land/animal gates, not a replacement, unlike the two
  earlier attempts today. Verified via `.claude/scratch/test_phase_gate.py`, same paired-seeded
  setup as the other two tests today: champion (`phase_gate_enabled=False`) vs candidate (same
  config + `phase_gate_enabled=True`, `land_min_days_left=12`, `animal_purchase_last_day=18`,
  `crisis_backlog_ratio=2.5`, pilkwang's own real values), same 4 opponents/seeds.
- Result: champion 7/8 wins, avg margin +15,939.6 (same baseline). Candidate 6/8 wins, avg margin
  **+12,960.0** -- almost identical magnitude to the land_reserve_multiple cut (+12,934.1), better
  than the ROI gate (+10,502.9). Per-opponent deltas: chaitanyajamble -7,254/-6,291 (worst matchup
  got worse again), daisy023 -3,018/-3,018, kiykhoi -5,670/-1,541, rajan1673 +2,358/+597 (again the
  only improvement).
- Notes: **this is the third consecutive test today where ANY additional restriction on land/animal
  purchasing -- naive multiplier cut, ROI-payback replacement, now additive phase-window cutoffs --
  regressed against this same 4-opponent set, with the identical qualitative fingerprint each time:
  chaitanyajamble (our worst matchup) gets meaningfully worse, daisy023/kiykhoi get mildly worse,
  rajan1673 is the sole, modest beneficiary.** That convergence across three structurally different
  mechanisms is itself the finding: the problem is not which formula gates land/animal spending,
  it's that THIS opponent set (or our current champion's spending level generally) is already at or
  below the point where more restriction helps, at least against chaitanyajamble specifically.
  Likely explanation: pilkwang's specific day-window *numbers* (12/18) were tuned against HIS
  surrounding system (staged herd targets, per-quadrant crop mix, melon-tile caps, opponent
  fingerprinting) -- lifting just the numbers without the system they regulate is the same failure
  mode as copying his reserve formula in isolation two entries above. `phase_gate_enabled` stays
  merged, wired, and off by default -- `best_config.json` unchanged. **Given three independent
  mechanisms all point the same direction, further hand-picked land/animal gate changes (of any
  kind) are unlikely to help without either (a) a real Optuna search finding different threshold
  values than pilkwang's own, or (b) testing against a wider/different opponent pool than these 4,
  since chaitanyajamble specifically may just reward more spending than any of these three
  mechanisms allow.**

---

## 2026-08-22 — dedicated study: champion vs pilkwang_agent, 16 seeded episodes, 0W-16L
- Command: `.claude/scratch/study_vs_pilkwang.py` -- champion (`best_config.json`) vs `pilkwang`
  (`src/opponents/pilkwang_agent.py`, the extracted "Structured Economic Policy" notebook), 8
  distinct seeds (`SEARCH_SEEDS[:8]`), both seats each = 16 episodes.
- Result: **0W-16L-0T, avg margin -96,779.0.** Every single episode lost, margins ranging -75,501
  to -114,884, no seed/seat combination came close to competitive. Avg champion money $33,040 vs
  avg pilkwang money $129,819 -- pilkwang averages **3.93x** our money, closely matching the
  leaderboard rating gap in `docs/PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md` (pilkwang structured-policy 2008 vs
  our ~500, also ~4x). Two rows (seed 7042, both seats) produced bit-identical results -- expected,
  not a bug: neither agent's logic depends on which player slot it's assigned, so a seed alone
  fully determines each side's own outcome regardless of seat order.
- Notes: this is not noise -- IDEAS_TRIED.md already established the self-play noise floor at one
  seeded config beating an identical copy of itself by +1,285 margin; a -96,779 average with zero
  wins across 16 independent seeded worlds is a real, dominant, structural gap, not variance.
  pilkwang is in the SAME architectural tier we are (genuinely reactive per-turn logic, not a
  precomputed tape -- see PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md's tier split), so this rules out "we're
  fighting an unfair precomputed-schedule opponent" as the explanation here; the gap is earned by
  better reactive strategy, not architecture category. Directly contextualizes today's two failed
  reserve-gate experiments (land_reserve_multiple cut, ROI-payback gate) above: both tried to copy
  one narrow piece of pilkwang's design (the reserve formula) in isolation and both regressed --
  this study confirms the overall gap to pilkwang is far too large (~$97k avg margin) to be
  explained by one formula difference, supporting PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md's own conclusion that
  the real gap is structural (phase-scoped day-windowed parameters, policy decoupled from logic,
  opponent fingerprinting), not a single reserve/threshold knob -- consistent with why hand-copying
  isolated pieces hasn't transferred so far.

---

## 2026-08-22 — implemented + tested an ROI-payback purchase gate, regressed worse than a flat cut
- Command: added `roi_gate_enabled`/`roi_margin` to `agent.py` (new `_animal_roi_ok`/`_land_roi_ok`
  helpers, wired into the GOOSE/COW-SHEEP/BUY_LAND gates in `_market_orders`, replacing
  `animal_reserve_multiple`/`land_reserve_multiple`'s flat cushion with a projected-payback check
  when enabled -- see `agent.py` DEFAULT_CONFIG's `roi_gate_enabled` comment for the formula).
  Verified via `.claude/scratch/test_roi_gate.py` -- same paired-seeded-episode setup as the
  land_reserve_multiple test above: champion (`roi_gate_enabled=False`, i.e. unchanged) vs candidate
  (same config + `roi_gate_enabled=True`, `roi_margin=1.5`), same 4 opponents/seeds.
- Result: champion 7/8 wins, avg margin +15,939.6 (same baseline as the previous entry). Candidate
  6/8 wins, avg margin **+10,502.9** -- worse than the naive `land_reserve_multiple=1.0` cut
  (+12,934.1). Per-opponent deltas: chaitanyajamble -4,014/-2,803 (worse), daisy023
  **-15,918/-15,585** (previously a comfortable win, now barely positive), kiykhoi
  **-13,424/-13,738** (previously a blowout win, cut roughly in half), rajan1673 +10,293/+11,695
  (the only improvement, and a bigger one than before).
- Notes: motivated by the user's critique that the flat reserve multiplier was "an if-else based
  choice" that should be replaced by the dynamic ROI/payback reasoning the codebase already has for
  crop/animal scoring (`_crop_score`/`_animal_score`) -- a fair critique of the *design*, but the
  more principled mechanism performed WORSE empirically, not better. Root cause is almost certainly
  the same one already on record in this file's "production/sell-side hypothesis refuted" update
  (2026-08-21): *"the real constraint is capacity scale itself... raising that scale keeps
  backfiring... because of cash-flow/capital competition while ACQUIRING it"* -- a purchase that
  pencils out in isolation (this animal/quadrant would pay for itself given its own $/day rate)
  still competes with every other purchase and with existing operations for the same thin labor
  pool and the same simple greedy-nearest-tile dispatcher, which the ROI formula has no way to
  see. Loosening the gate just lets more purchases through than the dispatcher can productively
  service, exactly the same failure mode as every "spend more" attempt in the "Spending posture"
  section of `IDEAS_TRIED.md` -- this is now a *third* independent confirmation of that root cause
  in a single session (naive multiplier cut, then this). `roi_gate_enabled` stays merged and wired
  (a real, correctly-implemented mechanism, confirmed via this test to actually change behavior as
  designed) but defaults OFF -- `best_config.json` unchanged. **Practical implication for next
  steps**: further reserve-formula tuning (multiplier or ROI-based) is very unlikely to help without
  first improving dispatch/execution capacity itself -- the one category this project's own history
  (`IDEAS_TRIED.md`'s "Dispatch / routing efficiency" section) shows *actual verified wins* for,
  unlike every "spend more" attempt in the "Spending posture" section.

---

## 2026-08-22 — tested lowering land_reserve_multiple alone (pilkwang-formula-inspired), regressed
- Command: `.claude/scratch/test_land_reserve.py` — paired seeded comparison, champion (`best_config.json`,
  `land_reserve_multiple=3.284`) vs candidate (same config, `land_reserve_multiple=1.0`), both vs
  4 opponents (chaitanyajamble, daisy023, kiykhoi, rajan1673), 2 seated episodes each, same seeds
  for both configs.
- Result: champion 7/8 wins, avg margin +15,939.6. Candidate 6/8 wins, avg margin +12,934.1.
  Per-opponent deltas (candidate - champion): chaitanyajamble -5,209/-2,031 (our worst matchup got
  worse), daisy023 -10,541/-10,541, kiykhoi -1,249/-2,311, rajan1673 +1,214/+6,624 (only win).
- Notes: motivated by user observation that real match money rarely drops below ~$700-1000
  (confirmed via `.claude/scratch/money_trace.py` over 15 local games: per-game minimums range
  $8-1064, median $722) despite `money_reserve`/`seed_money_floor`/`hire_money_floor` all being
  tuned thin — traced the actual driver to `animal_reserve_multiple`/`land_reserve_multiple`'s
  proportional cushions on big-ticket costs (land cushion at champion's multiple is $3,284-$13,136
  for the three quadrants), not the flat floors, which are working as designed. Pilkwang Kim's
  published closed-form hire-reserve formula (`max(20, 3×marginal_hire_cost)`, see
  `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md` section 1) validates our HIRE reserve's shape specifically
  (our `hire_reserve_multiple≈2.5`/`hire_money_floor=10` is already comparably thin) but says
  nothing about land/animal reserves — extending "make it more pilkwang-like" to
  `land_reserve_multiple` was an untested generalization, and this result shows it doesn't
  transfer: **isolated reserve-loosening regressing is the same pattern documented 9+ times
  already in `docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`** ("Spending posture" section) for `max_hires_per_day`,
  `max_structures`, and `animal_reserve_multiple` specifically. `land_reserve_multiple=3.284` was
  not a guess -- it came out of the actual verified 10W-0L v14 search (see this file's 2026-08-22
  "Optuna search" entry) as part of a coupled package, and isolating it back down breaks that
  package the same way isolating other spend knobs down always has. Config NOT changed --
  `best_config.json` still has `land_reserve_multiple=3.284`. The genuinely untested idea this
  project's own notes point at instead (`IDEAS_TRIED.md`'s "production/sell-side hypothesis
  refuted" update, 2026-08-21) is a real forward-looking cash-flow plan using
  `income_lookahead_days`/`income_discount` (currently `income_lookahead_days=0` in the champion,
  i.e. not actually looking ahead despite existing) to let the cushion shrink when income is
  genuinely imminent, rather than hand-picking a smaller flat multiple -- not tried this entry.

## 2026-08-22 — removed opponent-wealth risk scale, added crash-sell + shed-clustering mechanisms
- Command: trace analysis (`.claude/scratch/analyze.py` over all `output/games/*/actions.csv`) +
  seeded ablation/smoke checks (`.claude/scratch/run_seeded.py`, `.claude/scratch/test_new_mechanics.py`),
  drafted by `agy --model gemini-3.1-pro-high` (Opus 4.6 was quota-limited), independently reproduced
  and then hand-applied to `src/agent.py`/`src/optimize.py`/`src/best_config.json` after review.
- Result: **movement-order check**: of 44,787 unit walks across all locally-run matches, only 432
  (<1%) took more steps than Manhattan distance — confirmed `farm_utils.step_toward`'s greedy x-then-y
  order is not a real inefficiency, left unchanged. **Tile-drift check**: average distance from
  occupied tiles to the nearest shed tile grows **+3.57 tiles** from game start to end — a real
  effect, confirmed by two independent runs of the same script. **Refactor-off parity**: `submission/
  main.py` (old code) vs the edited agent.py with both new mechanisms off, seed 42 vs `saiteja_agent`:
  identical `(20136.0, 33741.0)` both times — the refactor itself is behavior-preserving once the
  relative-wealth block is gone (note this is NOT equal to the pre-edit champion's `(22078.0,
  55163.0)`, since `best_config.json` had `risk_sensitivity=1.67`, a real nonzero effect being
  removed on purpose). **Smoke test with both new mechanisms on** (`crash_sell_enabled=True`,
  `cluster_shed_weight=1.0`) vs `saiteja_agent`, two different seeds: completed 720 steps, `DONE`/
  `DONE` both times, no exceptions.
- Notes: two mechanism changes, both **unverified by a real search/promotion gate, smoke-tested only**:
  (1) removed `relative_wealth_enabled`/`wealth_margin_scale`/`risk_sensitivity`/`risk_scale_min`/
  `risk_scale_max` and `_standing_asset_value` entirely (project owner's call: opponent-total-wealth
  is the wrong signal; only per-item exposure should matter) — a real, working mechanism that had
  actually been promoted (see 2026-08-22's "Optuna search" entry above, v14 champion), not a no-op
  being pruned, so this is a real behavior change to the shipped agent, not just new inert
  infrastructure; (2) added `crash_sell_enabled`/`crash_sell_margin`/`crash_sell_multiplier`/
  `crash_sell_chunk` (dump our smaller item exposure to crash the price before the opponent's larger
  one lands — offensive, distinct from the existing defensive `opponent_race_discount`) and
  `cluster_shed_weight` (pulls new pasture/crop tile claims toward the shed instead of pure local
  clustering) — both default off/0.0 per this project's no-op-by-default rule, wired into
  `KNOB_SPECS`/`optimize.py`'s `sample_config` for the next real search. `submission/main.py`
  regenerated via `build_submission.py` (also updated: dropped `_standing_asset_value` from
  `ROBUST_AGENT_FUNCS`, or the build would fail). `best_config.json` had its 5 dead keys removed.
  **Next step**: a real Optuna search (`optimize.py`) to actually tune `crash_sell_*`/
  `cluster_shed_weight` and re-verify the champion now that the wealth-risk mechanism is gone — this
  entry only confirms the code change is stable and behavior-preserving-when-off, not that the new
  mechanisms help.

## 2026-08-22 — second near-tier sourcing pass: 5 agents found, ZERO usable. The ~500 band looks exhausted
- Command: Antigravity CLI, second sourcing pass instructed to dig deeper than the first (deeper pagination, all sort orders, forks, low-vote kernels). Each candidate then measured here against the champion, 2 seeded episodes, same acceptance test as batch 1.
- Result: **0 of 5 usable.** `tuannm3812` (author score 481) -47,896 [-0.997], `emanuellcs` (492.6) -47,896 [-0.997], `beicicc` (unknown) -92,144 [-1.000] — all far STRONGER than us and fully saturated. `shrutikondabathula` (126.7) +37,404 [+0.986] and `pavloivanin` (unknown) +34,832 [+0.981] — we sweep them, saturated the other way. Both extremes are equally useless to a squashed objective. Also: `tuannm3812` and `emanuellcs` returned identical margins and have identical 20-function AST signature sets (different md5, so forks of one source) — the agent's own dedup check missed it. All five moved to `.claude/scratch/rejected_opponents/`.
- Notes: **the generalisable finding is that an author's leaderboard score does not predict the strength of the notebook they publish.** Four cases now: moncefelm (442 -> beats us by 89k), tuannm3812 (481 -> 48k), emanuellcs (492 -> 48k), beicicc (unknown -> 92k). People near our score either do not publish, or publish forks of strong agents while submitting something else. Combined with batch 1's 5-of-6 hit rate, sourcing from Kaggle is now at clear diminishing returns for this band. The remaining option for filling the gradient band is generating calibrated opponents ourselves — degraded variants of our own champion, whose strength can be dialled precisely by config. Caveat worth respecting: tuning primarily against variants of oneself is a known echo-chamber failure mode (noted in `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md` s7, "model deterioration in self-play"), so such variants should supplement the six real near-tier opponents, never replace them.
## 2026-08-22 — holdout ROTATION added; caught and fixed a flaw in the generated rotation sets
- Command: `src/optimize.py` — `HOLDOUT_ROTATIONS` replaces the single fixed `HOLDOUT_OPPONENTS`; `--holdout-rotation N` (default 0) selects one; the `--opponents` default is now *derived* as "near-tier opponents not in the active holdout, plus champion" so pool and holdout cannot contradict each other; study name auto-suffixed `_rot<N>` unless `--study-name` is passed. Implemented by the Antigravity CLI to spec; diff reviewed against a pre-change backup.
- Result: rotation mechanics correct — all 7 near-tier opponents held out at least once across 3 rotations, every pool >= 4 opponents, `kawa` in every rotation as a structural-collapse guard, leak guard verified firing per-rotation (rot1 correctly rejects `chaitanyajamble`, which only it holds out). **But the generated rotation SETS had a real flaw: rotation 0 — the default — held out both `chaitanyajamble` (-0.42, the only near-tier opponent we lose to) and `ektarr` (+0.20, the only roughly even one), leaving an all-winning pool (+0.52..+0.95).** That is a milder replay of the exact failure that flattened v12. Rebalanced to `[ektarr,nagatakengo,kawa]` / `[chaitanyajamble,daisy023,kiykhoi,kawa]` / `[rajan1673,umutdorukztrk,kawa]`; every pool now retains at least one informative matchup (rot0: chaitanyajamble+daisy023; rot1: ektarr; rot2: chaitanyajamble+daisy023+ektarr) and coverage of all 7 is preserved.
- Notes: added a module-level assertion (`_GRADIENT_ANCHORS = {chaitanyajamble, ektarr}`) that fails at import if any rotation holds out *both* — the invariant is "a pool of matchups we already win lopsidedly is nearly as uninformative as a pool we lose lopsidedly", which is the generalised lesson from v12 and is easy to violate again when rotations are edited by hand. Study separation per rotation matters because each rotation is a different opponent pool and therefore a different objective; mixing them in one study would corrupt MedianPruner comparisons, the same reason this file's study name has been bumped eight times. End-to-end smoke on the default rotation confirmed clean.

## 2026-08-22 — sourced 6 near-tier opponents; search landscape went from flat to informative
- Command: Antigravity CLI tasked to find/adapt/verify opponents in the 350-800 leaderboard band; new modules in `src/opponents/`. Registry + default pool wired by hand afterwards. Verified independently here (not taking the agent's report on trust — two of its reported numbers did not reproduce: iamsdt 45,050 claimed vs 1,896 measured; moncefelm 163,413 vs 85,523, partly seed variance).
- Result: all 6 run clean 720-step episodes (DONE/DONE). Measured against the current champion, 2 seeded episodes each, with the squashed value in brackets: **daisy023 0W-2L for us +8,730 [+0.52]**, **kiykhoi +18,656 [+0.85]**, **rajan1673 [+0.87]**, **umutdorukztrk +27,138 [+0.95]** — all give real gradient. **iamsdt +34,408 [+0.98]** and **mansiaggarwal88 +36,097 [+0.98]** are near-saturated (registry only). **moncefelm is NOT near-tier despite its author's 442 score: we lose 0W-2L by -89,308 [-1.0000], fully saturated** — a 71KB "compiled strategy", almost certainly a fork of a strong agent. Registry only, excluded from the pool.
- Notes: default `--opponents` is now `chaitanyajamble,daisy023,kiykhoi,rajan1673,umutdorukztrk,champion` — a deliberate spread of losing (-0.42), even (0.00) and winning (+0.52..+0.95) matchups, all inside the band where `_squash` still has slope. **Effect on the search landscape is large:** two random trials on the new pool scored -1.1436 and -0.8620, a spread of **0.28**, versus **0.0212 across all 21 completed trials** of the v12 run on the old pool. The optimizer can finally distinguish configs. Attribution added to `CREDITS.md`. Import note: these modules export only a `<name>_agent` alias (two of them define no `agent` symbol at all), unlike most existing opponents.

## 2026-08-22 — SEEDED PAIRED EVALUATION added; unseeded noise floor measured, and it is larger than any result we have promoted on
- Command: `src/optimize.py` — `play_episode(..., seed=None)`; two disjoint frozen panels `SEARCH_SEEDS` / `HOLDOUT_SEEDS` (12 each, asserted disjoint); seeds threaded through `evaluate_config` / `verify_candidate` / `verify_holdout` / `_worker`; `--seeded/--no-seeded`, default **ON**. Implemented by the Antigravity CLI to a written spec; diff reviewed line-by-line and independently re-verified here.
- Result: **pairing verified.** Identical config as both candidate and champion, seeded, 4 episodes → `(2W, 2L, 0T, avg margin 0.0)`, exactly reproducible across runs. The same self-vs-self comparison **unseeded** → `(3W, 1L, 0T, avg margin +1285.25)`. **That is the noise floor of every promotion decision made before today** — a config "beating" an identical copy of itself 3W-1L. v11 was promoted on 6W-4L, comfortably inside it.
- Notes: motivated by the competitor survey (`docs/PARAMETER_MODEL_FINDINGS.md`) — the two top-scoring authors both tune on frozen seed panels ("32 configs on held-out seeds"; "frozen 12-seed panels"). Seed effect is enormous: the same config vs kawa scores 63,732 on seed 42 and 111,771 on seed 7. Panels are disjoint so the holdout gate is out-of-sample on *worlds* as well as opponents. Agent closures are now rebuilt per episode — they carry `unit_targets` state that would otherwise leak between episodes and break determinism; caught during implementation, not in the original spec.

## 2026-08-22 — v12 search returned a null result, but the SEARCH SETUP was broken: objective saturation + holdout removing every winnable opponent
- Command: `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 400 --n-jobs 6` (v12 win-rate objective, unseeded, 12-opponent default pool).
- Result: **0W-10L-0T, avg margin -11,127.8**, no promotion. But the run is not usable evidence: only **21 of 400 trials completed (95% pruned)**, and the total spread across *all* completed trials was **0.0212** (best -1.2783, worst -1.2996). The landscape was essentially flat, so MedianPruner was killing trials on noise.
- Notes: **root cause is an interaction between two changes made earlier the same day, each correct in isolation.** `_squash` saturates any blowout matchup to a constant ±1.0; the new holdout gate removed *all four* near-tier opponents from the search pool. Result: exactly **1 of 12 search-pool opponents ("champion") gave the objective any gradient** — kawa -142k → -1.0000, premaananda108 -75k → -0.9999, and so on for the other ten. 22 of every trial's 24 episodes were informationally worthless. **v12 therefore says nothing about the heuristic ceiling and must not be cited as evidence for it.** Fixed by rebalancing: `HOLDOUT_OPPONENTS` is now `[ektarr, nagatakengo, kawa]` (one even matchup, one we sweep, plus kawa purely as a structural-collapse guard, since it can never be a promotion target), and the default `--opponents` pool is now `rajan1673,chaitanyajamble,champion` — all three verified to give real gradient (+0.87 / -0.42 / 0.00). Trials drop from 24 episodes to 6, every one informative. **Real remaining bottleneck: only two opponents in the entire registry are near our ~500 score.** Sourcing more of that tier now matters more than any further tuning.

## 2026-08-22 — OBJECTIVE FIX: trials were scored on raw coin margin, but the competition rates win/loss/tie only
- Command: `src/optimize.py` -- new `_squash()` (tanh of margin/`win_scale`) applied inside `_risk_adjusted_score`; `--win-scale` flag (default 15000, `0` restores legacy raw-margin scoring); threaded through `evaluate_config`/`make_objective`/`_worker`. Study renamed to `robust_agent_config_v12_winrate_objective`. Prompted by the user pointing at the competition's stated ranking rules: *"The actual coin difference in a match does not affect the rating change -- only the win, loss, or tie outcome matters."*
- Result: **the old objective was pointed at the wrong target, and measurably so.** Worked example on a representative 4-opponent margin vector (kawa -142k, prvsiyan -97k, chaitanyajamble -6k, nagatakengo +2k): candidate A improves kawa by 20k (still a loss, worth zero rating) and scores **+12,277** better than baseline under the old raw-margin score; candidate B flips chaitanyajamble to a genuine WIN and scores only **+1,526** better. The old objective preferred the worthless improvement by ~8x. Under the squashed score, A is **identical to baseline** (-0.9567, correctly worth nothing) while B improves to -0.8536 -- B correctly preferred.
- Notes: root cause is that kawa/prvsiyan blowouts (-97k..-142k) dominate the pool average by an order of magnitude over the genuinely contestable near-tier matchups (+-6k..25k), so "maximize average margin" mostly meant "lose slightly less lopsidedly to opponents we cannot beat". **This also explains a pattern running through this whole log**: `verify_candidate`'s promotion gate has always been win-based (`wins > losses`) while the search objective was margin-based -- the search was optimizing a different quantity from the gate it had to pass, which is why so many runs produced a "best trial" that then failed verification. tanh rather than a hard win/loss indicator because at 2 episodes/opponent a 3-valued signal is too noisy for TPE, and it would give zero gradient on a matchup where every episode loses (a config 500 coins from flipping an opponent would look identical to one 500k away). `verify_holdout` (added in the entry below) was rewritten in the same pass for the same reason -- it now treats losing an episode the champion won as an outright regression, and compares squashed rather than raw margins below that. **All prior studies' trial values are in different units and are not comparable to v12's** -- a harder break than any previous rename, which only changed search space or opponent pool.

## 2026-08-22 — CHAMPION DRIFT BUG: new mechanisms shipped with nonzero DEFAULT_CONFIG defaults silently degraded the champion; corrected to no-op defaults
- Command: `.claude/scratch/test_champion_drift.py` (4 episodes each, alternating seats, champion as-currently-run vs the same config with `lambda_labor=0, lambda_land=0, income_discount=0, income_lookahead_days=0`), found while validating the new holdout gate below.
- Result: **the live champion had silently stopped being the config verified 9W-1L.** `best_config.json` never pinned `lambda_labor`/`lambda_land`/`income_lookahead_days`/`income_discount`, so it inherited the nonzero defaults (3.0 / 15.0 / 2 / 0.7) added with shadow pricing and cash-flow lookahead earlier today. Measured cost to the champion: vs `chaitanyajamble` **-7,650.8 -> -13,173.0**; vs `ektarr` **+173.0 -> -2,317.2** (2W-2L either way). After setting all four defaults to 0.0/0 in `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, the champion re-measures vs `chaitanyajamble` **-6,755.0** (back inside its long-standing -3,890..-6,990 band across many prior entries) and vs `ektarr` **+683.0**. `submission/main.py` rebuilt.
- Notes: **this also corrupted a promotion decision.** The v11 robustness check (entry below) compared v11's `chaitanyajamble` number against *historical* baselines from this log rather than a concurrently-measured champion, and concluded v11 had "doubled the deficit". Measuring both side by side under the new holdout gate shows v11 at -15,413.5 vs champion at -14,666.5 -- essentially identical, i.e. that particular regression was the drift, not v11. v11's real regression was `ektarr` (-6,875.8 vs champion -2,825.5), which is genuine and independently reproduced, so the revert itself still stands -- but the headline reason given for it at the time was wrong. Root cause was the reasoning "default to a small nonzero value so the mechanism actually does something to test against", which ignored that an unpinned key means the *champion* changes too. Rule going forward, now written into `agent.py`'s own comment and already followed by `opening_enabled`/`broke_phase_*`: **a new mechanism defaults to a no-op and the search turns it on.** The mechanisms themselves are unaffected -- they remain fully searchable knobs in `KNOB_SPECS`/`sample_config`; only their defaults changed.

## 2026-08-22 — added an out-of-sample holdout gate to promotion (`verify_holdout`); validated by replaying v11 through it
- Command: `src/optimize.py` -- new `HOLDOUT_OPPONENTS = [chaitanyajamble, ektarr, rajan1673, nagatakengo]`, `verify_holdout()`, `--holdout-episodes` (default 4) / `--holdout-tolerance` (default 0.5), plus an argparse guard refusing any run that puts a holdout opponent in `--opponents`. Validated with `.claude/scratch/test_holdout_gate_on_v11.py`.
- Result: **the gate catches the failure it was built for.** Replaying v11's exact best-trial params (parsed straight from its own LOG entry) against the restored champion: `ektarr` v11 -6,875.8 vs champion -2,825.5 -> flagged REGRESSED, gate FAILED, promotion blocked. `chaitanyajamble` -15,413.5 vs -14,666.5, `rajan1673` +20,255.8 vs +26,730.5, `nagatakengo` +6,164.0 vs +10,609.5 all passed on tolerance. Leak guard verified: `--opponents kawa,ektarr` now exits with an error instead of silently destroying the out-of-sample property.
- Notes: promotion now requires BOTH gates -- candidate-vs-champion head-to-head (`wins > losses`, unchanged) AND no material regression on the held-out set. The check deliberately asks "is the candidate worse than the CHAMPION here", not "does the candidate win here": `chaitanyajamble`/`ektarr` are losing-or-even matchups for the champion too, so demanding wins would reject everything. Tolerance is relative (a fraction of the champion's own margin) because margins differ by orders of magnitude across opponents -- a -6k matchup and a -100k one can't share one absolute threshold. Holdout runs only when the first gate passes (it costs 2 x episodes x 4 opponents, pure waste on an already-rejected candidate). Same structural idea as the Halite IV 4th-place solution's evolutionary setup (`input/HaliteIV_0Zeta/evolutionary_optimization.py`), which scores every genome against a standing pool of baseline bots plus retained older genomes rather than against the current best alone -- see `input/index.json`.

## 2026-08-22 — reverted the v11 promotion after the robustness check above; back to the v6-derived champion
- Command: `git checkout -- src/best_config.json` (the file at `HEAD` was already the exact pre-v11 champion -- the v11 promotion was the only uncommitted change to this file, so this is a clean, lossless revert, not a reconstruction).
- Result: `best_config.json` restored to the v6-derived champion (`sell_fraction_base: 0.4629`, `money_reserve: 420`, `animal_reserve_multiple: 2.596`, `animal_enabled: true`, the hand-fixed `priority_weight_empty_build: 65.0`/`priority_weight_empty_pasture_place: 55.0` -- see the "diagnostic pasture-priority-bug fix" entry).
- Notes: acted directly on the robustness-check finding immediately above. The v11 promotion technically cleared this project's standing promotion gate (10-episode dedicated verification vs the prior champion, wins > losses), but that gate only ever tests candidate-vs-specific-old-champion in self-play -- it has no built-in check against diverse real opponents. This is the first time that gap actually produced a bad promotion: v11 beat the old champion 6W-4L (+1,942.7, a thin margin) while genuinely regressing against `chaitanyajamble` (an out-of-sample near-tier opponent with a long, stable multi-entry history in this log, -3,890 to -6,990 band, now -15,329.7) and losing cleanly to `ektarr`. Net effect: v11's edge over the old champion doesn't reflect a general improvement, just a shift that happens to beat that one specific opponent's specific weaknesses. Real follow-up implication: the promotion gate itself should probably include a fixed out-of-sample opponent check (e.g. `chaitanyajamble`/`ektarr`, excluded from every search's own `--opponents` pool) before writing to `best_config.json`, not just the candidate-vs-old-champion self-play match -- not implemented yet, flagging as a real gap in `optimize.py`'s `verify_candidate` rather than a one-off mistake.

## 2026-08-22 — implemented cash-flow lookahead ("MPC-lite"); 6-episode result is real but genuinely mixed, opponent-dependent
- Command: implementation of `guaranteed_income`/`_cushion()` in `_market_orders` (`src/agent.py`), motivated by an external second-opinion review's proposal to let projected near-term income (not just current liquid cash) satisfy the reserve cushion -- the same "genuine forward-looking spending plan" idea already flagged as untested in this file's "Spending posture" section, and the same "MPC / multi-day lookahead" approach recommended earlier in this session's own chat log. The pasted proposal's example code referenced fields that don't exist in this game's actual data model (`crop_spec["steps"]`, `tile.get("age")` -- real fields are `max_yield_day`, `planted_day`) and wasn't implemented as literal code; instead reused `_opponent_profile` (already correct, already tested, generic over "a farm dict") called on our own farm, avoiding a second hand-written tile scan entirely. New `income_lookahead_days`/`income_discount` config keys; the guaranteed-income estimate offsets (never eliminates) the existing land/animal/hire reserve cushions, applied uniformly via a small `_cushion()` helper -- a purchase still always needs `remaining >= cost` regardless of projected income.
- Result: `.claude/scratch/ablate_cash_flow_lookahead.py`, 6 episodes each vs kawa/prvsiyan, `income_discount` 0 (off) vs 0.7 (on, `income_lookahead_days=2`). **Real but opponent-dependent effect, not a clean win**: vs kawa, margin improved -127,922.5 -> -112,209.2 (~12% better); vs prvsiyan, margin worsened -110,146.8 -> -119,235.0 (~8% worse). Both still 0W-6L either way -- no win flipped in either matchup.
- Notes: kept in the code (correctly implemented, verified via trace and this ablation to compute and apply as designed) with the reasoned defaults (`income_discount=0.7`, `income_lookahead_days=2`) left as-is -- not promoted to `best_config.json`, and the mixed direction isn't strong enough evidence to hand-tune the default off a single 6-episode read either way. Same pattern already seen once this session with `opponent_race_discount`: a single fixed value isn't universally right across opponents, which is what eventually motivated making that mechanism opponent-adaptive rather than a flat constant. `income_discount`/`income_lookahead_days` are real Optuna-searchable knobs now (`optimize.py`), so a proper search (or a similar opponent-adaptive treatment) is the honest next step rather than another hand-picked guess.

## 2026-08-22 — v10 shadow-pricing search: labor shadow price converges near-zero (search effectively disables it); land shadow price converges high; animals still not chosen
- Command: `robust_agent_config_v10_shadow_pricing` search, identical setup to `v9_solo_optimize` (`--opponents pass,pass,pass,pass`, 500 trials, n_jobs=6) run immediately after implementing shadow pricing (`lambda_labor`/`lambda_land` in `_crop_score`/`_animal_score`/`_plan_units`, see the implementation entry above) -- a deliberate controlled comparison to test the specific hypothesis (from an external second-opinion review, see `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md`/chat log) that correctly pricing labor scarcity into crop scoring would make animals naturally competitive with crops, without needing `animal_enabled` hardcoded.
- Result: **hypothesis not confirmed.** The best trial has `animal_enabled: false`, same as v9 -- and critically, `lambda_labor` converged to **0.45** out of an allowed [0, 15] range, with every other high-scoring trial shown clustering in the same 0.43-0.53 band. Given the search was completely free to set this coefficient to whatever value would help, converging near-zero is the search's own way of saying "pricing labor this way doesn't make animals worth it" -- not an artifact of a narrow search range. `lambda_land`, by contrast, converged high (24.9, near the top of its [0, 80] range) -- land-scarcity shadow pricing carries real value independent of the animal question.
- Notes: dedicated verification improved from v9's 2W-8L (avg margin -1,666.5) to **4W-6L-0T (avg margin +288.4)** -- real progress, still short of promotion (`best_config.json` unchanged). Net read: keep land shadow pricing, but the specific "price labor correctly and animals will naturally win" theory doesn't hold up empirically here. Best guess why: shadow pricing only affects the crop/animal *scoring* used to decide what to plant -- it doesn't touch the deeper structural gap already established this session (`docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`, "Dispatch / routing efficiency"), which is that the animal pipeline (build -> buy -> place -> feed -> care -> collect, six separate dispatch-tier steps, each competing independently for the same scarce labor) is mechanically more fragile to execute reliably than crops' simpler plant -> water -> harvest chain, regardless of how correctly the *decision to plant* is priced. Pricing the input decision correctly doesn't fix a bottleneck in the output execution.

## 2026-08-22 — v9 solo-optimization search: pure-output optimum disables animals entirely; directly contradicts a pasted external report's "Pure Animal Pivot" recommendation
- Command: analysis of the v9 search below, prompted by a user-pasted diagnostic report (from an unspecified other LLM) recommending a structural rewrite of `agent.py` to force early pasture-building and override reserve thresholds for animal purchases ("empty pasture is a wasted asset").
- Result: **the search's own best trial has `"animal_enabled": false`** -- when optimized purely for our own absolute economic output against a passive opponent (no market competition, no opponent behavior to react to), the discovered local optimum skips animals entirely and goes all-in on crops. This directly contradicts the pasted report's premise that forcing more animals/pastures would help -- even with zero opponent interference to blame, our current execution/dispatch pipeline still doesn't make animals pay off over crops.
- Notes: this doesn't mean animals are worthless in general -- it means the *current* agent's animal pipeline (build -> buy -> feed -> care -> collect, competing for the same scarce labor as crops) isn't yet efficient enough to beat crops even in the easiest possible setting. Combined with the same-day "highly favor animal buying" test (see below/`IDEAS_TRIED.md`, 9th documented regression from over-weighting animals) and this search's own failure to beat the real champion (2W-8L, see the auto-logged entry immediately below), two independent experiments today pushing in opposite directions (more animals vs. zero animals) both moved away from the current champion's balance and both underperformed it. That's real triangulating evidence the current crop/animal balance, while unverified as optimal, isn't obviously wrong -- the pasted report's recommendation to hand-rewrite `agent.py` toward a pure-animal strategy is not supported by this project's own evidence and was not implemented.

## 2026-08-22 — tested "highly favor animal buying" (max_structures 4->14, animal_reserve_multiple 2.6->0.3) under the new reserve architecture: regressed, reverted
- Command: `.claude/scratch/ablate_animal_favor.py`, 6 episodes each vs kawa/prvsiyan, alternating seats -- explicit user request to test whether loosening the animal-purchase gate now pays off given today's reserve-architecture rework, following a 1-episode ad hoc test the turn before that looked ambiguous and was correctly flagged as too small a sample to trust.
- Result: **regressed against both opponents, consistently.** vs kawa: -121,143.5 (old) -> -137,542.2 (new). vs prvsiyan: -105,757.0 (old) -> -121,015.5 (new). Both still 0W-6L either way -- no win flipped -- but every single one of the 12 new-config episodes had a worse margin than the corresponding old-config baseline, not a mixed bag.
- Notes: reverted `best_config.json` back to `max_structures=4`, `animal_reserve_multiple=2.595799954753388`. This is the ninth documented "spend/expand more aggressively" intervention this session that regressed (see `docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`, "Spending posture") -- notable that it regressed even under today's corrected reserve architecture (proportional-to-cost cushions, not a dominant flat buffer), which was itself partly motivated by the hypothesis that the old architecture was artificially suppressing beneficial aggression. It wasn't -- loosening the animal gate specifically still doesn't pay off, architecture fix or not. The standing diagnosis (our dispatch/execution layer can't yet productively absorb faster capital deployment, not that the deployment itself is the reason kawa/prvsiyan pull ahead) holds up again.

## 2026-08-22 — diagnostic run before launching v8 training found a real, independent bug: pasture starved by its own dispatch-priority weight; hand-fixed and verified
- Command: `python src/run_match.py submission/main.py:submission_agent kawa_route_agent:kawa_route_agent` plus `.claude/scratch/compare_strong_opponents.py`, run per the user's explicit request to inspect behavior before spending compute on a v8 search.
- Result: **pasture count was stuck at 2 for 24 straight days** in both kawa/prvsiyan matches (target should already be 4 from day 0, since `BUILD_PASTURE` is free and `max_structures=4`/`pasture_target_ratio` justify it immediately). Root cause: `priority_weight_empty_build=15.2` was one of the two lowest of 11 dispatch-priority weights (only `empty_coop_place=6.4` was lower), ranked behind harvest/collect_fertilizer/empty_plant/weeds/care/feed/fertilize -- with that many higher-priority crop-tending tasks competing for the same labor every turn, `BUILD_PASTURE` essentially never got a turn. **Confirmed independent of the same-day reserve-architecture rework** via `git stash` -- the identical symptom (pasture stuck at 2, hands flat at daily cap, animals near 0) reproduced with the pre-rework code too. Also found `priority_weight_empty_pasture_place=14.1` (placing a bought animal onto a built pasture, the very next pipeline step) was just as starved.
- Fix: hand-raised both in `best_config.json` -- `priority_weight_empty_build` 15.2 -> 65.0 (matching `priority_weight_empty_plant`'s rank, its land-claiming sibling), `priority_weight_empty_pasture_place` 14.1 -> 55.0. Re-verified with the same trace: **pasture now reaches its target of 4 and holds it**, instead of stalling at 2 for most of the game -- confirmed real, not a fluke (both kawa and prvsiyan matches show the fix).
- Notes: animal count is still low (peaks at 2, crashes to 0 late) even with pasture now correctly built -- but this is now attributable to `animal_reserve_multiple=2.6`, a value searched *before* today's reserve-architecture rework when it sat subordinate to a dominant flat buffer and barely mattered. Under the new `max(floor, multiple * risk_scale * cost)` design it's the entire purchase gate, and it's now genuinely too conservative at that stale value -- exactly the kind of thing the pending v8 search is supposed to re-discover, not something to hand-guess further. `priority_weight_empty_build`'s starvation, by contrast, was independently confirmed as a real bug (not a stale-value artifact) via the git-stash isolation test, so hand-fixing it rather than waiting for a search was the right call. `hires_today` flat at the daily cap (6) every single day was initially mistaken for a hiring bug during this investigation -- `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` line 132 clarifies hands disappear at day end and must be re-hired fresh each morning, so hitting the daily cap every day is expected, not broken.

## 2026-08-22 — rebuilt the reserve/risk-scale architecture: reserve requirements now scale with purchase cost, not a flat buffer
- Command: structural rewrite of `_market_orders`'s land/animal/hire spending gates in `src/agent.py`, motivated directly by the user's "purge these parameters, the flat reserve kills the whole idea of risk analysis" critique and grounded in a real competitor's published formula (`pilkwang`'s `Structured Economic Policy` notebook, `max(floor, multiple * marginal_cost)` reserve shape -- see `docs/COMPETITOR_STRATEGY_NOTES.md`).
- Change: every gate (`BUY_LAND`, `BUY_ANIMAL` for GOOSE/COW/SHEEP, `HIRE`) now requires `remaining >= cost + max(fixed_floor, reserve_multiple * risk_scale * cost)` instead of the old `remaining - money_reserve*risk_scale >= cost * multiple * risk_scale` (a flat buffer *added to* a proportional term, rather than the two being combined via `max`). `money_reserve` is repurposed from the dominant term (champion-tuned to 420, more cash than the strong-opponent benchmark's entire early-game bank) into a small, deliberately-unscaled survival floor -- default lowered to 40, search bounds narrowed from (20,500) to (10,150) to match `hire_money_floor`'s scale. New `land_reserve_multiple` knob gives land its own cost-scaled reserve (it previously had none at all, just the flat buffer). `hire_money_floor` also de-scaled from `risk_scale` (was scaled before, now fixed, matching `seed_money_floor`'s existing precedent).
- Result: verified via `.claude/scratch/trace_reserve_redesign.py` (instruments the live formula against a real kawa match, current champion config as the base). Confirmed the stale `money_reserve=420` floor never binds for a real purchase -- animal cushion is always >=$920, land cushion always >=$1,329, both comfortably above the floor, so the `max()` correctly picks the proportional term every time. Confirmed the fix actually restores `risk_scale`'s effect size: the same `risk_scale` range this session already had (0.89-2.35) now swings the animal-purchase cushion from $920 to $1,608 (~75%) across one match, versus the old design where the same range moved the total by only ~11% (the flat 420 swallowed the rest of the signal). `submission/main.py` rebuilds and runs clean, no exceptions.
- Notes: this is a mechanism-correctness fix, not yet a verified win -- `best_config.json`'s `money_reserve=420` and missing `land_reserve_multiple` (falls back to the new default, 1.5, via `build_submission.py`'s `DEFAULT_CONFIG` merge) are now running under new semantics they were never tuned for. Mechanically safe (confirmed above), but a fresh search is needed to actually find good values under the new formula shape, not just avoid breaking anything. Optuna study renamed (v7 -> v8, `robust_agent_config_v8_reserve_rework`) since both the search-space shape (`land_reserve_multiple` added) and two existing keys' (`money_reserve`, `hire_money_floor`) real-world meaning changed -- same MedianPruner-contamination reasoning as every previous rename this session.
