## 2026-08-22 — infrastructure finding: 8-way concurrent Optuna workers hit SQLite lock contention, lost ~half the requested trial budget
- Command: n/a -- observed while checking on the 1000-trial `v6` continuation run (PID 413216, see the search entry directly below).
- Result: the log contains multiple `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` tracebacks (surfaced through SQLAlchemy/Optuna's `StorageInternalError`). Trial numbering only reached ~910 (up from the study's starting count of 400) despite `--n-trials 1000` -- roughly half the requested trials never ran, because one or more of the 8 worker processes crashed on a locked-database write instead of completing their allocated share. The run still finished and produced an honest final summary/verification from whatever trials did survive (1W-9L, did not beat champion) -- no data corruption, just reduced throughput.
- Notes: `optimize.py`'s `DEFAULT_STORAGE` is a plain `sqlite:///...` URL with 8 concurrent worker processes hammering it -- SQLite's single-writer model isn't built for that level of concurrent write contention under sustained load. Worth a real fix later (e.g. a WAL-mode connection string, a retry/backoff wrapper around the commit, or a lower default `--n-jobs`) rather than accepting silently-lost trial budget on every large parallel run. Not fixed this pass -- flagged for the config/infrastructure redesign already tracked in `docs/CONFIG_INFRASTRUCTURE_NOTES.md`.

## 2026-08-21 — v5 champion (real promotion, risk_sensitivity near its max) verified against near-tier opponents: clean sweep vs rajan1673, regressed vs chaitanyajamble
- Command: `.claude/scratch/verify_v5_champion.py` -- 10 episodes each, alternating seats, against `rajan1673_agent` and `chaitanyajamble_agent` (neither is in `optimize.py`'s own opponent pool). Follow-up to the v5 search below given this session's own recent lesson about trusting small/narrow samples.
- Result: **rajan1673: 10W-0L, avg margin +19,782.2** -- unaffected, still a clean sweep. **chaitanyajamble: 0W-10L, avg margin -13,621.5** -- a real regression from the pre-v5 baseline (2W-8L, avg -6,990.3, see the "built a relative-wealth" entry below).
- Notes: the promotion itself is legitimate by this project's own standing gate (10-episode dedicated verification vs the specific prior champion, wins > losses -- see the v5 search entry above), and this isn't the earlier 4-episode-noise trap since it used the project's standard sample size throughout. But it's a real trade-off, not a clean win: `risk_sensitivity` landed at 1.997 (its search ceiling of 2.0), and the resulting more-reactive spending helps against the champion/10-opponent pool this was searched over but hurts specifically against chaitanyajamble, an opponent that pool doesn't include at all. Net effect depends on which opponents matter most going forward -- kept the promotion for now (matches project convention: the dedicated gate is the actual promotion mechanism, not a hand pick), but flagging this for a decision rather than treating it as settled.

## 2026-08-21 — built a relative-wealth (CPPI/tournament-theory) risk-scale mechanism; a 4-episode "win" evaporated at 10 episodes, shipped as a default no-op
- Command: implemented `_standing_asset_value` + a `risk_scale` multiplier on every reserve gate in `_market_orders` (`src/agent.py`), wired through `train_rl.py`/`build_submission.py`. User's proposal: since only win/loss matters, not margin (`docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`), the agent should get more conservative once ahead of the opponent's total wealth and more aggressive once behind. Researched the finance literature first (user request): this is CPPI (`cushion = value - floor`, `risky_allocation = multiplier * cushion`) with the floor replaced by the opponent's total value, and "tournament theory"/"gambling for resurrection" (trailing funds increase risk, leading funds reduce it) from the mutual-fund-manager literature -- both real, citable frameworks, not invented from scratch. First implementation used `_opponent_profile`'s existing `supply` dict (only near-ripe yield) for the "unrealized" half of value -- user caught that this misses all sunk capital in freshly-planted crops/just-bought animals, so a cash-poor-but-asset-rich opponent (exactly kawa/prvsiyan's early-game shape) would look artificially poor. Replaced with `_standing_asset_value`, which values every standing crop at full expected yield and every live animal at its purchase cost, regardless of growth/readiness stage.
- Result: verified the mechanism computes correctly (direct trace: `risk_scale` rises above 1.0 while ahead, drops to its floor while behind, opponent value now visible from day 1 instead of only near-harvest). But the win-rate ablation told a different story depending on sample size. **At 4 episodes/opponent**, a symmetric version (`risk_scale_min=0.4`, loosens when behind) regressed against kawa/prvsiyan (consistent with 8 prior "spend more when behind" failures, `IDEAS_TRIED.md`); an asymmetric version (`risk_scale_min=1.0`, only ever tightens when ahead) looked like a real win against two closer-tier opponents: rajan1673 3W-1L->4W-0L, chaitanyajamble 1W-3L->2W-2L, total win count 4/8->6/8. **At 10 episodes/opponent, that result evaporated**: rajan1673 was already a clean 10W-0L at baseline -- the mechanism made it 9W-1L (a regression); chaitanyajamble was an unchanged 2W-8L either way (avg margin -6990 vs -7948, within noise). The 4-episode "win" was noise, not signal.
- Notes: kept the mechanism as infrastructure (code is correct and does what it says), but reverted the hand-picked `risk_sensitivity=0.5`/`risk_scale_min=1.0` "verified" defaults -- `risk_sensitivity` now defaults to `0.0`, a structural no-op (confirmed: submission runs identically to pre-mechanism baseline). This is a real methodology lesson worth generalizing, not just about this one feature: a 4-episode/opponent sample flipped a losing comparison into a "clean win" that a 10-episode sample fully reversed. Every other verified change this session used 6+ episodes minimum; this one didn't, and got burned for it. Left `wealth_margin_scale`/`risk_sensitivity`/`risk_scale_min`/`risk_scale_max` in `optimize.py`'s search space (`risk_scale_min` bounded to [0.7, 1.5], not the full symmetric range, given the separate 8-episode kawa/prvsiyan evidence against going lower) so a real Optuna search with the dedicated multi-episode verification gate gets a chance to find actual support, if any exists, instead of another hand-picked guess.

---

## 2026-08-21 — MAJOR REFRAME: kawa_route_agent and prvsiyan_frontier_agent are pre-solved fixed-route scripts, not live reactive strategies
- Command: read `src/opponents/kawa_route_agent.py` and `src/opponents/prvsiyan_frontier_agent.py` source directly (both are real public Kaggle notebooks, publicly shareable per `docs/RULES.md` §3.5), prompted by the isolated `max_structures` test below reconfirming the same "shell-outrunning-animals" regression this session has hit 8 times against these two specific opponents.
- Result: **both are scripted route-players, not adaptive heuristics.** `kawa_route_agent.py`'s `_kawa_actions(obs)` (line 139) picks one of 5 giant hardcoded per-step action tables (`_ACTIONS_10C4S_3Q`, `_ACTIONS_8C6S_3Q`, etc.) keyed off which shop unlocks first, and just plays that fixed sequence back -- with a handful of small live patches on top (weed-avoidance detours via `_weed_repair_action`, a feed-timing guard, market-crash-aware sell reordering, and a coarse opponent-layout-family switch at step 24-72). `prvsiyan_frontier_agent.py`'s own docstring says outright: *"Soil modal route with exact RC5 observation-driven weed repair"* and it literally reuses `_kawa_actions` under a renamed key (`_MOON_TERMINAL_NS["_kawa_actions"] = lambda obs: _MODAL_NS["_ROUTE"]`, line 1822) -- it's built on the same fixed-route technique, not an independently-derived live strategy.
- Notes: this reframes the entire "why can't we match their animal/pasture pacing" investigation that's driven most of this session's spending-posture experiments. These two agents aren't solving the live turn-by-turn decision problem at all -- they're replaying an answer key computed offline with full foresight of the whole 30-day game (deterministic starting layout + market curve), which is exactly why their cash-burn schedule looks "perfectly" tuned and why 8 straight documented attempts to imitate it via reactive reserve/threshold tuning (`IDEAS_TRIED.md`, "Spending posture" section) all regressed -- a live heuristic structurally cannot replicate a pre-solved schedule's efficiency by tuning thresholds harder. Doesn't mean these opponents are unbeatable or that the benchmark is worthless (beating a solved script with a good-enough live heuristic is still a real, useful signal), but it does mean their exact animal/pasture curve is the wrong specific target to chase -- worth weighting benchmark comparisons more toward the near-tier opponents found earlier this session (`rajan1673_agent`, `chaitanyajamble_agent`, genuinely live/reactive, closer to our own live rating) for near-term decisions, and treating the kawa/prvsiyan gap as a longer-run stretch goal rather than a bug to hunt down. Updated `docs/tests/IDEAS_TRIED.md`.

## 2026-08-21 — isolated test: raising max_structures alone (4->16) still regresses post-dispatch-fixes, confirming the animal-lag problem is unchanged
- Command: `.claude/scratch/isolated_max_structures_test.py` -- calls `agent.make_agent(config)` directly (not the built submission) with `best_config.json` as base, single knob changed (`max_structures` 4->16), vs kawa and prvsiyan, one episode each. Motivated by the user re-flagging "the same animal problem stands" and asking to run games and check mechanism-level, not rerun Optuna blindly.
- Result: confirms two things. (1) **`best_config.json`'s `max_structures: 4` is a live bug**: `actions.csv` from a fresh `run_match.py` game shows our submission does exactly 7 `BUILD_PASTURE` actions, all on day 0, then **zero more for the entire 29-day game** -- `4` is below the farm's day-0 starting pasture count (7), so `target_structures = min(max_structures, ...)` is satisfied by the starting tiles alone from turn 1 onward, permanently blocking new pasture regardless of land/cash available (`src/agent.py:490-493`). Matches `PLACE` (animal) unit-turn counts: 19 for us vs kawa's 45. (2) **Raising it alone still regresses**, even with this session's other fixes (dispatch rewrite, CARE reorder, stale-money fix) in place: final money 32,327->14,078 vs kawa, 37,445->13,677 vs prvsiyan. Pasture only grew to 9-11 (not close to kawa's 14) while animal count oscillated 5-11 and never converged to fill the new slots -- new pasture shells sit unstocked, permanently displacing crop tiles that were producing income, for a net loss. This is the same "12 pasture / 5 animals" shell-outrunning-animals pattern documented earlier this session (2026-08-21 "fixed a real bug (pasture target gated on today's headcount)"), now reconfirmed to persist even after later dispatch-efficiency fixes -- rules out "it was just bad dispatch before" as the explanation.
- Notes: `max_structures: 4` in `best_config.json` is real, live, stale, and independently worth fixing (it's not being used as intended by the already-fixed formula) -- but the fix isn't as simple as raising it in isolation, per this test and the prior session's identical finding. See the entry above for why: kawa/prvsiyan's own pacing (the thing we'd be tuning toward) turns out to be a pre-solved route, not a target a reactive threshold can reliably hit. Did not change `best_config.json` -- left at its current (buggy but not regression-tested-worse) value pending a decision on direction.

## 2026-08-21 — ablation: opponent_race_discount's optimal value is opponent-dependent, not universal
- Command: 4-episode alternating-seat check of 3 configs (baseline: current tuned `opponent_incoming_threshold=4`/`opponent_race_discount=0.54`; `aware_off`: `opponent_awareness_enabled=False`; `aggressive`: `threshold=1`/`discount=0.3`/`lookahead=5`) vs kawa and vs prvsiyan separately -- prompted by the user's question "what opponent affects my game."
- Result: **the ranking flips by opponent.** vs kawa: baseline best (-112,922), aggressive worst (-125,124). vs prvsiyan: aggressive best (-109,815), baseline worst (-134,521) -- even `aware_off` (-121,785) beat baseline against prvsiyan. Swings are 15-25k in both directions, consistent enough across each opponent's own 4-episode set to trust the direction, not just noise.
- Notes: confirms the game really is opponent-dependent in a way a single static config can't resolve (echoes the discussion-forum "may be non-transitive" finding from earlier this session) -- whatever value Optuna converges on for this knob will always be a compromise, right for some opponents and wrong for others, not a universal optimum. Motivated the dynamic mechanism in the next entry.

## 2026-08-21 — made opponent_race_discount opponent-adaptive: scale it by the opponent's observed concentration and scale, not one fixed number
- Command: extended the existing opponent-tile scan (`_opponent_incoming_supply`, renamed `_opponent_profile`) to also compute, from the opponent's *public* tiles/land/labor only (never anything private): `concentration` (fraction of their occupied tiles growing/producing each item -- an opponent running 80% one crop has a much bigger stake in it than one running an even split) and `scale` (0-1, their overall land+labor size, normalized against this session's traced strong opponents' peak of ~4 quadrants/~14 hands). `_market_orders`' race-discount is now `opponent_race_discount - opponent_concentration_sensitivity * (concentration * scale)`, clamped to [0.1, 1.0], instead of the flat `opponent_race_discount` alone. New `opponent_concentration_sensitivity` config key (default 0.3), added to `KNOB_SPECS` and `optimize.py`'s search space. Updated all 3 call sites (`agent.py`, `train_rl.py`, `build_submission.py`'s generated template) and `ROBUST_AGENT_FUNCS`.
- Result: verified mechanically sound (rebuilt submission runs clean, no exceptions). Effect vs the two opponents from the ablation above: **kawa -116,930 (between baseline's -112,922 and aggressive's -125,124), prvsiyan -125,889 (between aggressive's -109,815 and baseline's -134,521)** -- exactly the signature expected of a working adaptive mechanism: it lands in between the two static extremes for both opponents instead of matching either one's best case, avoiding each one's worst case instead. Spot-checked 3 more pool opponents (boatlee_v16 -111,559, saiteja -107,605, romanrozen -123,119) -- all inside this session's usual range, no catastrophic misfire anywhere.
- Notes: kept -- this is a genuinely new capability (opponent-adaptive behavior from only publicly-observable state), not just another static-value guess, and it did what it was designed to do: trade away either opponent's individual best case for more consistent performance across different opponents. Whether that trade is net-positive across the *full* 9-opponent pool (not just the 5 spot-checked here) and whether `opponent_concentration_sensitivity=0.3` is the right default are both open -- the new knob is in the Optuna search space to find out. Doesn't extend to any other config knob yet (hire/land/animal spending is still static) -- this session's broader finding remains that *execution capacity*, not any single reactive parameter, is still the dominant gap to the 2500+ pool.

## 2026-08-21 — made _plan_units' dispatch tier order a tunable hyperparameter (11 new priority_weight_* knobs)
- Command: user question, prompted by the CARE fix two entries back ("this was priority tier 12 -- can't we make priority dynamic, this is a hyperparameter-based model, maybe this opens ground"). Refactored `_plan_units`' 9 shared-contention batch tiers (feed/care/harvest/fertilize/collect_fertilizer/water/empty_coop_place/empty_pasture_place/weeds) plus the 2 land tiers (empty_build/empty_plant, which share `info["empty"]` with each other) into named runner functions in a registry, executed in `sorted(tiers, key=lambda name: -config[f"priority_weight_{name}"])` order instead of a fixed call sequence. Added 11 new flat `priority_weight_<tier>` config keys (matching how every other knob here works, not a nested dict, so `KNOB_SPECS`/`optimize.py`'s search space pick them up with no special-casing), defaults reproducing the exact hand-tuned order this function used before this change. Shed-pickup tiers and the final drop/pass fallback stay structurally fixed (per-unit prerequisites, not tasks competing for a turn's labor the same way). Added all 11 to `KNOB_SPECS` (RL) and `optimize.py`'s `sample_config` (Optuna), generated from a new `PRIORITY_TIER_NAMES` constant so the three lists (dispatch code, KNOB_SPECS, Optuna) can't drift out of sync.
- Result: confirmed behavior-preserving at default weights -- `actions.csv` from a fresh match shows CARE/FEED/WATER/HARVEST rates matching the pre-refactor numbers within normal run-to-run variance (CARE 1.7% vs 1.5%, etc.), and a 6-episode verification landed within noise of the pre-refactor baseline (kawa -119,661 -> -120,452, prvsiyan -117,108 -> -117,416).
- Notes: this is infrastructure, not a strategy change by itself -- nothing is different yet until the weights actually get searched. `KNOB_SPECS` grew from 13 to 24 entries (`optimize.py`'s search space likewise) -- a real tradeoff worth flagging: this session's Optuna searches were already showing signs of plateauing/struggling in the smaller 13-22-dim space (4 full 150-trial searches, best verification 2W-8L, see `IDEAS_TRIED.md`), and nearly doubling the dimensionality with the same trial budget will explore each one more sparsely, not less. Worth either a larger trial budget for the next search, or narrowing which of the 11 new knobs actually get searched (e.g. fix the shed-pickup-adjacent ones and only vary feed/care/water/harvest, the four that actually showed a real gap in the actions.csv comparison) rather than opening all 11 at once. Not yet run.

## 2026-08-21 — used the new actions.csv log to find and fix a real dispatch-priority gap: CARE was starved 5x relative to kawa
- Command: `python src/run_match.py submission/main.py kawa_route_agent:kawa_route_agent`, then aggregated the resulting `actions.csv` by action verb as a % of each side's own total unit-turns -- first real use of the new per-run action log (see two entries back) for decision-making instead of an ad hoc script.
- Result: CARE was **0.9% of our unit-turns vs kawa's 4.6%** -- a 5.1x gap, the largest of any action category (HARVEST 2.4% vs 5.7%, FEED 1.8% vs 4.6%, COLLECT_FERTILIZER 2.3% vs 4.4% were all real but smaller gaps). Root cause: CARE sat at priority tier 12 of ~16 in `_plan_units`, behind feed/harvest/fertilize/collect_fertilizer/water/several pickup-and-placement tiers -- with the persistent labor shortage established all session, it was chronically starved. Moved it to right after FEED (paired same-animal maintenance, and CARE banks a real yield bonus per `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` every day it lands), kept behind harvest/fertilize/collect_fertilizer/water since those have real decay/urgency consequences CARE doesn't.
- Verified: CARE's share rose 0.9% -> 1.5% in a fresh single-match check (now tracking close to FEED's rate, as expected for paired maintenance). 6-episode alternating-seat verification: kawa -123,737 -> -119,661, prvsiyan -117,108 -> -112,221 -- both slightly better, no regression, though modest enough to sit inside this game's usual noise band (individual margins still span -83k to -153k).
- Notes: kept the fix -- real mechanism, verified working as intended, no downside. This is the first time `actions.csv` (added this session specifically to stop re-deriving per-turn action breakdowns by hand) was used end-to-end for a real decision -- found a concrete, previously-undiagnosed gap in about two minutes of aggregation instead of writing a new instrumentation script.

## 2026-08-21 — run_match.py now saves a continuous per-turn action log by default (actions.csv)
- Command: n/a -- infrastructure change to `src/run_match.py`, smoke-tested with a 48-step run.
- Result: every saved run now includes `actions.csv` in its `output/games/<run>/` folder -- one row per unit (farmer + each hand) per step per player, both players, with position and the exact action taken that turn. Reconstructed entirely from `env.steps` after the run (`.action`/`.observation` already carry everything needed) -- works for any opponent, including the black-box packed ones we don't control the source of.
- Notes: user request -- local test runs were only logging final output (score/status), not the continuous per-turn decisions/movement that this whole session's actual debugging kept needing. This directly replaces a pattern that recurred all session: writing a fresh ad hoc instrumentation script (`tier_instrument.py`, `production_audit.py`, `water_distance_probe.py`, `compare_strong_opponents.py`, `order_count_probe.py`, `full_analysis.py`) every time a "what is each unit actually doing" question came up, each reconstructing largely the same position/action extraction from scratch. Now it's captured for every run automatically -- filter `actions.csv` for whatever the question is instead of re-deriving it. Doesn't replace the deeper instrumentation scripts entirely (those can see WHY our own agent picked an action -- task tier, cost -- which isn't in the raw action log for either player), but covers "what happened, turn by turn, for both sides" for free going forward.

## 2026-08-21 — reverted the sell_backlog_multiple force-sell fix -- it regressed, not improved
- Command: 6-episode alternating-seat verification vs kawa/prvsiyan, same methodology as the prior entry, after adding a "force a sale once shed backlog crosses a multiple of max_sell_chunk, regardless of price" override (motivated by the MILK-accumulation leak found in the production audit two entries back).
- Result: **regressed on both** -- kawa -122,315 -> -146,590, prvsiyan -103,326 -> -131,090 (the same stale-money-fix baseline from the immediately preceding entry). Reverted the override entirely (kept `sell_backlog_multiple` as an inert, unused config key/knob for later); confirmed back to the good state on re-verification (kawa -123,737, prvsiyan -117,108 -- consistent with the pre-override baseline within this game's usual noise band).
- Notes: the reasoning behind the fix was wrong -- unlike crops (which mature/decay on a clock) or the wind-down window (where "later" genuinely doesn't exist), shed inventory of animal products doesn't decay while waiting, so forcing a sale at a crashed price permanently realizes a loss instead of waiting for the market's real, if slow, price recovery. "Some revenue beats zero" doesn't hold when the alternative isn't zero, it's "more revenue, later." The `MILK`-accumulation observation itself was real (see two entries back) but the fix needs to be smarter than a flat backlog trigger -- e.g. only force-sell once actually near a hard cap (shed capacity, or max_held on the source tile) where the alternative genuinely is wasted production, not just "large backlog." Not re-attempted this pass.

## 2026-08-21 — fixed a real stale-money bug (BUY_ANIMAL was last in line for cash every turn); reordered to prioritize animals; small real improvement, and a new leak found
- Command: user flagged (from the fresh replays) that our animal count grows much slower than the opponents', and that land expansion pace (even romanrozen's, one of the stronger opponents) doesn't look like the actual differentiator -- opponents instead hyper-optimize the *current* field with animals and keep every tile filled. Traced `_market_orders`: `money = farm["money"]` was read **once** at the top and every subsequent gate (`BUY_LAND`, `HIRE`, `BUY_ANIMAL`) checked against that same stale snapshot, never accounting for what earlier orders queued *the same turn* had already committed. Since orders are processed in order per turn and `BUY_ANIMAL` was the last block in the function, it was the most likely of the three to lose the race for cash on any turn where multiple things were individually affordable but not combined.
- Fix: added a real running balance (`remaining`, decremented as each order gets queued -- BUY_SEED, BUY_ANIMAL, HIRE, BUY_LAND, BUY_PRODUCT fertilizer/wheat -- sell proceeds not added speculatively, so this is conservative not optimistic) and **moved the animal-purchase block ahead of hire and land** so animals get first claim on available cash each turn, matching the replay-observed "animals/current-field first" pattern. Also fixed a separate, smaller leak found while auditing this (`.claude/scratch/production_audit.py`, previous entry): `FERTILIZER` was never in the `sellable` list at all, so any fertilizer collected past what got used for FERTILIZE just accumulated in the shed unsold for the whole game (1 -> 10 units in the audited run) -- added it to `sellable`.
- Result: 6-episode alternating-seat verification vs kawa/prvsiyan. kawa: -122,315 avg margin (was -125,096 same methodology last test -- flat, within noise). **prvsiyan: -103,326 avg margin (was -122,401 -- ~15% better, directionally real given individual episode margins span -77k to -154k)**. Net: no regression, one real if modest improvement.
- Notes: kept both fixes -- the stale-money bug was real and independent of whether reordering priority helps (a turn queuing unaffordable *combined* orders is wrong regardless). New leak found while re-auditing after this change, not yet fixed: **MILK accumulated heavily unsold in one traced run (3 -> 13 units)** despite being in the `sellable` list -- MILK is a premium item (base $160, `above_target=1.6`, crashes hard on oversupply per `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`'s price table) and once our own selling (or the shared market) craters its price below our `sell_fraction`-based threshold, the sell gate stops firing and it just piles up rather than selling at *some* price. This is a real, plausible mechanism for a genuine leak once animal output scales up (exactly the direction this session is trying to push) -- worth a dedicated look: either a backlog-size override (sell regardless of price once shed quantity crosses some threshold, similar to how wind-down already ignores the threshold entirely) or a smarter price-recovery-aware threshold. Not yet fixed.

## 2026-08-21 — production/sell-side audit: no leak found -- the gap is capacity scale, not execution efficiency
- Command: `.claude/scratch/production_audit.py` (ad hoc) -- ran current `submission/main.py` (unmodified `best_config.json`) vs `kawa_route_agent`, sampling our own private state (only observable for our own side) every day: unwatered-tile count, harvest-ready backlog (crops and animal products separately, using the real `_plant_harvest_ready` condition -- first draft of this script used a naive `yield_units > 0` check and wildly overcounted "ripe" tiles that weren't actually harvestable yet, fixed before trusting the result), feed/care compliance, and shed inventory buildup.
- Result: **production/sell efficiency is essentially clean, not a leak.** Unwatered-tile count stayed at 0-4 all game (near-zero); harvest-ready backlog for both crops and animal products stayed at 0 for the *entire* 29-day game (nothing sits ripe unharvested); shed inventory doesn't pile up except `FERTILIZER` (1 -> 10 units, day 18-28, collected from animals but never sold down -- a real but small leak, ~$1000 of unrealized value at $100 base price). Feed compliance tracks animal count reasonably well most days; care compliance is weaker (several days show 0 cared despite animals present) -- a modest, secondary yield-bonus loss, not a major one.
- Notes: this refutes the standing "production/sell-side gap" hypothesis from the last few entries. The actual constraint is simpler and less interesting than hoped: our capacity itself (this run's `max_structures=4`, animal count topping out at 7, land rarely past 1-2 quadrants) stays small, and *at that small scale* we're already using it about as efficiently as the mechanics allow. Raising capacity is the real lever -- but every isolated attempt to raise it this session backfired (see `IDEAS_TRIED.md`, "Spending posture" section), specifically from cash-flow/capital competition, not from failing to use the extra capacity once built. Reframes the open question: this looks less like "we're bad at running a bigger farm" and more like "our reactive threshold-based spending logic can't find the same tight, non-starving cash-flow schedule that lets kawa's pre-solved replay scale up without ever running out of money." A live heuristic agent replaying fixed reserve/backlog thresholds each turn may just not be able to reproduce that precision -- worth considering a genuine forward-looking spending *schedule* (planned a few days ahead against projected income) rather than more reactive gates, though not attempted this pass. Minor fix worth doing regardless: sell `FERTILIZER` down instead of letting it accumulate unsold.

## 2026-08-21 — fixed a real bug (pasture target gated on today's headcount, not intended labor); loosening the animal-purchase gate on top still regressed
- Command: user watched the 4 saved replays and flagged that kawa/prvsiyan/boatlee_v16/saiteja all "flatten their coins" and build out animal capacity in the first few turns, fully utilized, while our agent holds much more cash early. Traced the code: `target_structures = min(max_structures, ceil(len(units) * pasture_target_ratio * n_quadrants))` -- on day 0, `len(units)=1` (lone farmer, no hires yet), so target caps near 1 *no matter how high `max_structures` is set*, directly contradicting the surrounding comment's own claim that building is free and shouldn't be delayed. Fixed by replacing `len(units)` (today's actual headcount) with `1 + config["max_hires_per_day"]` (our intended near-term labor scale) -- lets a single farmer start building toward the real ceiling immediately instead of waiting for hires that haven't landed yet.
- Result: **the fix itself works as intended** -- tested with `max_structures` raised to 14 (isolated, `best_config.json` otherwise untouched): pasture count jumped to 9 by day 0 (was frozen at 6 all game before any of today's pasture fixes) and reached 12 by day 12, confirmed via `.claude/scratch/compare_strong_opponents.py`. But **overall money was still worse than the unmodified baseline** (14,789 vs ~17,877-33,378 range) -- animal count lagged far behind pasture count (12 pasture, only 5 animals -- 7 empty unstocked shells), meaning building is no longer the bottleneck but affording the animal to fill it still is. Loosening that gate too (`animal_reserve_multiple` 2.18 -> 1.1, same isolated methodology) made it **dramatically worse** (final money 2,461, cash near-zero most of the game) -- buying animals faster just drains cash without whatever compensating income mechanism the real strong opponents have once their infrastructure comes online.
- Notes: kept the `target_structures` formula fix (`agent.py`) -- it's a real, well-evidenced logic bug independent of whether loosening spending helps, and directly matches the user's replay-based diagnosis. Did **not** keep either of the two `best_config.json` overrides tested (`max_structures=14`, `animal_reserve_multiple=1.1`) -- `best_config.json` and `submission/main.py` are back to their pre-test state (max_structures=4, animal_reserve_multiple=2.176), confirmed via diff before rebuilding. This is the seventh "spend/build more aggressively" intervention this session that regressed on its own; the strong opponents' coin curve also flattens early (kawa: $25-1650 through day 8) but then explodes from day 10+ -- our agent doesn't replicate that explosion even when given matching capital posture, which points at the production/sell side (not just spending gates) as the remaining gap.

## 2026-08-21 — clustering-biased empty-tile placement: implemented, no measurable effect either way
- Command: added an optional `cost_fn` to `_assign_nearest` (default unchanged: plain Manhattan distance) and used it for the `empty_build`/`empty_plant` tiers only -- new land now gets claimed against `distance - 2.0 * occupied_neighbor_count` instead of pure distance, biasing toward growing the footprint as a contiguous block (including tiles claimed earlier the same turn, via a `claimed_this_turn` set) rather than pure nearest-available, on the theory (from the water-distance probe below) that trip *count* matters more than trip *distance* once distance is already near-optimal. Verified: `.claude/scratch/water_distance_probe.py` (fixed to pass through the new `cost_fn` param -- it broke on the first run with every turn falling back to PASS, a `TypeError` from an unrelated stale wrapper signature) and a 6-episode alternating-seat verification vs kawa/prvsiyan.
- Result: **no measurable change either way.** Water-tier assignment distance: mean 1.60 (was 1.62), 78% at distance <=2 (unchanged). Margins: kawa avg -125,096 (6 episodes, individual range -81k to -155k) vs an earlier same-methodology baseline of -120,786 to -126,653; prvsiyan avg -122,401 (range -93k to -142k) vs earlier baselines of -89,760 to -112,899 -- both new results sit inside the noise band of prior runs, no clear win or loss.
- Notes: kept the code (harmless -- `cost_fn` defaults to unchanged behavior everywhere else, and this is a real, principled mechanism even if unproven at `CLUSTER_BONUS=2.0`). Two live hypotheses for the null result, not yet tested: (a) the bonus is too weak relative to typical distances (1.6-3.4) to consistently outrank a closer-but-isolated tile, or (b) single-run/6-episode variance (individual margins spanning -81k to -155k against the same opponent) is simply too high to detect a real but modest effect at this sample size -- would need either a stronger bonus or more episodes to tell the difference. Did not pursue further this pass, consistent with this session's practice of reporting null/negative results plainly rather than tuning until something looks good.

## 2026-08-21 — implemented a genuine day-phased "go broke early" mechanic; still regressed
- Command: added `broke_phase_days_frac`/`broke_phase_reserve_scale` to `agent.py` (`DEFAULT_CONFIG` + `_market_orders`) -- a real day-phase toggle (disabled by default, frac=0/scale=1 is a no-op) that scales `money_reserve`, `hire_money_floor`/`hire_reserve_multiple`, and `animal_reserve_multiple` down to `broke_phase_reserve_scale` of normal for the first `broke_phase_days_frac` of the season, then reverts to normal after -- unlike the flat "aggressive" config tested earlier (which lowered reserves for the *whole* game), this one only discounts the early phase and recovers normal caution afterward, matching "go broke early, catch up on margin later" specifically. Tested with `broke_phase_days_frac=0.4`, `broke_phase_reserve_scale=0.1`, `max_structures=14` (animal-favored), full 5-crop diversity (`crops=[WHEAT,CARROT,TOMATO,STRAWBERRY,MELON]`, already the default "diversified" profile). 2 episodes/opponent alternating seats, full 9-opponent pool.
- Result: **regressed again** -- overall avg margin -112,929 (baseline) -> -134,538 (broke-phase), worse on 6/9 opponents (boatlee_v16 -129k->-151k, rayk_c95 -79k->-128k, kaito -88k->-155k, romanrozen -129k->-148k among the largest swings), better only on kawa (-146k->-134k) and tran_hh (-118k->-89k), both modest.
- Notes: this is the **sixth** independent aggressive/expansionary intervention this session that regressed (after max_structures alone, reserved expansion capacity, higher max_hires_per_day alone, the flat aggressive-early config, and now this properly time-phased version), against the same two that helped all session (dispatch rewrite, opponent_awareness_enabled) -- both waste reductions, not spending increases. The phased design was a real, more faithful implementation of the "go broke early, win late" idea (not a straw-man flat version), and it still didn't work -- strong confirmation that the blocker is execution capacity (our dispatcher can't yet turn extra early hires/animals/land into proportional output before the phase reverts), not that the previous test's aggression was permanent/unbounded. The `broke_phase_*` mechanic itself is kept in `agent.py` (default-disabled, zero behavior change for existing configs) since it's a real, reusable lever -- just not a working one with the current dispatch layer. Recommend not pursuing further hand-tuned aggressive-posture variants without first improving dispatch/execution efficiency further.

## 2026-08-21 — tested a hand-built "aggressive early spending" config (low reserves, high max_structures, earlier land): regressed across the whole pool
- Command: built `.claude/scratch/cfg_aggressive.json` from `best_config.json` with `money_reserve` 460->80, `hire_money_floor` 40->10, `hire_reserve_multiple` 4.72->1.2, `hire_backlog_ratio` 2.42->1.2, `max_hires_per_day` 7->8, `animal_reserve_multiple` 2.18->1.1, `max_structures` 4->16, `land_startup_days` 6->2, `startup_days` 2->1, `land_utilization_threshold` 0.63->0.5. Motivation: the ranking only scores the final-day coin count (`docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`: "coin margin doesn't matter, only win/loss"), and the traced top opponents (kawa, prvsiyan) are visibly near-broke through day 6-8 before exploding from day 10+ -- a legal, rules-consistent "spend everything early, compound later" pattern our config's conservative reserves don't attempt. 2 episodes/opponent alternating seats across the full 9-opponent pool.
- Result: **regressed almost everywhere** -- overall avg margin -107,602 (baseline) -> -128,051 (aggressive), worse on 7/9 opponents (boatlee_v16 -117k->-150k, rayk_c95 -116k->-164k, tran_hh -89k->-136k, prvsiyan -116k->-142k among the largest swings), roughly flat on kaito/pilkwang only. Isolated one knob back out (`land_utilization_threshold` restored to baseline 0.6315) to check if premature land-buying was the main culprit -- margin stayed regressed (vs kawa even slightly worse, -139k->-145k; vs prvsiyan partial recovery -148k->-119k but still well below the -90k baseline), so it's not a single-knob problem.
- Notes: this is the **fifth** independent "spend/expand more aggressively" intervention this session that backfired (after max_structures alone, reserved expansion capacity, higher max_hires_per_day alone, and now this combined preset), against only two that helped (the dispatch algorithm rewrite, and turning on `opponent_awareness_enabled`) -- both of which *reduced waste* rather than *increased spending/risk*. The pattern is consistent enough to state plainly: our mechanical/dispatch layer cannot yet productively absorb the same aggressive economic posture kawa/prvsiyan run -- more hands, more land, and more animals bought sooner just front-load more waste (idle units, unreachable land, under-serviced pastures) given the current routing, rather than replicating their efficient execution of that posture. The user's underlying economic reasoning (animals structurally favored, early sacrifice is rules-legal and literally what top opponents do) is correct and confirmed against the game's own rules text and this session's traced data -- but our agent isn't mechanically ready to execute it yet. Closing that execution gap (not further reserve/threshold tuning) is the prerequisite before an aggressive-early posture can pay off here.

## 2026-08-21 — replay review found two real bugs: frozen pasture target, and opponent-awareness disabled by a noisy search
- Command: user watched a saved `output/games/` replay directly and flagged two things: animals bought late, and pasture ("barn") space not expanding when new land unlocks. Verified both against the code and a fresh `.claude/scratch/compare_strong_opponents.py` trace.
- Result:
  1. **Pasture count was frozen at its day-0 starting value (6) for the entire 29-day game, in every match traced**, regardless of unlocking all 3 quadrants (75 tiles). Root cause: `target_structures = min(max_structures, ceil(unit_count * pasture_target_ratio))` in `_plan_units` only scales with labor, never with land -- and `best_config.json`'s `max_structures=4` is *below* the game's starting pasture count, so `structures_committed < target_structures` is never true again after day 0. Fixed by multiplying the formula by `n_quadrants` (unlocked quadrant count, read from `farm["unlocked_quadrants"]`) so the target actually grows as land expands. Confirmed the fix works mechanically: with `max_structures` manually raised to 14 for a test, pasture grew from the frozen 6 to 10 over the game -- but final money *dropped* in that same test (consistent with the earlier `max_structures=16` experiment this session: more pastures means more animal capital cost + shed-pickup logistics competing with hiring/land for the same early cash, and a hand-picked value doesn't yet balance that). Left `max_structures` at its current searched value (4) rather than hand-guessing a third time -- the formula fix changes what values are *worth* searching, so re-running Optuna is the right next step, not another single manual guess.
  2. **`opponent_awareness_enabled` was `false` in the live `best_config.json`** -- a noisy 2-episode-per-trial Optuna search had toggled off a feature (lower our sell threshold when the opponent's public tiles show incoming supply, so we sell into the still-healthy price first) that has no real game-theoretic downside, only a threshold to tune. Hand-set to `true` in `best_config.json` and removed from `optimize.py`'s searchable params entirely (hard-coded `True` in `_suggest_config`) so a future noisy trial can't disable it again. Verified: 4 episodes alternating seats vs kawa (avg margin -137,523 -> -131,644, ~4% better) and vs prvsiyan (-104,274 -> -105,908, within noise) -- a real but modest win, not close to closing the larger gap on its own.
- Notes: both fixes are live in `agent.py`/`optimize.py`/`best_config.json`; `submission/main.py` rebuilt and verified running clean (no exceptions, real actions). Launching a 4th Optuna search now that the pasture-target formula actually means something different (land-scaled, not effectively capped-and-frozen) to find a `max_structures`/`pasture_target_ratio`/`animal_reserve_multiple` combination that captures the pasture-scaling benefit without the capital-competition cost that sank the two manual guesses.

## 2026-08-21 — optimize.py: Optuna search over robust_agent config

## 2026-08-21 — rewrote _plan_units dispatch to global nearest-pair batch matching; real but modest improvement, doesn't close the gap
- Command: replaced the per-unit fixed-processing-order sequential-nearest greedy in `agent.py`'s `_plan_units` with `_assign_nearest`, a helper that repeatedly assigns the globally-closest (unit, tile) pair across all currently-unassigned eligible units and a tier's candidate list, for every priority tier that draws from a shared/contested `info[]` list (feed, harvest, fertilize, collect_fertilizer, water, empty_coop_place, empty_pasture_place, care, weeds, empty_build, empty_plant). Shed-pickup tiers and the sticky-continuation phase are unchanged (no shared-resource contention there, or already correct). Verified 6 episodes alternating seats vs `kawa_route_agent` and `prvsiyan_frontier_agent` (best_config.json unchanged).
- Result: **0W-6L vs both, but avg margin improved** -- kawa -141,382 (single-sample, prior trace) -> -120,786 (6-ep avg); prvsiyan roughly flat (-111,440 -> -112,899). Land/idle trace (`.claude/scratch/compare_strong_opponents.py`) still shows the same shape as before the fix: stuck at 2 quadrants (never reaches 3), idle tiles still 25-31 of 50 most of the game. So the fix measurably reduces wasted travel and lifts final money somewhat (~15% in the head-to-head trace: 29,060 -> 33,378) but does **not** close the larger structural gap -- 7-8 units still can't clear the ~63% occupancy `land_utilization_threshold` needs to unlock a 3rd quadrant, regardless of how efficiently they're routed.
- Also retested `max_hires_per_day=12` (above the current KNOB_SPECS/Optuna bound of 8) now that dispatch is more efficient, on the theory that the earlier documented failure of more hands (see `DEFAULT_CONFIG` comments) might have been an artifact of the *old*, less efficient dispatch rather than a fundamental labor-capacity problem: **also regressed** (kawa -134,757, prvsiyan -118,748 avg margin, both worse than the max_hires_per_day=7 baseline above). Rules that theory out -- more hands still doesn't pay off even with the improved routing, so the gap isn't a dispatch-efficiency-caused hiring reluctance either.
- Bonus fix found while extracting `submission/main.py`: adding `_assign_nearest` to `agent.py` without updating `build_submission.py`'s `ROBUST_AGENT_FUNCS` reproduced the exact same silent-NameError-into-PASS-every-turn bug documented earlier this session for `_dynamic_sell_fraction` -- the second time this bug class has bitten. Added a real automated check this time (`_check_completeness` in `build_submission.py`, called from `build()`) that walks the extracted functions' ASTs for references to other module-level names and errors before writing the submission if anything's missing, instead of relying on remembering to update the list by hand a third time.
- Notes: keeping the dispatch fix -- it's a real, verified improvement with no observed regression, and the build_submission.py completeness check is a genuine safety net regardless of outcome here. But this is now the fourth single-lever intervention this session (max_structures, reserved expansion capacity, dispatch algorithm, hire count) that measurably failed to close the kawa/prvsiyan gap on its own. Not attempted: a fundamentally different crop/land/labor strategy from day 0 (rather than incremental changes to the current one), which would need to be designed and tuned as a cohesive whole rather than as one more knob nudge -- out of scope for this pass. Next planned step per user direction: re-run Optuna against the new dispatch algorithm (the existing best_config.json was tuned against the old one).

## 2026-08-21 — instrumented _plan_units tier usage: watering is 33-35% of all unit-turns; two naive fixes both made it worse
- Command: `.claude/scratch/tier_instrument.py` (ad hoc, monkeypatches `agent._plan_units` with a counting copy, no changes to `agent.py` itself) vs `kawa_route_agent`, counting which priority tier each unit-turn landed in and its "arrived" rate (real action vs still-walking).
- Result: **water is 33.4-35.8% of all unit-turns across runs** (fresh + sticky combined), by far the largest category, with only ~10-42% "arrived" (most water-tier turns are pure travel, not an actual WATER action). `empty_build` (claiming new land for pasture) is essentially never reached: **0.3% of unit-turns**, flat across every config tried below. This confirms the earlier trace finding (kawa/prvsiyan reach quadrant 3, we never do) at the mechanism level: our 7-8 units spend so much of their turns just traveling to water an ever-growing plant footprint that almost none ever reach the bottom of the priority ladder where land-claiming lives.
- Tried, both regressed vs. baseline (best_config.json, final margin +115,460 for baseline i.e. 133143 vs 17683 in that particular run):
  1. **Raised `max_structures` 4->16, `pasture_target_ratio` 0.615->0.6** (shift footprint toward pasture/animal, which needs feed+care instead of daily water): final margin worsened to 154,733 (168770 vs 14037). `pickup_animal` jumped to 7.0% of unit-turns -- the capital cost (~$400-500/animal) and shed-pickup logistics for 16 pastures outweighs the water savings within a 29-day season at this labor level.
  2. **Reserved a fraction of hands (0.15/0.25/0.4) to try empty_build/empty_plant FIRST, before the maintenance ladder**, to guarantee land-claiming labor regardless of maintenance backlog: made things *worse* at every fraction tried (final margin -162,076 to -163,810 vs baseline's -115,460, i.e. a much bigger loss). Root cause: `target_structures` is already capped at `max_structures=4` almost immediately given `pasture_target_ratio`, so the reserved units skip empty_build (already "full") and go straight to empty_plant -- planting *more* crop tiles without adding labor to water them, which increases total water demand rather than relieving it. `empty_plant%` rose from 9.7% to 18-20% and water% rose from 33.4% to 42-48% in lockstep.
- Notes: **both single-lever fixes made things worse, not better** -- reported plainly rather than picking whichever run looked best. This is evidence the current config is a real local optimum for the current labor supply, consistent with two independent Optuna searches (this session) converging to a similar low-`max_structures` region and both still losing 0W-10L in verification, and with `DEFAULT_CONFIG`'s own comment documenting that raising `max_hires_per_day` alone to 12 was tried previously and also lost (idle land, more units walking not working). All three single-lever interventions tried across this session's investigation (more structures, reserved expansion capacity, more hands) fail independently -- the actual bottleneck looks like per-action travel distance/dispatch efficiency itself (most water-tier turns are pure walking, ~10% arrived), not which tier gets priority or how much labor exists. A genuine fix likely needs the dispatch algorithm to reduce travel cost per completed task (e.g. global nearest unit-tile pairing instead of the current fixed-unit-order sequential-nearest greedy), not a config value -- not yet attempted.

## 2026-08-21 — traced submission vs kawa_route_agent and prvsiyan_frontier_agent: found the real bottleneck is dispatch scaling, not hiring config
- Command: `.claude/scratch/compare_strong_opponents.py` (ad hoc, reuses the `full_analysis.py` day-by-day tile/money/hands tracker from an earlier session) -- built `submission/main.py` fresh, ran it vs `kawa_route_agent` and vs `prvsiyan_frontier_agent`, sampling each day's *last* step (not first, to avoid the earlier "hands reset to 0 at day start" sampling bug).
- Result: **final money gap ~5-6x** in both matches (kawa 170,442 vs sub 29,060; prvsiyan 138,023 vs sub 26,583). Root cause chain, in order:
  1. **Hands**: both opponents ramp hired labor to 12-14 hands/day by day ~10 and hold it; our submission is flat at 7 hands/day the entire game (matches `best_config.json`'s `max_hires_per_day=7`, hit as a hard ceiling every single day, not a stall -- hands legitimately reset to 0 at day end per `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` and get re-hired fresh).
  2. **Idle tiles**: with only 8 units (1 farmer + 7 hands) working 50 unlocked tiles, our idle-tile count sits at 50-70% (25-36 of 50) most of the game. Both opponents, despite unlocking *more* land (75 tiles) and running *more* units (13-15), keep idle tiles in the single digits for most of the game -- their dispatch clearly scales with unit count in a way ours doesn't.
  3. **Land expansion stalls at quadrant 2**: opponents reach a 3rd quadrant (75 tiles) by day 10-11; our submission never does, plausibly because `land_utilization_threshold=0.75` never clears when half the current land sits idle from dispatch inefficiency -- a self-reinforcing trap (poor routing -> low utilization -> land gate never opens -> production stays capped -> can't afford more hands/structures either).
  4. **Structures/animals**: opponents run 12-14 pasture structures vs our 5 (`best_config.json`'s `max_structures=4`, near the low end of the knob's usual range) -- a secondary cap on top of the above, but smaller in magnitude than the labor/land gap.
- Notes: this reframes an assumption from earlier in the session -- naively raising `max_hires_per_day` is **not** a free win. `agent.py`'s own `DEFAULT_CONFIG` comments (lines ~94-102) document that this was already tried: local testing at `max_hires_per_day=12` left ~half of all unlocked land permanently idle and *lost* to a 1-hand baseline, because `_plan_units`'s greedy-nearest-tile dispatcher isn't sophisticated enough to keep that many hands productively busy across a wide board. The real gap to kawa/prvsiyan is dispatch/routing sophistication that scales with unit count, not a config value Optuna can find within the current knob set -- consistent with the last two Optuna searches both coming back 0W-10L against this same opponent pool. Closing this gap needs a `_plan_units` algorithm change (e.g. spatial/batch task assignment instead of independent greedy-nearest-tile per unit), not further config search.

## 2026-08-21 — RL training review before relaunch: fixed reward not being opponent-relative, and an opponent-money feature that saturated against real opponents
- Command: code review of `train_rl.py`/`agent.py`'s RL path (no live training run) prompted by "before RL, anything to improve" -- found via re-reading `extract_features`, `make_training_agent`, and cross-referencing `cash_scale`'s value against the money totals seen in the kawa/boatlee_v16/rayk_c95/saiteja/kaito comparison runs from earlier today. Smoke-tested both fixes together (`--hours 0.01 --n-workers 3`) -- ran clean, no exceptions.
- Result: two real fixes, not just tuning.
  1. **Reward was pure own-money day-over-day delta, never opponent-relative** -- but this competition's ranking is Elo-like and only scores win/loss (coin margin doesn't even matter per `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md`), so a day where we grow $500 while the opponent grows $2000 was scored as a *good* day. Changed the reward (in `make_training_agent`'s day-boundary and `finalize`) to `(our_delta - opponent_delta) / REWARD_SCALE` -- still dense per-day credit assignment, but now aligned with what actually wins.
  2. **`opp_cash_frac` (an RL input feature) saturated at 1.0 almost immediately against real opponents.** It reused `cash_scale` (currently 3700 in `best_config.json`) to normalize opponent money -- but that knob is tuned for OUR OWN sell-aggressiveness threshold, not for representing opponent wealth. Kawa alone reaches ~$15k by day 12 and $150k+ by day 29 (today's earlier trace), so this feature was pinned at 1.0 for the vast majority of any game against a strong opponent -- the policy literally couldn't tell "opponent modestly ahead" from "opponent astronomically ahead" for most of the game. Fixed by adding a separate `RL_MONEY_SCALE = 50000.0` constant in `agent.py`, decoupled from `cash_scale`, used only for the RL feature extraction (both `cash_frac` and `opp_cash_frac`).
- Notes: both bugs predate today's new opponent pool but were effectively invisible against melon_maxxer/multi_crop (whose totals stay in the low thousands, well within the old `cash_scale`-based normalization, and where absolute-growth and relative-growth rewards rarely diverged much). They'd have actively worked against training the moment real opponents were added, which is exactly what's about to happen with the next `train_rl.py --resume` run. Not fixed/investigated this pass: whether `REWARD_SCALE = 1000.0` itself still gives PPO-appropriate reward magnitudes now that rewards are margin-deltas between two much bigger economies -- flagged as worth watching in the next run's `policy_loss`/`value_loss` trend rather than pre-emptively guessing a new constant.

Confirms the earlier decision to keep RL out of the submission entirely was correct -- reinforces that closing the kawa gap requires the mechanical/config fixes (expansion thresholds, `max_structures`, dispatch depth), not more RL training against the current opponent pool.

## 2026-08-21 — robust_agent.py: added cross-turn target stickiness to _plan_units, cut movement waste 74.2% -> 69.3%
- Command: action-type histogram script (`.claude/scratch/action_histogram.py`, ad hoc) classifying every farmer/hand action across a full episode (`robust_agent` vs `melon_maxxer`) as MOVE vs a real action, before and after the change. Then `python src/build_submission.py` + `python src/run_match.py submission/main.py melon_maxxer:melon_maxxer` to verify the real submission file (not just the source-level agent) still runs clean.
- Result: MOVE share dropped **74.2% -> 69.3%**, with every productive action category increasing in absolute share (WATER 8.2%->9.4%, PLANT 2.2%->2.4%, HARVEST 1.9%->2.1%, COLLECT_FERTILIZER 1.6%->2.0%, FERTILIZE 1.5%->1.9%, FEED 1.3%->1.7%, CARE 0.6%->0.8%). Single-trial score vs melon_maxxer also moved 35114 -> 37388 (not itself conclusive at n=1, episodes aren't seeded, but consistent with the histogram result).
- Notes: root cause, confirmed via the histogram matching the *original* 2026-08-20 "76% movement" diagnosis almost exactly -- the later footprint-scaling fixes (fewer hands, tighter land thresholds) never actually touched the dispatcher itself, they just shrank the board so the same broken ratio wasted less absolute time. `_plan_units` re-ran its full priority ladder from scratch every single turn with zero memory: a unit several tiles into a multi-turn walk toward (say) a water target would get yanked onto a newly-appeared higher-priority-tier task the instant one appeared anywhere on the board, abandoning the walk and starting over -- a real thrashing pattern, not just "greedy nearest-tile" as originally described. Fixed by adding a `unit_targets` dict (`{{unit_idx: (task_key, target)}}`) threaded through `_plan_units` and persisted in each caller's existing per-episode `state` closure (`robust_agent.make_agent`, `train_rl.py`'s `make_training_agent`, and the generated `submission/main.py` template in `build_submission.py`, which needed its own `state` dict added since it didn't have one at all): once a unit picks a target it's not yet standing on, that commitment survives to the next turn and is only abandoned if the target itself becomes invalid (task completed, tile state changed), not because something else became nominally higher-priority. `WATER` deliberately was *not* demoted below `PLANT` despite the movement problem -- confirmed via `docs/GAME_GUIDE.md` that missing even one day of watering turns a plant into a weed with zero grace period, versus HARVEST's gradual decay -- so the fix targets movement waste specifically, not priority reordering, which could have made things worse. Also caught and fixed a second latent instance of the exact bug class from the entry below: the two new module-level dicts `_plan_units` now depends on (`STATIC_TASK_ACTIONS`, `TASK_INFO_KEY`) aren't `def`s, so `build_submission.py`'s AST-based function extractor couldn't see them; added a parallel `_extract_assigns` extractor and a `ROBUST_AGENT_CONSTANTS` list so this class of drift gets caught structurally instead of by accident. 69.3% is still high -- more gains likely available (e.g. genuine multi-task local routing instead of one-task-at-a-time commitment) but out of scope for this pass.

## 2026-08-21 — full-game tile/quadrant/animal trace vs kawa_route_agent: found the actual levers, not just symptoms
- Command: extended the prior comparison script (`.claude/scratch/full_analysis.py`, ad hoc) to sample every day 0-29 instead of every 3rd day, and to correctly classify tiles by their real encoding (discovered mid-analysis: an unlocked-but-empty tile is a literal `None`, not a dict -- the earlier entry's tile-kind counts undercounted idle land by treating `None` as "not present" instead of "owned and doing nothing").
- Result, three separate confirmed findings:
  1. **Animals capped at 4 all game, and it's a literal config ceiling.** Our submission places its first animal day 3, reaches 4 animals by day 4, and *never grows past 4 for the rest of the 30-day game*. `src/best_config.json` has `"max_structures": 4` -- that's not a strategic choice the agent is making, it's a hard cap. Kawa places its first animal day 1, has 4 within the first day, and scales to **14 animals by day 12**, holding there.
  2. **Expansion is measurably late and incomplete.** Kawa unlocks quadrant 2 by day 7 and quadrant 3 by day 12 (75 tiles total). Our submission doesn't unlock quadrant 2 until **day 14** and *never unlocks quadrant 3 or 4 at all* -- capped at 50 tiles for the entire game while kawa reaches 75.
  3. **Idle-tile fraction jumps from ~single digits to 30-38 out of 50 (66-76%) right after the day-14 expansion**, and stays there through day 28 -- confirms and quantifies last entry's "land sits idle" finding: it's not just slow to expand, the land it does have mostly goes unworked once it has more than ~25 tiles to cover.
  4. Both agents show a sharp collapse in the last sampled day (29): planted-tile count crashes toward 0, idle/weed spike. This lines up with `wind_down_days`/`season_days` liquidation logic that both agents appear to run (stop planting, sell everything before season end) -- looks like deliberate end-of-season behavior in both, not obviously a shared bug, but flagged as unconfirmed.
  5. Separately, during days 0-13 -- before our land-utilization problem even kicks in, land use is comparable to kawa's in this window -- kawa's money already pulls far ahead (day 11: kawa $18,741 vs sub $779). Land utilization alone doesn't explain this early gap; likely animal count (already 12 vs our 2 by day 11) and/or crop/sell strategy differences. Not yet isolated.
- Notes: prioritized, concrete next actions (not yet done): (a) raise `max_structures` in `best_config.json` well above 4 -- trivial config change, `optimize.py`'s search space should also stop treating 4 as anywhere near an effective ceiling if its sampling range tops out too low; (b) loosen `land_utilization_threshold`/`land_startup_days` so quadrant 2+ unlock lands closer to kawa's day-7/day-12 pace; (c) the real hard part, per the previous entry, is still `_plan_units`'s dispatch efficiency -- unlocking faster without fixing that just means more idle tiles sooner, so (a)/(b) should probably follow, not precede, a dispatch-efficiency fix.

## 2026-08-21 — output/ reorg + root-caused why kawa_route_agent beats our submission ~7-9x: land sits idle, not a config/tuning problem
- Command: turn-by-turn comparison script (`.claude/scratch/compare_kawa.py` + `inspect_kinds2.py`, ad hoc, not saved as a reusable tool) re-running `kawa_route_agent` vs `submission/main.py` in-process and sampling `obs["farms"][i]` every few days for money, `unlocked_quadrants`, and tile `kind` counts (PLANT/PASTURE/WEED -- LOCKED/empty tiles don't get a dict entry at all, so "tiles with a kind" is a live-activity count, not a land-ownership count).
- Result: by day 15, kawa has unlocked **all 4 quadrants** and sustains **68 actively-planted tiles**; our submission has unlocked only **2 quadrants** and has just **5 planted tiles** (day 21: 9). Weed count on our side (3-4) is comparable to or exceeds our own planted count at the same turns -- land is going idle/weedy faster than it's being replanted. Money follows directly: kawa compounds from a day-3 cash crash ($62) to **$110,433 by day 27**; our submission crawls to **$18,558** over the same span -- roughly 6x behind and the gap is still widening at that point (final: 132880 vs 20927). Both agents show **zero hired hands** the entire sampled window (day 3-27) -- this gap is not about hiring more hands, since kawa achieves 68 planted tiles with a single farmer and no hands at all.
- Notes: this reconfirms, with hard numbers, the routing-efficiency limitation first flagged in the 2026-08-20 "robust_agent: scaling-strategy redesign" entry ("~76% movement vs ~24% actual harvest/water/plant" from the greedy-nearest-tile dispatcher) -- except now it's clear the ceiling isn't about *hand count* at all, since kawa's canned route (reconstructed from a real player's actual efficient path, not live decision-making) keeps far more land continuously worked using *fewer* units than we do. **The real lever is `_plan_units`'s per-turn task/movement assignment in `robust_agent.py`, not `optimize.py` config tuning** -- Optuna can retune `land_utilization_threshold`/`max_hires_per_day`/etc. all day without touching the actual bottleneck, which is how efficiently each turn's farmer/hand movement converts unlocked land into continuously-planted, continuously-harvested land. Next step (not yet started): investigate whether `_plan_units` is leaving land idle due to task-priority ordering, poor locality in its nearest-tile-first dispatch, or simply not re-planting fast enough after harvest -- worth a dedicated instrumented trace (action-type histogram like the earlier entry did) rather than more Optuna runs.
- Also reorganized on-disk outputs at the user's request: `run_match.py` now saves to `output/games/<timestamp>_<slug>/` (was `docs/tests/runs/`) and also copies a `best_config.json` snapshot into each run folder; `train_rl.py`'s default `--checkpoint-dir` is now `output/training/` (was `src/rl_checkpoints/`); `optimize.py`'s default Optuna storage is now `output/optuna/optuna_study.db` (was `src/optuna_study.db`). All existing files migrated, `.gitignore` updated to a single `output/` rule, all three entry points re-verified working after the move.

## 2026-08-21 — src/opponents/kawa_route_agent.py: pulled a public Kaggle notebook as a local benchmark opponent, and it exposed a huge strategy gap
- Command: `kaggle kernels pull boatlee/v20-adaptive-r1-multi-route-agent`, decoded its base85+zlib-packed `main.py` (SHA-256 verified against the notebook's own hash check), installed as `src/opponents/kawa_route_agent.py`. Then `python src/run_match.py kawa_route_agent:kawa_route_agent random` and both seat orders vs `submission/main.py` (the just-rebuilt, just-verified-5W-1L-better one from the entry below).
- Result: vs random, **164565 vs 0** (our own agent scores ~25000-31000 vs random in prior entries -- ~5-6x higher). Vs our current submission: as P0, **107349 vs 15099**; as P1, **142035 vs 18241** -- decisive wins both seats, ~7-9x our score.
- Notes: `boatlee`'s public notebook ("V20-Adaptive-R1 | Multi-Route Agent") is explicitly *not* original strategy -- its own docstring calls it "a behavioral reconstruction from twelve public traces" of a third-party player ("Kawa"), not that player's hidden source. Mechanically it's a canned-route agent: classify the board into one of 5 known layouts by shop-unlock order, replay a precomputed action sequence turn-by-turn, then patch the result with reactive rules (weed repair, feed guard, room evacuation, debt repayment, sell-slot ranking, season-end liquidation, plus counters aimed at two other specific named opponent bots). This generalizes badly outside its known configurations by construction (exactly the "bad for scaling" read that motivated pulling it) -- but the margin against our current agent is too large to be about scaling edge cases; it reflects a real gap in in-game strategy quality, not just config tuning. Installed **only** as a local sparring/benchmark opponent (`kawa_route_agent` function, matching the `melon_maxxer`/`multi_crop` convention) for `run_match.py` and `train_rl.py`'s opponent pool -- explicitly not part of our own submission, since it's someone else's reconstruction of a third party's play, not our work. Attribution and provenance recorded in the module's own docstring.

## 2026-08-21 — build_submission.py: fixed a real bug that made the live Kaggle submission a no-op, verified the rebuild, added PPO value clipping
- Command: `python src/build_submission.py`, then `python src/run_match.py submission/main.py <opponent>` (real file-path loading, same mechanism `kaggle_environments` uses for an actual submission) vs `melon_maxxer`/`multi_crop`, then a dedicated 6-episode alternating-seat head-to-head: rebuilt `submission/main.py` vs the previously-live submission (recovered from `git show HEAD:submission/main.py`).
- Result: the freshly-rebuilt submission initially **threw `NameError: name '_dynamic_sell_fraction' is not defined` on every single turn** and silently fell back to `SAFE_FALLBACK` (PASS) the entire game -- confirmed root cause: `build_submission.py`'s hardcoded `ROBUST_AGENT_FUNCS` extraction list was never updated when `robust_agent.py` added `_dynamic_sell_fraction()` (the newer `sell_fraction_base/day_weight/cash_weight` scaling logic that replaced the old flat `sell_fraction` config key). Fixed by adding it to the list; a static call-graph check (walk every included function's AST for calls to other locally-defined functions not yet included) now confirms zero missing dependencies. Rebuilt submission verified clean (no swallowed exceptions) vs melon_maxxer (35114 vs 5533) and multi_crop (32977 vs 8027). **Dedicated 6-episode head-to-head vs the previously-live submission: 5W-1L, avg margin +2783.**
- Notes: **the config actually live on Kaggle (submission 55653308, public score 470.4) was already stale** -- it predates the most recent local `optimize.py` promotion (the `sell_fraction_base` schema in `best_config.json`) and had never been rebuilt/resubmitted. Given the 5W-1L verification above, the rebuilt `submission/main.py` is a real, evidence-backed improvement over what's currently live, not just a newer number -- pending the user's explicit go-ahead to actually submit. Also added PPO value-function clipping to `train_rl.py`'s `ppo_update` (standard PPO2/Baselines technique): a live 8h run's post-fix generations (176-185, see previous entry) showed `value_loss` climbing every single generation (1.58 -> 3.30 over 10 generations) alongside shrinking entropy and an already-regressed eval -- classic critic divergence corrupting the GAE advantage estimates the policy update depends on. Clipping the value estimate's per-update movement to `old_value +/- CLIP_EPS` (mirroring the existing policy-ratio clip) directly targets that failure mode; smoke-tested (4 workers, tiny run) with no crash, not yet verified against a full run. Also fixed `run_match.py` to support loading a raw file path (e.g. `submission/main.py`) the same way `kaggle_environments` loads a real submission (exec by path, last callable wins) instead of only Python-style `module:function` imports -- this bug meant the actual submission file had never been verified the way Kaggle actually runs it, only via `make_agent(best_config)` at the source level, which is why the missing-function bug went undetected.

## 2026-08-21 — train_rl.py: found and fixed a real PPO replay-buffer staleness regression during the 8h unattended GPU run
- Command: unattended `python train_rl.py --hours 8 --resume` (RTX 4070 Laptop), monitored via `training_log.csv` every 30 min across several hours and two prior restarts (see gen 1-175).
- Result: `eval_margin` (deterministic policy vs. champion) degraded steadily once the 12,000-episode replay buffer filled — from a best of -4604.8 (gen 125) down to -9229.9 (gen 161), while `mean_margin` on the same stale-mixed batch stayed flat/noisy, masking the regression until dedicated eval checks caught it. Root cause: with `--replay-episodes 12000` and ~200 new episodes/generation, episodes sat in the buffer for ~60 generations (2.5h+) before aging out, so most of every PPO update was importance-sampled against `old_log_probs` from a policy dozens of generations stale — breaking the near-on-policy assumption the clipped surrogate objective depends on.
- Notes: fixed in `src/train_rl.py` — `--replay-episodes` dropped 12000 -> 2000 (~10 generations turnover, ~25-30min lag), `--max-ppo-epochs` 15 -> 8 and `--target-kl` 0.08 -> 0.04 (now feasible again on the smaller buffer without re-triggering the earlier "stuck at epoch 1" problem), and added an elite-retention safeguard: 3 consecutive evals landing >1500 below `best_margin` now auto-reverts the live network+optimizer to `best.pt` and clears the replay buffer, so an unattended run can't keep drifting away from its own best result for hours undetected. Repaired the live checkpoint by hand once (model weights from `best.pt` gen 125, fresh Adam optimizer state, emptied replay buffer, generation counter kept continuous at 175) and relaunched; confirmed clean resume in the log. Known, accepted tradeoff: the smaller buffer means less GPU work per update, so `gpu_util_avg` will likely sit below the originally-requested 65-75% target — correctness of the learning signal took priority over hitting that band. Also built `Kaggriculture_RL_Colab.ipynb` (self-contained, embeds all source via `%%writefile` since the repo is private) to optionally run training on Google Colab in parallel; flagged in the notebook itself that Colab's free-tier ~2 vCPUs will likely bottleneck the CPU-bound rollout collection harder than this machine's 22 cores, so it's additive throughput, not a guaranteed speedup, unless run on a higher-CPU Colab Pro/Pro+ runtime.

## 2026-08-20 — robust_agent.py: scaling-strategy redesign from reverse-engineered #1 player data + submission rebuild
- Command: ad hoc Python match runs (`run_match.py`-equivalent) at each iteration, then `python src/optimize.py --n-trials 80 --n-jobs 8 --opponents melon_maxxer,multi_crop,champion` (see entry below this one for that run's own numbers), then `python src/build_submission.py` + ad hoc verification loading `submission/main.py` by file path.
- Result: iterating from a naive "copy the #1 player's raw numbers" version through several bugfixes down to a moderate-scale version, then letting the (updated) Optuna search tune it: final promoted config beats the previous champion 10W-2L (avg margin +3530), and beats melon_maxxer 27085 vs 4307 / 31357 vs 5580 and multi_crop 34159 vs 8590 / 30266 vs 7412 (two runs each). `submission/main.py` regenerated via the new `build_submission.py` and re-verified (same numbers, zero stderr, `DONE`/`DONE`).
- Notes: reverse-engineered the leaderboard #1 player (Ryo Hasegawa)'s strategy from two public replays -- aggressive day-0 hiring ramping to 8-12 hands/day, fast land expansion (all quadrants by day ~10), a large pasture-only (no coop) animal operation, near-zero cash tolerance, and a MELON+WHEAT -> STRAWBERRY -> WHEAT crop-phase rotation -- and initially just copied those numbers directly into `DEFAULT_CONFIG` (`max_hires_per_day: 12`, `pasture_target_ratio: 1.0`, immediate land/animal spending). That version was badly broken in three distinct, sequential ways, each real and worth remembering: (1) day-0 cash crash -- hiring/land/animals all competing for the same thin starting pile with no dedicated floor for the (very cheap) hire cost, fixed by giving HIRE its own `hire_money_floor` separate from the general `money_reserve`, and a separate `animal_reserve_multiple` for the much pricier animal purchases; (2) reward is money *at game end*, not cumulative -- continuing to hire/build/buy land in the final days (no time to pay back) and leaving shed inventory unsold was pure waste, fixed with an explicit `wind_down_days` liquidation phase (force-sell everything, stop all new spending) plus a `season_days` cutoff so crops that can't mature before the season ends never get planted; (3) **land bought faster than the crew could physically reach and tend it** -- at `max_hires_per_day: 12` with land bought on pure affordability, ~half of all unlocked tiles sat permanently empty (confirmed by direct tile-count inspection: 46-63 empty tiles out of 75-100 unlocked, and *growing* over time) because our simple greedy-nearest-tile unit dispatcher spends most turns walking a wide board rather than working it -- confirmed via action-type histogram (76% movement vs ~24% actual harvest/water/plant/etc). This is a genuine limit of the current routing, not something more tuning fixes: blindly copying a top player's raw scale numbers without matching their (unknown, presumably more sophisticated) unit routing actively lost to a modest single-hand baseline in local head-to-head testing. Backed off to a moderate scale (`max_hires_per_day` ~5-7, `land_utilization_threshold` restored to a real gate ~0.5-0.7 instead of pure affordability) and handed the rest to `optimize.py`'s Optuna search rather than continuing to hand-guess -- that search's own promoted config (see below) landed on `max_hires_per_day: 7`, `land_utilization_threshold: 0.60`, `pasture_target_ratio: 0.45`, validating the moderate-scale hypothesis with a real verified result instead of a guess. Also added `src/build_submission.py` (AST-based, extracts the relevant functions from `farm_utils.py`/`robust_agent.py` + bakes in `best_config.json`) so `submission/main.py` is generated instead of hand-maintained -- this caught one real bug immediately (`import math` was missing from the generated file, since `_plan_units` now uses `math.ceil` but the old hand-copy predated that code and the generator's first draft forgot it) before it could have reached a real submission.

- Command: ad hoc Python, loading `submission/main.py` via the exact file-path mechanism `kaggle_environments` uses (`env.run(['submission/main.py', opponent])`, not a direct import), vs melon_maxxer (both seats), multi_crop, random, and self-play
